no target naming in log lines that do exist ("...on Blastoise!"); no pacing/delay between actions (everything resolves instantly, making the log unreadable in real time); no visual turn separator in the log. **M25d — Battle UI polish**: Pokémon name and level not displayed above/near the HP bar during battle, unlike the real games; plus a togglable combat-debug overlay (off by default) showing per-move stat breakdowns — power, accuracy, and the full `DamageCalculator.calculate()` output (STAB, type effectiveness multiplier, crit, random roll, final damage) — wired off existing signals (`move_executed`, `secondary_applied`, etc.) with no new calculation logic needed; structurally separate from the real player-facing battle log and from the corner-band doubles sprite layout, not merged into either; panel placement (bottom dock vs. togglable side panel) remains an implementation-time design detail, not locked now. **M25e — Background art replacement with CFRU reference PNGs**: Phase 5a's original tile/tilemap/palette-decoded reconstruction was flagged as visually incorrect; superseding both Phase 5a's own note AND this entry's own earlier "re-attempt the decode" plan (a simplified prompt replaced that with a direct pull instead), this sub-phase replaces the flawed output with already-composited flat PNGs pulled from a CFRU reference repo — a direct crop-and-copy, not a re-decode — matched by terrain name against this project's existing 11 background ids, updating `battle_background_smoke_test.gd`'s hardcoded 240×160-per-file dimension assertion to match, and resolving the standing keep-vs-delete call on `scripts/gen_battle_backgrounds.py`. **M25f — Bespoke move-animation data research + balance/setup fixes**: a recon pass into what additional per-move animation data source actually holds for Flamethrower/Thunder/Surf beyond Phase 5b/5c's own pull (the current bespoke animations were flagged as looking nothing like the real games', so this investigates what's available before attempting a redo), plus matching random-opponent team generation's Pokémon levels to the player's own team level (currently produces oddly-matched or duplicate-with-shared-state opponents — related to but distinct from M25a's hardlock bug). **M25g — Doubles AI-scoring gaps carried over from M24c**, previously flagged in M24c's own closing report but never assigned a milestone until now: `_score_move_doubles`/`choose_action_doubles` never received the `ai_flags` treatment M24c gave the singles-only `_score_move`/`choose_action` path, silently ignoring the real AI-flag combination for 77 of 854 doubles-format trainers; and RISKY/FORCE_SETUP_FIRST_TURN shipped narrow — only a small bonus-scoring slice per flag, with source's full per-move-effect scope (~15-25 cases each) never ported — both to be reviewed and either scoped for real implementation or explicitly further deferred. ⚠️ **PREREQUISITE ADDED 2026-08-08 — THE `ai_flags` HOOKUP GAP, AND IT BLOCKS THE FIRST HALF OF THIS ITEM.** Neither statement above is wrong, but both sit on top of a gap neither names: `TrainerAI.from_trainer_data()` exists and copies `ai_flags` correctly (`trainer_ai.gd:98-100`) and has **zero non-test callers**, while the single live construction site — `battle_screen_shared.gd:1828-1829` — builds a bare `TrainerAI.new()` with a hardcoded `Tier.SMART` and never consults `opp_trainer_data`, which is resolved six lines above and handed to `set_trainer_data()` for money/items/intro only. So no trainer's real `ai_flags` reaches **either** scoring path today. The doubles half of this item is therefore **untestable until that is fixed** — wiring `ai_flags` into `_score_move_doubles` changes nothing observable while every trainer still runs at the `AI_FLAG_BASIC_TRAINER` default. The RISKY/FORCE_SETUP_FIRST_TURN half is unaffected and can proceed independently. The fix is owned by **M27Q Q1**, which records the measured consequence: parsing every `.tres` (⚠️ NOT `grep "^ai_flags = "` — Godot omits an `@export` at its default, so a plain grep silently misses the **15 trainers whose flags are 0**) gives 1183 × `1` / **253 × `7`** / 15 × `0` / 13 × `11` / 7 × `3` / 5 × `23` / 1 × `15` = 1477 — **restated 2026-08-08 after the `CHECKVIABILITY` importer fix moved 80 trainers from `3` to `7`** (see Q1's own entry). Against the `7` every trainer used to run at, that is **1,205 strictly weaker** (1183+15+7), 253 unchanged, 13 mixed (`11` trades CHECK_VIABILITY for FORCE_SETUP), and only **6 stronger** — plus all 1,477 losing proactive switching, since zero of them carry `AI_FLAG_SMART_SWITCHING`. Source fidelity, but a deliberate call, not a free win. Do Q1 first, or fold it in here; either way it is not optional. **M25h — Bottom-region + secondary-screen redesign, tiered into M25h-1 → M25h-1.1 → M25h-1.2 → M25h-1.3 → M25h-1.4 → M25h-1.5 → M25h-2 → M25h-3 → M25h-4 → M25h-5 → M25h-6** (scope locked/expanded 2026-07-19, after three recon sessions and one further reconsideration, then renumbered again the same day once M25h-1.1/h-1.2/h-1.3 had already shipped under the "h-1.x" pattern, then reorganized a third time the same day to insert a dedicated visual-QA pass and a real playthrough pass after the log/debug merge, then reorganized a fourth time once M25h-3's own audit surfaced two concrete follow-up items that needed real sub-phases of their own — see below; every earlier framing of this sub-phase, including its own immediately-prior "keep Item/Switch inline as a disclosed simplification, real separate Bag/Party screens are future/out-of-scope" call, is superseded by the plan below — that boundary has been deliberately reversed, not just refined: the project has moved past its backend/mechanics-first phase into building fully polished, real-games-authentic views, so real separate secondary screens are now in scope for M25h itself). **Renumbering note**: Item and Switch were originally scoped as standalone M25h-2/M25h-3 in the same session that reversed the inline-simplification call; once M25h-1.1/h-1.2/h-1.3 shipped as sequential sub-sessions of M25h-1 itself (window art, real fonts, button-chrome/cursor), Item and Switch were renumbered into that same h-1.x sequence — M25h-1.4 (Item) and M25h-1.5 (Switch) — to reflect that they're continuations of the same real-window-art/real-font/real-chrome build-out, not a structurally separate tier. M25h-4 (the log/debug-overlay merge) was never part of the h-1.x sub-sequence, but is itself renumbered in this same reorganization: it becomes **M25h-2**, continuing the sequence directly after h-1.5 rather than keeping a number that now reads as if it followed two sub-phases (h-2/h-3) that didn't exist yet when it was assigned. Two new sub-phases are inserted after it, both previously unscoped: **M25h-3 — Graphical UI polish** and (at the time) M25h-4 — Playthrough testing and debug. **Second renumbering, same day, after M25h-3's own audit landed**: M25h-3's audit found two concrete, real follow-up items that needed their own dedicated sub-phases rather than being folded into the playthrough pass — **M25h-4 — Real visual elements for Item/Switch screens** and **M25h-5 — Font corrections (Item/Switch list text)** — inserted immediately after M25h-3. The former M25h-4 (Playthrough testing and debug) is renumbered again to **M25h-6**, keeping its own scope completely unchanged, simply pushed later so the two audit-derived sub-phases land before the human-playthrough pass (a settled, fully-polished set of screens is a better playthrough target than one with known, already-identified visual gaps still open). **M25h-1 — the shared inline region**: exactly what source itself actually keeps inline (re-confirmed directly against `battle_controller_player.c`, not assumed) — the top-level action prompt, Fight/move-select, and Run — collapsed into pages of ONE bottom-anchored region, reusing the existing `Menu` enum and `_build_top_menu`/`_build_fight_menu`/`_build_target_select_buttons` functions as-is (relocated/resized, not rewritten) — the existing `field_slot` threading already supports doubles per-slot sequencing with no new mechanism needed. Sized/positioned at the real games' own literal ~75-95%-of-screen-height proportion, sourced from `sStandardBattleWindowTemplates[]` (`battle_bg.c`) — `B_WIN_MSG` at tilemapTop=15/height=4 tiles = y=120-152px of the 160px screen — verify visually once built and adjust only if proven necessary, not pre-adjusted on paper now. Two carried-forward open items to resolve during THIS sub-phase's own implementation, not decided in advance: whether the literal 75-95% figure needs adjustment after a real screenshot check, and the `PlayerHealthGroupD1` clearance risk (that doubles slot's own health group currently ends at y≈474px/73.1% of a 648px viewport — only ~12px of clearance below a region starting at 75%). Also includes, as a small natural fit alongside this same layout work: **delete** `Side0Label`/`Side1Label` (confirmed redundant M23.2-era plain-text HP scaffolding, superseded by Phase 4b's real health-box HP bars, plus a confirmed real bug — `_refresh_ui()` feeds them via `BattleParty.get_active()`, hardcoded to `get_active_at(0)`, so in doubles they never reflect field slot 1 at all) — deleted now, not deferred. **M25h-1.4 — Item (Bag) as a real separate screen**: a genuine, polished full-screen Item/Bag view matching source's own real screen-swap architecture (`OpenBagAndChooseItem`/`CloseMainBattleScreen`) rather than an inline page — a real scene transition in and out, not a resized panel. Starts from this project's own already-implemented battle-item set/logic (`ItemManager`), presented as a real screen; full inventory-browsing/pocket-tab polish can layer on incrementally, but the screen-level architecture itself must be real from the start, not a placeholder standing in for one. Sequenced first of the two (before M25h-1.5) — simpler, no forced-replacement-style flow to route through it, making it the safer real-screen build to shake out the scene-swap pattern before Switch's own extra complexity. **M25h-1.5 — Switch (Party) as a real separate screen**: the same treatment for Switch — a genuine full-screen party view matching source's own `OpenPartyMenuToChooseMon`/`OpenPartyMenuInBattle` architecture, replacing the current inline button list, including the forced-replacement (fainted Pokémon) flow routing through the same real screen rather than a separate inline path. **Adjacent scope explicitly excluded from the h-1.x arc** (tracked separately, not forgotten — just deliberately not bundled into h-1.4/h-1.5/h-2 to keep this sequence clean): the 2 still-unfixed battle backgrounds (sky/underwater, still on Phase 5a's flawed reconstruction, no acceptable CFRU replacement found during M25e). The font-variant gap originally flagged here (`FONT_NARROW`, source's own real font for its `B_WIN_MOVE_NAME` window and, per M25h-3's own later audit, also Item's own list rows) is no longer an open/unscoped exclusion — it's now concretely scoped as part of **M25h-5** below, alongside a second, independently-confirmed FONT_SMALL mismatch on Switch's own list rows. **M25h-2 — the log/debug-overlay merge** (renumbered from its original M25h-4 label — see the Renumbering note above; sequenced AFTER M25h-1.4/M25h-1.5 — needs a settled final menu architecture to build against, not the old inline-everything shape): retire M25c's standalone always-visible accumulating log entirely; build one shared "last action" data structure feeding BOTH M25d's existing raw stat-breakdown content AND the human-readable narrative text the log used to carry (move-announcement/target-naming/damage-effectiveness text, M25c's own work) into the single F3-toggled debug-overlay view, replacing two independent listeners with one. F3 stays off by default — a deliberate, disclosed tradeoff (a player gets zero textual battle info without opening it) accepted for now, revisit only if it proves a real UX problem in practice. **Log-history decision, resolved** (was previously an open flag in this scoping pass, now settled): the merged F3 view preserves M25c's own full accumulating multi-turn scrolling history, rather than reducing it to a single-most-recent-action display — M25c already built the scrolling behavior, nothing about M25h-1.4/h-1.5 changes the underlying log data, and dropping it would be a real feature loss for no real gain. M25h-1.4 and M25h-1.5 are independent of each other and of M25h-1's own internal region sizing, and may be built in either order or in parallel (though the current plan sequences Item before Switch — see M25h-1.4's own entry), but both must land before M25h-2. **M25h-3 — Graphical UI polish**: a dedicated visual-QA pass across the full h-1.x arc plus M25h-2, once every structural piece (shared region, real window art, real fonts, real chrome/cursor, Item, Switch, the merged log/debug view) is actually built and there is a settled whole to look at — catch and resolve remaining visual rough edges rather than guessing at them piecemeal mid-build. Explicitly includes revisiting M25h-1.3's cursor-position placeholder (never fully source-verified against the real games' own cursor offset math, shipped as a reasonable-looking approximation at the time), plus whatever else surfaces once h-1.5/h-2 are in and reviewable as a whole — pocket-tab UI, Switch screen layout, and any other inconsistency found along the way. **M25h-3's own audit findings, resolved into two new sub-phases below** (completed 2026-07-20): the cursor-position placeholder is now split-resolved — Item/Switch's own text-prefix cursor is confirmed source-accurate (`BagMenu_Print(WIN_ITEM_LIST, FONT_NORMAL, gText_SelectorArrow2, 0, y, ...)`, `item_menu.c` — the same "▶"-as-text trick this project already uses), while the TOP menu and Fight/move-select screens turned out to be a real, confirmed 2×2 GRID in source (`B_WIN_MOVE_NAME_1..4` literally labeled "Top left/Top right/Bottom left/Bottom right" in `include/constants/battle.h`; `ActionSelectionCreateCursorAt`/`MoveSelectionCreateCursorAt`'s identical bit-math tile-paste cursor, `battle_controller_player.c`), not the vertical list this project currently builds — a real layout-shape gap, not a cursor-position detail, deferred to a future regrid pass (not yet its own numbered sub-phase — see M25i's own note below). The audit also found two concrete, real, fixable follow-up items, promoted to their own sub-phases immediately below: real screen-specific visual elements never actually pulled for Item/Switch (both currently reuse ONLY the shared h-1.1/h-1.2/h-1.3 elements), and two confirmed font mismatches (Item list rows use FONT_NORMAL but source uses FONT_NARROW; Switch list rows use FONT_NORMAL but source uses FONT_SMALL). Also confirmed as a disclosed, permanent simplification rather than a fixable gap: TARGET_SELECT's whole text-menu approach has no direct source basis at all (real doubles targeting bounces the target's own health box in place via `DoBounceEffect(gMultiUsePlayerCursor, BOUNCE_HEALTHBOX, ...)` with D-pad cycling, `battle_controller_player.c:426-518` — not a menu of any kind) — flagged as a real architecture mismatch, large enough to need its own dedicated arc rather than folding into this polish pass; see **M25j** at the very end of this milestone. **M25h-4 — Real visual elements for Item/Switch screens**: pull and wire real screen-specific visual assets — backgrounds, list-row styling, slot-layout art, or whatever source actually uses beyond the generic shared window frame/font/cursor already reused — for both the Item/Bag screen (M25h-1.4) and Switch/Party screen (M25h-1.5), following the same "pull real reference art first" discipline as h-1.1. Both headers (Item's "ITEMS", Switch's "Choose a POKéMON.") are already correctly FONT_NORMAL per the same audit and need no change. **M25h-6 — Playthrough testing and debug** (renumbered from its original M25h-4 label once the audit's own two follow-ups were promoted ahead of it — see the second renumbering note above; scope itself unchanged): a real, hands-on playthrough pass covering everything M25h built — the shared bottom region, Item/Bag, Switch/Party, and the merged log/debug view — following the same "a human playthrough surfaces what automated tests can't" precedent already established by M25a's own original bug-hunt and the M23.12 full-playthrough milestone. There is no longer an "explicitly out of scope / future-only" carve-out for separate Bag/Party screens within this sub-phase — that note is retired along with the framing that produced it. **M25i — Reserved for a future scoping session**: once M25a-h are implemented, a follow-up scoping session continues building out M25's remaining battle-UI-mastery/battle-game-modes scope (including the combat-debug overlay noted above, and the TOP-menu/move-select 2×2 regrid flagged by M25h-3's own audit) from there; deliberately left open/unscoped, no speculative content added. **M25j — TARGET_SELECT redesign** (appended at the very end of M25, after M25i — a large structural change, not routine polish, per M25h-3's own audit finding above): replace the current text button-list targeting menu with source's real in-place health-box bounce/D-pad-cycle interaction model (`DoBounceEffect(gMultiUsePlayerCursor, BOUNCE_HEALTHBOX, ...)` plus D-pad-driven battler-cycling, `battle_controller_player.c:426-518`), rather than the invented list-menu solution this project built to solve the same functional problem. Deserves its own dedicated scoping-then-implementation arc — mirroring how M25h itself was scoped before being built — not a quick regrid; deliberately sequenced last since it's the single largest remaining departure from source's real interaction model found anywhere in this milestone. **Status detail** (relocated verbatim from the old Status column, 2026-07-26; only the leading status emoji was dropped — it is what the C/T/A column now replaces): M25a/b/c/d/e done, M25h-1/h-1.1/h-1.2/h-1.3/h-1.4/h-1.5/h-3/h-4 done (M25a: 4 real playthrough bugs found and fixed; M25b: Fight/Item/Switch/Run menu + the Bug 3 idle-animation fix; M25c: move-announcement/target-naming/damage-effectiveness/pacing/turn-separator log authenticity; M25d: name/level display + combat-debug overlay; | M27 | **Full RPG rescope** — UI & overworld: shops, bag/inventory, item-use, plus the overworld scene, map system, player movement, NPC/trigger logic, and encounter-triggering infra. **DECOMPOSED INTO TWELVE LETTERED BLOCKS 2026-07-28 (M27A–M27L), Rob-approved.** ⚠️ **THAT RANGE IS THE 2026-07-28 RECORD, NOT THE LIVE BLOCK LIST — four blocks were appended after it**: `M27M` (map authoring tooling, 2026-07-30), `M27N` (field weather) and `M27O` (whiteout & respawn, both 2026-07-31), and `M27P` (overworld polish, opened 2026-08-07, open and empty — see its own section below). Kept as-written rather than silently widened, so what Rob actually approved on the day stays readable. Uppercase block letters are deliberate and load-bearing, exactly as in M26 — `M27E` can never be confused with an older lowercase label. **No downstream milestone number moves**: M32 and M34 are retired as slots (tombstone rows above, M21 precedent), M29/M30/M33 are narrowed, M28/M31 clarified, M35 expanded — so every existing citation in this file, `docs/decisions.md` and commit history stays valid. **The scope of record for all twelve blocks is `docs/overworld_scope.md`** (§30 roadmap, §31 the approved renumbering); this row is the index, not the detail. **M27A — Import foundation**: importer spike on one real map, FRLG 32-bit metatile attributes, the measured 0–15→5-bucket elevation table, walking skeleton (one map + step resolver + collision + camera), and **defines the save shape** (box rule, per-instance fidelity). **M27A COMPLETE 2026-07-28 (proof of concept).** `scripts/gen_map_import.py` converts Pallet Town end to end: three plane atlases (Ground/Objects/Overhangs per §1.6's layer-type routing) plus `pallet_town.json` carrying five per-cell arrays. Every scoping prediction held — 480 cells for 24×20, collision only {0,1}, elevation only {0,1,3} matching the independent Python measurement (198/12/270) exactly, 729 metatiles = 640 primary + 89 secondary. **One real bug found and fixed**: tileset label→directory is NOT derivable by lowercasing (`gTileset_PalletTown` lives in `secondary/pallet_town_frlg`); the authoritative mapping is the INCBIN path in `src/data/tilesets/metatiles.h`, now parsed — **this is the same gap that limited the §1.7 collision sweep to 79% coverage**. **Layer routing proven empirically**, not just by composite: split renders confirm the above-plane holds roofs and tree canopy and nothing walkable, so 'walk behind the house' is per-metatile layer type with no Y-sorting, exactly as §1.6 predicted. New `scripts/overworld/`: `metatile_behavior.gd` (all 240 MB_* constants, GENERATED not hand-typed), `map_data.gd`, `step_resolver.gd` (outcome enum + two-sided directional check + ledge-as-redirect, semantics ported / expression Godot-idiomatic per §1.8). `scenes/overworld/pallet_town.tscn` builds the TileSet programmatically and hosts the skeleton. Verified by real non-headless run: map renders correctly, six southward steps advance one cell each with the sprite tweening to the right pixel, and eight westward attempts stop after two cells on real collision. `m27a_step_resolver_test` **32/32** across import integrity (incl. the histogram cross-check and unexpected-value guards), bounds, collision, elevation wildcards and ordering, the two-sided directional rule, ledges (two-tile hop, not climbable, jumpable even when solid), and the no-collision debug toggle. Regression: `damage_test` 24/24, `m26_b4_0_weather_continues_test` 38/38, `m26_b3_6c_test` 168/168, `item_registry_test` 309/309. **M27B — Import pipeline. FULL-REGION CONVERSION DONE 2026-07-28 — 421/421 Kanto maps, zero assertion failures.** `python3 scripts/gen_map_import.py all` converts every map's per-cell data with the §1.4/§1.7 guards live; `subset` renders a curated eight chosen by measured structure. **Aggregate across 230,619 cells confirms every scoping prediction at full coverage**: elevation only {0:128696, 1:19909, 3:79671, 4:1919, 5:424}, collision only {0:107369, 1:123250} (boolean, as measured), layer types NORMAL 120318 / COVERED 110296 / **SPLIT 5**. **Two real bugs found by running everything rather than a sample, each invisible at one map**: (1) `gTileset_BuildingFrlg` points at `gMetatiles_Building_Frlg` (extra underscore), so label==symbol failed for all **234 indoor maps**; (2) **a tileset does not necessarily own its graphics** — `gTileset_SilphCo` has no `tiles.png` or palettes at all and borrows both from Condominiums, which broke the 20 Silph Co / Rocket Hideout / elevator maps. Resolution is now PER FIELD via `headers.h`'s struct (`.metatiles` / `.tiles` / `.palettes` each resolved independently through metatiles.h and graphics.h INCBIN/INCGFX paths). This also retires the ~79%-coverage caveat on §1.7's collision sweep. Screenshot-verified subset, all correct: Pallet Town (control), Six Island Ruin Valley (**the only Kanto map with SPLIT metatiles, plus elevation 5**), Seafoam Islands B4F (elevation 4, 11 behaviours), Diglett's Cave B1F (largest, 6800 cells), Saffron City (4 connections, Silph Co), Four Island (14 behaviours — most in Kanto), Route 23 (24x160), SS Anne Exterior (water-heavy). **THREE SCOPE ADDITIONS 2026-07-28 (Rob-approved), from measuring what hand-authoring on imported maps actually costs — see `docs/overworld_scope.md` §1.9.** Measured across all 421 maps / 230,619 cells: **collision varies by placement for 52.0% of metatiles and elevation for 52.1%**, and a per-metatile collision default would mis-set **29,827 cells (12.93%)** — so a painted tile can carry its art and its behaviour, but NEVER its collision or elevation. **(1) Bake tiles into the `.tscn` at import time** so the scene is the artifact and JSON becomes a build input, not a runtime one — M27A's PoC has `_paint()` rewriting every cell from JSON at `_ready()`, which **silently destroys anything hand-painted**; fine for a skeleton, wrong to build authoring on, and far cheaper to change at one map than at 421. **(2) The behaviour/elevation debug overlay becomes an EDITOR, not just a viewer** — collision and elevation cannot be inferred for a newly painted cell, so this is where they get set; without it there is no way to author a walkable building at all. **(3) Defaults are offered and visibly marked as defaults** — inheriting from the cell painted over (or the metatile's most common value) is right ~87% of the time, so the remaining 13% must be visible rather than silent; the map format needs to record defaulted-vs-explicit, which is a data-shape requirement that cannot be retrofitted onto a format storing only final values. **Re-import policy DECIDED 2026-07-28: per-map, refuse-unless-forced, NO merge.** A three-way merge keyed on provenance was designed and dropped — re-import is per-map rather than bulk, which makes clobbering a usage question, not an architecture one. The kept guard is ~5 lines: refuse to overwrite a map containing any `AUTHORED` cell unless `--force`, reading the provenance field Change 3 requires anyway. **Output split SETTLED**: `assets/maps/` (421 per-map JSON + validation renders) is gitignored **as a directory**, so a new kind of generated output cannot quietly become tracked; `assets/map_atlases/` IS tracked — atlases are shared per tileset PAIR (**60 pairs for 421 maps**), are runtime dependencies of tracked scenes, and follow Godot's commit-texture-plus-.import convention. The shared layout is a **7x reduction** (180 PNGs / 360 files vs 1,263 / 2,526) and was nearly free to adopt, since `get_tileset()` already cached by pair and only the filenames were per-map. **CHANGE 1 SHIPPED 2026-07-28** — maps now bake to real artifacts. `scenes/overworld/map_baker.tscn` reads the imported JSON and writes `scenes/maps/<Map>.tscn` + `<Map>_data.tres`; the walking skeleton instantiates the baked scene instead of painting every cell at `_ready()`, so hand-edits are no longer destroyed. Godot owns its own serialisation (PackedScene/ResourceSaver) rather than Python hand-writing TileMapLayer's binary `tile_map_data` blob. `MapData` became a `Resource` with `@export`s. **Baked layer order: Ground / Objects / Entities_P2 / Overhangs / Entities_P1** — TWO entity strata, and the split is NOT 'upper vs lower'. **Real finding that reshaped this**: elevation drives entity DRAW ORDER in source, not just movement permission — `sElevationToPriority` (`event_object_movement.c`) is `[2,2,2,2,1,2,1,2,1,2,1,2,1,0,0,2]`, lower draws on top, so elevation 4 -> priority 1 (above the overhang plane) but **elevation 5 returns to priority 2**, back to ground level. That odd/even alternation is the stacked-bridge mechanism, and guessing 'upper vs lower' would have got the three Sevii maps that use elevation 5 wrong. Table is GENERATED from source, not transcribed. **Re-import guard verified end to end**: with one cell flipped to AUTHORED the baker refuses (`0/1 baked`) and proceeds under `--force` (`1/1`). **One real bug caught by its own test**: `provenance` was declared, written to JSON, and never read back by `MapData.load_from`, so it persisted as an empty `PackedByteArray()` and the guard could never fire; fixed, with a fail-safe that treats a missing/short array as all-IMPORTED. `m27a_step_resolver_test` 32 -> **44/44**. The abandoned 26-June `scenes/maps/pallettown.tscn` spike is now **deleted** — genuinely superseded, per §3's recommendation. **Playable via F6 as of 2026-07-28**: `scenes/overworld/pallet_town.tscn`, arrow keys, camera follows. Input polls a HELD direction in `_process` rather than reacting in `_unhandled_input` — the latter fires once per key EVENT, so holding a direction gave a single step plus erratic OS key repeat: per-step logic correct, feel unplayable. Verified through the real input path (`Input.action_press`), not by calling `_try_step` directly: held ui_down walked 9 cells, ui_right 6 more, and the next press was correctly refused by collision at Oak's Lab. **Known and expected**: no border skirt yet (M27C), so walking to a map edge shows void rather than a sealed border. **SCAFFOLDING NOTE — `scenes/overworld/pallet_town.tscn` is TEMPORARY and should be deleted at M27C, not extended.** Two scene kinds exist and only one is permanent: `scenes/maps/<Map>.tscn` is the map ARTIFACT (five nodes, tiles baked, no logic, ~421 of them, named after source's own map name because that is the identifier `map.json` and the connection graph key on) and is hand-editable; `scenes/overworld/pallet_town.tscn` is a PLAYER CONTROLLER that hardcodes one map purely because M27A needed something to F6. It becomes **one generic `overworld.tscn`** — player, camera, input, resolver, plus a MapManager loading whichever map is current — NOT 421 controller scenes. **M27C (stitching) is the forcing function**: once maps connect, 'which map am I in' stops being a scene identity and becomes runtime state, with several chunks live at once and the player crossing between them without a scene change; a per-map controller scene cannot express that. The `PalletTown_Frlg` vs `pallet_town` naming mismatch is deliberate, not an oversight, and resolves itself when the controller stops being named after a map. **OBJECT-EVENT DATA HALF DONE 2026-07-28** — `gen_map_import.py` now emits a normalised `events` array per map, and the whole-tree script-label indexer resolves trainer identity. Across the 8 baked corridor maps: **132 events — 29 npc / 13 trainer / 7 item_ball / 33 warp / 20 trigger / 30 sign**, with **13/13 trainers resolved** to real keys, sight ranges and overworld sprites (e.g. `TRAINER_LASS_ROBIN`, sight 3, `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_LASS_FRLG`). object_events split three ways by their own fields: `TRAINER_TYPE_NORMAL` -> trainer, item-ball graphic -> pickup, else npc. **Also fixed a real baker bug**: Godot 4 routes post-`--` args to `get_cmdline_user_args()`, not `get_cmdline_args()`, so `_args()` always came back empty and **every bake silently fell back to the default, only ever producing Pallet Town while reporting `1/1 baked`**. Surfaced only because a request for 8 maps returned 1. **[Step 4] FRLG ROSTER CONVERTED — 623 new `_FRLG` files, purely additive. COMPLETE 2026-07-28. THE M27B TRAINER BLOCKER IS CLOSED.** `gen_trainer_data.py` now reads both rosters; **1,477 trainers (854 `_RSE`, 623 `_FRLG`)**. **623, not 624** — the earlier figure counted `TRAINER_NONE`'s blank sentinel, excluded from Kanto exactly as it already was from Hoenn. **The acceptance check passed on the first regen: 623 new files, 0 modified, 0 deleted.** Under the retired `trainer_id` scheme this same merge would have rewritten **808 of 854** existing files — this is the Step 1 re-keying paying for itself, and the check is worth repeating for any future roster addition (an existing `_RSE` file changing means the key scheme has a bug). Classes and pics needed **zero** new infrastructure: all 49 Kanto `Class:` values already resolve against the shared enum (which carries 50 `_FRLG` classes), and all 62 `Pic:` values resolve to stems via Rule B. **Three converter gaps the Kanto roster exposed, none of which Hoenn ever reached.** (1) **Nidoran, a genuine latent collision**: `pokemon.json` names the gendered forms `Nidoran♀`/`Nidoran♂` and `normalize()` strips non-alphanumerics, so **both collapsed to `NIDORAN` and one silently overwrote the other**; the party file additionally spells them `Nidoran F`/`Nidoran M`, matching neither. Invisible until now because **zero Hoenn trainers use Nidoran and 19 Kanto ones do**. Fixed with aliases resolved by SYMBOL from the data, so a pokédex renumber fails loudly rather than mis-mapping. (2) **`Faint Attack`** is the pre-Gen-VI spelling — source itself declares `MOVE_FAINT_ATTACK = MOVE_FEINT_ATTACK, // Pre-Gen VI name`, so it is a rename resolving to move 185, not a second move. (3) **`Stardust`** stays unresolved and that is correct: no `holdEffect` in source, a `POCKET_ITEMS` treasure with only a price, outside the held-item roster for the same reason Nugget already was. After the two fixes, **zero unresolved species and zero unresolved moves**. **A correction to the portrait-gap size**: the reference ships **86** `_frlg` front pics but only **62 are referenced by any converted trainer** — the other **24 have no consumer in either roster**. `[M26B3-1]`'s '86 sprites' is the size of the available art, not the gap; **pulling 62 satisfies the whole roster**. That is the proof no hand edits existed to lose — but it also means a genuine edit could hide inside re-bake churn, so review a future bake with `sed -E 's/ unique_id=[0-9]+//'`. This is the concrete detection method for §1.9's re-import-vs-hand-edits risk — a scene that is no longer reproducible carries hand-authored content a `--force` re-bake would silently overwrite. **Non-destructive** (restores either way) and deliberately **not wired into the baker**; a standalone check a re-bake session runs first. Verified both directions: clean corridor exits 0; an injected hand-placed node exits 1 and is named in the diff. **Sequence complete.** Remaining and explicitly out of scope: the **62** Kanto portrait pulls (the remaining half of `[M26B3-1]`, tracked by the dangling-stem counter), overlay-as-editor (M27B Change 2), and warps/stitching (M27C). **[Kanto portraits] `[M26B3-1]` CLOSED — all 62 referenced `_frlg` front pics pulled. 2026-07-28.** Asset audit first, per `[M26B3-1]`'s own discipline: all **62 present at source, all 64×64, all palette-mode** — identical in shape to the 93 already pulled, so a flat copy with no decode, exactly as that entry predicted. **Transparency needed no special handling**: unlike the ball sheets (which carry no tRNS and required explicit index-0 tagging), these already carry `tRNS = 0`, so `shutil.copyfile` preserves it. It FAILED on this pull (`expected 62, got 0`) exactly as designed, and its constants are updated with that history recorded at the assertion rather than silently reset. Regression: `trainer_data_smoke_test` 5091/5091, `m26_trainer_category_party_test` 128/128, `m26_b3_6c_test` 168/168, `m24b_test` 61/61, `battle_ui_sprite_smoke_test` 222/222, `m27a_step_resolver_test` 70/70, `m24c_test` 27/28/28. **Visually verified**, not just counted: a 62-sprite contact sheet confirms zero blank/transparent sprites and an opaque-pixel fraction of 0.17–0.65 (median 0.27), consistent with real trainer art on transparent backgrounds. **M27B STATUS, corrected 2026-07-28** (the previous "still to do" list had gone stale — three of its four entries were already shipped, verified against the codebase rather than the note):

- ✅ **Script-label indexer** — built; 18,315 labels; **432/432** Kanto trainer placements resolve.
- ✅ **`FLAG_HIDE_*`-in-`trainer_type` edge case** — handled by construction, not by accident: the object-event split is an exact-match `tt == "TRAINER_TYPE_NORMAL"`, so the **9 real Kanto cases** (Seafoam Islands / Victory Road boulders) fall through to `npc` and can never parse as trainers.
- ✅ **Second entity container + elevation-change reparent** — the baker emits `Entities_P2`/`Entities_P1` ordered by source's own `sElevationToPriority`, and the player controller reparents between them via `_reparent_for_elevation()`.
- ✅ **Object events, both halves** — 6 `@tool` node classes over `OverworldEntity`; 132 events across the 8 corridor maps.
- ✅ **Kanto trainers + portraits** — 623 trainers converted, 62 portraits pulled, dangling-stem counter back to 0/0.
- ✅ **Change 3 — the format field (`attr_explicit`)** — per-ATTRIBUTE two-bit explicitness, landed before any UI because §1.9 says it cannot be retrofitted onto a format storing only final values. Collision and elevation are set independently, so per-cell provenance cannot express a half-decided cell. Imported cells are stamped explicit on both; "needs review" is precisely `AUTHORED and not explicit`. Round-trip guarded through baked `.tres` → mutate → save → reload.
- ✅ **Change 2 — the overlay** — read half committed (`c7caac2e`); write half (per-cell collision/elevation painting) plus `addons/map_overlay_editor` and the Save-Map-Data affordance committed 2026-07-29. Vocabulary measured and small, as predicted: **83 behaviours present but the top 12 cover 97.5% of cells**, only **5 elevations** (0/1/3/4/5), boolean collision, and **3 ledge directions** in Kanto (S/W/E). *This bullet previously read "no overlay scene or script exists yet" — stale since the read half shipped.*

**[M27B overlay — events mode, sight lines, dead doors, stacks, counts banner, the baker guard, and the write half] COMMITTED — 2026-07-29.** The freeze and undo defects are fixed, the temporary diagnostics are gone, and painting persists through a real Save affordance. The `check_bake_diff` false positive is fixed too — all 8 corridor scenes reproducible. **One item remains unfixed and known: click-to-select disables itself**, so `next_in_stack`'s cycling is unreachable through the UI.

**Two generated files, both `@tool`, both emitted by `gen_map_import.py` beside `metatile_behavior.gd`.** `map_constants.gd` — **939 maps**, `MAP_*` constant → map directory name. Generated because the two are **not related by any string transform**: `MAP_SSANNE_EXTERIOR` → `SSAnne_Exterior_Frlg`. Every region is emitted, not just Kanto, so a constant ABSENT from the table means the importer produced a destination source does not define (a bug), which is a different diagnosis from a destination that exists and is merely unbaked (the expected M27C gap). `movement_types.gd` — all **89** `MOVEMENT_TYPE_*`, plus the **4** with a fixed facing. `NPC.movement_type` is a free-text `String`, and retyping it in the inspector is the fastest way to turn a rotating trainer into a fixed-facing one, so a typo is cheap to introduce and — once rays key off it — invisible; both `NPC` and the overlay check membership rather than assuming.

**Events mode** (`Mode.EVENTS`, appended so serialised ints don't shift and it joins the F3 cycle free). Markers per kind; the overlay resolves entities via `map_root` with a `get_parent()` fallback, which is a genuinely new data source — every other mode derives from `cell_info()`, these are placed NODES the overlay could not previously see. Dead doors: a warp whose `dest_map` has no baked scene draws in warning colour. **The corridor's 33 warps resolve to 20 distinct destinations and every one is unbaked**, so the first render is entirely flagged — correct, and the point.

**Trainer sight rays — fixed-facing only, and the truncation was wrong twice before it was right.** Rays draw for the 4 `FACE_*` types only; rotating and walking trainers get a hollow marker. Across **432 Kanto trainers**: **261 fixed (60.4%) / 97 rotate in place (22.5%) / 74 walk (17.1%)**. **Routes are the WORST-covered map kind at 53.7%** (outdoor dungeons 77.6%, interiors 60.2%) — the opposite of an early guess based on the 8-map corridor's unrepresentative 85%. Zero Kanto trainers are `TRAINER_TYPE_SEE_ALL_DIRECTIONS`, so the single-direction model is correct for all of them. **Correction 1**: `CheckPathBetweenTrainerAndPlayer` (`trainer_see.c:708-742`) masks out **only** `COLLISION_OUTSIDE_RANGE`, and `GetCollisionFlagsAtCoords` (`event_object_movement.c:6561`) raises four flags — so **directional impassability, elevation mismatch and another object event each stop a sight line**. The first implementation checked the collision bit alone and drew rays too long. Now walks `StepResolver.resolve()` (so the line can never disagree with the movement rules it describes) plus an object-event check, since the resolver models terrain and knows nothing about placed entities; only `npc`/`trainer`/`item` block, because warps/triggers/signs come from separate source arrays and occupy no collision slot. **Correction 2**: **ledges STOP sight** — measured across **all 421 imported maps and all eight `MB_JUMP_*` behaviours: 1042 of 1042 carry `collision = 1`, no exception** (EAST 39, WEST 41, SOUTH 962; NORTH and all four diagonals occur zero times). This matters because `resolve()` tests ledges *before* collision and reports `LEDGE_JUMP` — a redirect, correct for walking and wrong for looking — so any outcome other than `NONE` ends the line. *An earlier "155 of 155" figure sampled 80 maps using ids 59-62, which is `JUMP_SOUTH` plus three diagonals, missing east and west entirely; the conclusion survived re-measurement, the sample never supported it.*

**Stacked cells — 22 in Kanto, all legitimate, across SIX idioms not three.** Every one was read: **warp + sign 5** (a Gym door that is also readable), **gated triggers 6** (paired `coord_events` on one VAR at DIFFERENT values — Route 22's three *and* Oak's Lab's three, the game's opening sequence), **scriptless cutscene actors 4**, **hidden item beneath an obstacle 4** (Snorlax on `ITEM_LEFTOVERS` ×2, Mr Fuji on `ITEM_SOOTHE_BELL`, Giovanni on `ITEM_MACHO_BRACE`), **readable prop 2** (`OBJ_EVENT_GFX_CLIPBOARD` supplying the visual, a `bg_event` supplying the text), **trigger under an NPC 1**. The last three were missed by a five-cell reading that claimed three idioms, and they are exactly the ones a loud marker would misrepresent. Exclusivity of the gated triggers is guaranteed twice: distinct `var_value`s in the data, and `GetCoordEventScriptAtPosition` (`field_control_avatar.c:1180-1196`) **returns on the first match**, so even a same-value pair could not double-fire. **Marker is deliberately calm** — shouting about a normal idiom on 18 of 419 maps trains the eye to skip it.

**Counts banner**, gated to `EVENTS` as a sibling of the review banner rather than by widening its gate (that one counts unconfirmed CELL defaults and belongs to `PROVENANCE`). Separates **pending by design** (dead doors, stacks) from **defects** (undefined warp constants, typo'd `movement_type`); only defects colour the border.

**Baker guard (`_scene_divergence`)** — wires reproducibility into `map_baker.gd` itself, refusing to overwrite a scene that is not what the bake would produce unless `--force`, and printing the differing region windowed on the first differing CHARACTER (head-truncation printed two identical-looking lines and hid a real cause for three runs). Derived, not declared: a per-entity `authored` flag was rejected because `@export` setters fire on every scene load, so it cannot be set automatically and a flag you must remember to tick is one that eventually is not. **Verified: `.tres`-only changes do NOT trip the scene guard**, so M27C's connections re-bake will not need `--force`.

✅ **FIXED — and the drift was never the bug.** [This bullet read "UNFIXED — the guard's scratch save perturbs the real save", and proposed a single-save restructure as the recommended fix. Measuring first killed that plan; the old text is superseded, not merely updated.] The symptom was real: `check_bake_diff.py` reported `Route1_Frlg` non-reproducible, permanently, with one more map joining per non-`--force` bake. **Measured 2026-07-29 before changing anything: every differing line was a `SubResource` id — four ids and their references, nothing semantic — and it is fully DETERMINISTIC**, two consecutive runs producing byte-identical id sets. So the ids are neither random nor content-derived: they are assigned per `ResourceSaver` call in process order, which means a guarded bake (scratch save first) and a `--force` bake produce different sets for a byte-identical scene. **The value records how many times the saver ran, not anything about the map.**

That makes the real defect an ASYMMETRY between two tools, not the double save. `map_baker._normalise_text` already stripped these labels and was right to; `check_bake_diff.normalize()` did not, on an explicit written premise — *"it compares two saves to the same path, where the ids are stable and a difference would be real"* — **both halves of which are false**. A rule that differed between the two implementations is exactly how the false positive survived. Fixed by giving `check_bake_diff` the same three label rules (keep the type stem, drop only the random suffix), with both docstrings now cross-referencing each other and recording that they must stay in step. **`check_bake_diff --all`: 8 of 8 reproducible** — no map needed re-baking, since the difference normalises away rather than needing to be rewritten.

**Loosening a detector demands proving it still detects**: injected `sight_range = 5` → `99` into `ViridianForest_Frlg`, and the check still exits 1 and names that edit and only that edit — cleaner than before, because the id noise that used to surround a real change is gone. Restored from backup and re-verified.

**The double save REMAINS, deliberately.** It is now inert (both tools normalise what it perturbs), and the two ways to remove it are both worse: packing a second TileSet cannot be shown to help without understanding Godot's id generator, and saving before deciding would put a crash window in the one guard protecting hand-authored entity data. Flagged, not chased. Also corrected while here: a comment claiming the scratch file lives in `user://` while the line beneath it wrote to `scenes/maps/`, contradicting `_remove_scratch()`'s own docstring.

⚠️ **`check_bake_diff.py` has no automated coverage.** Section W tests the baker's copy of these rules; the Python copy is verified only by the both-directions run above. Two hand-kept implementations of one rule set is a real drift risk, documented at both sites rather than solved.

**[M27C C1 — connections and the border block in the pipeline] COMPLETE — 2026-07-29.** The stitching metadata M27C needs, emitted and round-tripped; consumed by nothing yet (C2/C4 build the loader). `gen_map_import.py` gains `extract_connections()` and border reading; `MapData` gains `connections` / `border` / `border_width` / `border_height`, a `Connection` enum whose ordinals match the reference's own (`include/constants/global.h`), and `loadable_connections()`. **421/421 maps regenerated with every guard green and the cell histograms byte-identical**; **8/8 corridor maps re-baked WITHOUT `--force`** and not refused, which is the first real confirmation that a `.tres`-only field addition does not trip the scene guard; `check_bake_diff --all` 8 of 8.

**Three things measurement caught that sampling would not.** (1) **Offset is load-bearing** — 104 of 266 connections in the reference and **12 of the corridor's 15** carry a nonzero one (Route 3 at +60), and Pallet Town, the obvious fixture, is the ONE corridor map whose offsets are both 0. (2) **Border dimensions are not uniform** — 441 of 785 layouts omit them entirely and every one of those ships a 4-entry `border.bin`, so 2x2 is the reference's own default, but **7 layouts declare 3x2 and Viridian Forest is one of them**, already in the corridor; a hardcoded 2x2 would have mis-shaped a map that ships today. (3) **`map.json`'s "up" is NORTH and "down" is SOUTH** — a crossover that would stitch the region inside out while still looking plausible, so it is asserted rather than commented.

**Destinations stay raw `MAP_*` constants**, matching what `Warp.dest_map` already stores, so both kinds of link resolve through the one `MapConstants` table — and so "source does not define this destination" (a bug) stays distinguishable from "defined but not baked" (the expected M27C gap). `loadable_connections()` is the question a chunk loader actually asks: it drops DIVE/EMERGE (real in the data — 7 each — but §1 settles that they warp rather than stitching) and anything unbaked. **The corridor has 15 connections of which 3 dangle**, so "has a connection" and "has a neighbour to load" are genuinely different questions, and an edge failing the second is one the border skirt must cover. **Viridian Forest has NO connections at all** — warp-only, like an interior — so the stitchable subgraph is 7 maps, not 8.

**Tests.** New **Section Y**, 13 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **294 → 307** (308 reported with Z.99), gated **104 → 117**, both re-measured from real runs. Every assertion reads a real baked artifact rather than a fixture, deliberately: the failure being guarded is the one Change 1 nearly shipped — a field declared, written to JSON, never read back, round-tripping empty while looking present. Y.08/Y.09 check actual TYPES, since JSON returning floats and typed-`Array[Dictionary]` assignment failing silently are both this project's own documented gotchas. Fixtures chosen to discriminate rather than for convenience: Viridian Forest is simultaneously the zero-connection map and a 3x2 border, so it catches a hardcoded border AND an assumption that every map stitches. Also closed a fragility introduced in the same session — the section loads three artifacts but originally gated on two, so a partial checkout would have crashed instead of gating and the count would never have balanced to say so.

**[M27C C2 — the generic overworld controller] COMPLETE — 2026-07-29.** `scenes/overworld/pallet_town.tscn`/`.gd` are **DELETED**, not extended, per the scope doc's own note; `scenes/overworld/overworld.tscn`/`.gd` replace them, with a new `MapManager` (`scripts/overworld/map_manager.gd`) as a real scene-tree node rather than something built in code. Movement, camera and the elevation reparent carried over essentially unchanged — they were correct, and this is a restructure, not a rewrite. Which map you start in is now an `@export`, not a `const` baked into a scene name. Verified end to end: the scene boots headless and reports *"in PalletTown_Frlg at (12, 0) (1 chunk(s) live) — 24x20, 2 connection(s), 1 loadable"*, i.e. C1's data read by C2's controller with the dangling Route 21 North correctly excluded.

**THE POINT IS THE COORDINATE SPACE, not the loading.** Every cell coordinate crossing the `MapManager` boundary is now GLOBAL, with each chunk holding the offset to its own local cells. Today one chunk is live at origin (0,0), so global and local are equal and the conversion is a no-op — introducing it *now*, while it costs nothing, is deliberate. The alternative is a player controller written against map-local cells that must be re-plumbed when C4 loads neighbours, and **that failure mode is silent**, because everything works perfectly on the one map being tested. `strata_at()` is looked up per cell for the same reason: at a seam the correct parent belongs to a DIFFERENT chunk, so a container cached at spawn would keep parenting the player into the map it started in.

**Every positional assertion in Section AA uses a NONZERO origin**, and AA.06 explicitly asserts the conversion is not the identity — because an origin of (0,0) cannot distinguish a correct conversion from a missing one, and a manager that had forgotten to convert at all would pass every test written against the live configuration. Routing is proven by the two synthetic chunks carrying DIFFERENT per-cell values, so a query reaching the wrong chunk gives a wrong answer rather than a right one by coincidence. Unowned cells fail safe in both directions — solid, elevation 0 — so a step into nowhere is refused and nothing falls through the world before C3's skirt exists.

**Deliberately NOT built (that is C4):** deriving a neighbour's origin from a connection's direction and offset, loading neighbours, hysteresis unload, and cross-seam stepping. `resolve_step()` hands the owning chunk its own local cells and translates the answer back, but `StepResolver` is built against a single `MapData` and cannot span a seam; resolvers are cached per chunk, which is the shape that stays right when C4 arrives.

**Tests.** Section **AA**, 14 assertions, **ungated** — the maths has nothing to do with baked artifacts, and gating it would leave a fresh checkout with no coverage of the one thing C2 introduces. `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **307 → 321** (322 with Z.99), gated unchanged at 117; both paths re-measured from real runs (322/322 full, 205/205 fresh-checkout). A real leak was caught by the engine's own warning rather than by any assertion on this section's first run — `register_chunk()` deliberately does not parent the chunk root (only `load_chunk()` does, since a synthetic chunk has no scene to own it), so the test's roots are the test's to free.

**[M27C C3 — the border skirt] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30.** Walking to a map edge no longer shows void. `MapManager` paints each chunk's border block outward at load time into layers it owns, never into the baked scene — the scene is a reproducible artifact, and which edges need a skirt is not a property of the map anyway, it changes as C4 loads and unloads neighbours.

**The rule is OWNERSHIP, not connections**, and the corridor proves why that matters. "Skip edges that have a connection" would leave four real edges as void: **Viridian City north and south (24 cells each), Pewter City south (24) and east (20)** — because Viridian City is 48 wide while Routes 1 and 2 are 24 and sit at offset 12, so those edges are *connected and half-uncovered*. Pallet Town happens to be a perfect fit (24 wide, Route 1 also 24, offset 0), which is exactly why reasoning from it would have produced the wrong rule — the same trap its `offset: 0` set during C1. Keying on `chunk_owning(cell) == ""` also means C4 needs no new edge logic: a loaded neighbour takes ownership and a repaint clears the skirt over it. Matches source, whose `GetMapGridBlockAt` falls through to `GetBorderBlockAt` per CELL and never asks whether an edge is connected.

**Impassability comes free.** `GetBorderBlockAt` returns `| MAPGRID_COLLISION_MASK` — solid is inherent to the getter, not a separate rule — and `MapManager` already answers unowned cells as solid at elevation 0. So the skirt is purely visual over a query that was already correct, which is the simpler of the two options and the one source supports.

⚠️ **A SCREENSHOT CAUGHT WHAT THE DATA COULD NOT.** The headless check reported 2432 skirt cells against 2432 expected, the layer at child index 0, the right TileSet, and correctly no overpaint inside the map — every measurable property correct. It still rendered with **every other row blank**. A metatile routes to one or two of the three planes (§1.6) and the first cut painted only the ground plane: Pallet Town's border ids 28/29 are COVERED (ground+objects) but **20/21 are NORMAL — objects+overhangs, nothing on ground at all**. This forced a C1 addendum, `border_layer_type`, emitted parallel to `border`, since C1 had no reason to know the skirt would need it. **The same bug is waiting for hand-painting** — painting a NORMAL metatile into Ground alone renders nothing — which is the concrete reason the authoring block below needs a routing step.

**Depth 16 is measured**: the canvas is 1024x768 and the overworld camera runs at zoom 3, giving 21.3 x 16 cells visible and a ~11 x 8 half-extent when standing on an edge cell. 16 covers the wider axis with headroom for the camera's own smoothing overshoot and for non-native window sizes.

**Tests.** Section **AB**, 13 assertions, ungated. Most of them are the NEGATIVE MODULO: source's `GetBorderBlockAt` biases by `8 * borderWidth` before `%` purely because C keeps the dividend's sign, and GDScript behaves identically, so a direct transliteration reads a negative index — and roughly half of all skirt cells have a negative local coordinate on at least one axis, so that is the common case rather than an edge case. AB.08/AB.09 cover 3x2 borders because Viridian Forest is one of the seven and a 2x2 assumption would mis-tile a map already in the corridor. `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **321 → 334** (335 with Z.99). Verified by real screenshot after the routing fix: continuous canopy, correct parity, meeting the map's own tree line with no seam.

**Turning the skirt off is possible but implicit** — `_paint_skirt` bails on an empty `border`, and no imported map has one (0 of 421). That conflates "deliberately none" with "lost its border", which is the ambiguity shape this milestone keeps having to fix; a real opt-out wants an explicit flag. Worth knowing the reference never turns borders off either — interiors use a uniform void block (`8, 8, 8, 8`) rather than nothing.

✅ **`unload_chunk()` now repaints** (closed in C4). It did not, which would have left a hole where a neighbour's cells used to be owned — unreachable while only one chunk was live.

**[M27C C4 — stitching, and the crossing stutter] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30.** Pallet Town and Route 1 render contiguously and you walk between them. Neighbour placement is ported from the four `Fill*Connection` functions with `MAP_OFFSET` dropped (it is only the backup grid's padding). **The asymmetry is load-bearing**: `offset` shifts along the SHARED EDGE, but the perpendicular term uses the HOST's dimension going south/east and the NEIGHBOUR's going north/west — written uniformly it looks tidy and stitches the region inside out. Validated by walking the corridor graph: **12 edges, zero reciprocal conflicts**, producing real Kanto geography.

**`StepResolver` now takes a cell SOURCE rather than a `MapData`.** The seam is exactly four methods (`in_bounds`, `behavior_at`, `collision_at`, `elevation_at`), so only `resolve()` and its two helpers moved; `cell_info()` stays `MapData`-only as the overlay's per-map surface. `MapManager` supplies those four in global cells through an explicit `GlobalCells` adapter rather than duck-typed name matching. One rule set means §1.7's two-sided check works across a boundary whose two tiles live in different `MapData`s — a second implementation would be the two-hand-kept-copies failure this milestone already paid for with `check_bake_diff`.

⚠️ **THREE BUGS ROB FOUND BY LOOKING, NONE VISIBLE TO THE SUITE.** (1) The player teleported to the top of Route 1, exactly that map's height off: chunk roots sit at `origin * CELL`, so a child's `position` is already relative to the origin, and the step tween was assigning a GLOBAL cell's pixels there. (2) Crossing produced a fast pan from the new chunk's corner: a `Camera2D` carries its smoothing state as a position that gets reinterpreted on reparent — measured jumping to `(192,-640)`, precisely Route 1's origin, while its actual global position was correct. The camera now lives outside the chunk tree, which also removes a second hazard, since a chunk root is freed on unload and anything parented into it goes too. (3) Stepping back into Pallet Town the character snapped behind the grass: draw order was scene-tree order, which is CHUNK-major. Now plane-major via `z_index`, with entities straddling Overhangs at 2 and 4 so an entity outranks any chunk's ground while still passing under a canopy — putting entities simply on top would have fixed the glitch and broken the two strata. **All three were invisible at a single chunk on origin (0,0)**, which is exactly what C2's own comment warned about and what I then walked into anyway.

**The crossing stutter: 41.3 ms in a single `_try_step`, now 7.5 ms**, confirmed smooth by hand. **Measuring first killed two of my own assumptions.** (a) My first profile measured single chunk loads and reported 9.3 ms — the wrong thing entirely: crossing INTO Route 1 does not load Route 1, live since startup, it loads **Viridian City**, a cold tileset pair. (b) **Resource parsing was the bulk**, ~28 of the 41 ms — not the skirt, which I had assumed and asserted. `ResourceLoader.load_threaded_request` moves it off-thread; `request_neighbours()` is non-blocking and `_poll_pending()` installs at most ONE finished chunk per frame so arrivals cannot stack. The origin cannot be computed at request time (north/west needs the NEIGHBOUR's dimensions), so the host frame is recorded and the origin derived on completion; neighbours land ~112 ms after the step, ample against a whole map to cross. (c) Deferring the skirt a frame barely helped (14.6 → 13.2 ms), disproving the remaining assumption that instantiate mattered — it is ~0.6 ms. (d) **The real remainder was `chunk_owning()` called PER CELL** — a Dictionary walk, a `Vector2i` allocation and a `String` return, ~4000 times per repaint; resolving ownership to `Rect2i`s once gives the identical answer for 7.5 ms less. `refresh_skirts_near(rect)` also replaces the full sweep, since only chunks whose skirt region reaches the changed cells can differ.

**Skirt depth is PER AXIS (12 x 9), not a flat 16.** Measured: 1024x768 at zoom 3 shows 21.3 x 16.0 cells, a 10.7 x 8.0 half-extent from an edge cell, plus one for the camera lag measured at 11 px. One depth sized for the wider axis over-covered the vertical by 100% — 2432 skirt cells around 480 real ones became 1344. **An earlier version of that comment justified 16 as headroom for non-native window sizes; that was wrong** — stretch is `canvas_items` with aspect `keep`, so the visible WORLD area is fixed and letterboxing preserves it. Verified at the worst case (Pallet Town's bottom-left corner, two exposed edges) that no void shows.

**Hysteresis: load on arrival, unload NOTHING.** Rob's call, and the measurements support it — stitchable fan-out is at most 4 and **360 of 421 maps have none at all**, so this tops out near 5 live chunks. **The reference offers no guidance at all**: `LoadMapFromCameraTransition` keeps ONE map live and swaps it wholesale, neighbours existing only as 7 cells of edge fill. There is no streaming and no hysteresis to port, so this is deliberate original design.

**Tests.** Section **AC**, 22 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **334 → 356**. AC.05/AC.06 use maps of distinct width and height, because with equal dimensions both origin formulas agree and the test proves nothing; AC.17 asserts the local pixel is NOT the global one at a nonzero origin, the whole shape of the teleport bug; AC.21 pins the entity straddle rather than just the ordering.

**[M27C — warps decoupled from tile behaviour, and the Pallet/Viridian interiors] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30.** Two changes, one enabling the other.

**A warp now fires because it is a warp**, not because it stands on a warp-behaviour tile. `Warp` gained `@export var triggers: bool = true`; the importer stamps it from the destination cell's behaviour so imported maps keep source's own semantics, and a hand-placed warp on a plain floor tile just works. **Step 0 is why this was safe to do rather than obvious**: warps fire through FOUR paths in source, not one — `IsWarpMetatileBehavior`, `IsArrowWarpMetatileBehavior`, `IsDirectionalStairWarpMetatileBehavior` and `TryDoorWarp`. Reading only the first would have marked **103 of the corridor's warps inert** while looking correct. `WARP_TRIGGER_BEHAVIORS` carries all 24 names for that reason. The behaviour is still imported and still drives presentation (a door animates, a ladder does not); it just no longer decides whether the warp exists.

**10 interiors baked** — Oak's Lab, the Player's House (both floors) and the Rival's House in Pallet; the Viridian Gym, Mart, School, House and Pokécenter (both floors) — taking the corridor to **18 maps, 18/18 reproducible**, and giving C5 somewhere to warp TO. **36 of the 63 warps on baked maps now resolve to a baked destination**; the 27 that dangle are all Pewter/Route 2/Route 22 interiors, outside this batch's scope. Interiors carry no connections at all (warp-only, like Viridian Forest), so the stitching graph is unchanged.

**TradeCenter and UnionRoom are EXCLUDED, deliberately.** They are link-cable rooms: no wild encounters, no NPCs, no story, reachable only through multiplayer infrastructure this project has none of and has no roadmap slot for. Recorded here because the exclusion is a judgment call, not a gap — a future session finding two unbaked interiors in Viridian should not "fix" it. Revisit only if link/trade is ever scoped (it is listed among `docs/overworld_scope.md`'s own confirmed exclusions).

⚠️ **A bake bug that only appears in batches, and the batch is now the normal case.** Baking all 18 at once made Route 22 non-reproducible; baking it alone did not. Two coord-event triggers sit on the SAME cell, so both derived the same node name, and Godot silently renames the second from a PROCESS-WIDE counter (`@Node2D@N`) — a value that depends on how many nodes were created earlier in that run. Fixed with `_unique_name(base, used)` per bake. **Nothing was lost** — 6 triggers in, 6 in the scene; Godot renames rather than drops, so the damage was reproducibility and unreadable editor names, not data. Worth knowing that anything deriving a node name from map data needs a collision policy, because the failure is invisible at one map.

**Stacked triggers are mutually exclusive twice over**, checked because 22 Kanto cells stack events and a naive port could double-fire. `GetCoordEventScriptAtPosition` (`field_control_avatar.c:1180-1196`) does NOT stop at the first positional match — it stops at the first event that also passes `ShouldTriggerScriptRun`, continuing past ones whose variable does not match. Route 22's pair gates on the same VAR at DIFFERENT values, so at most one can ever qualify; order only decides ties, and there are none. Two cases in that function never return a script at all and matter for M27G: a NULL script runs `DoCoordEventWeather` instead (coord events double as weather tiles), and `TRIGGER_RUN_IMMEDIATELY` runs inline and lets the scan CONTINUE, so two events on one cell can both act if the first is immediate-run.

**[M27C C5 — warp dispatch] SCOPED 2026-07-30, C5-1 COMPLETE.**

**A WARP IS A HARD BOUNDARY, A CONNECTION IS A SOFT ONE.** Rob's call, and it is more source-faithful than what preceded it — `LoadMapFromCameraTransition` keeps ONE map live and swaps it wholesale, so the connection streaming C4 built is already original design past source. On a warp: fade out, drop EVERY chunk, load the destination and its connected neighbours, fade in. On a step across a connection: stream exactly as now. The player walks across a connection and expects seamlessness; they take a door and expect a cut, so the fade is correct presentation rather than a cover-up.

**This REMOVED work rather than adding it.** Two things fall out: the destination goes at origin `(0, 0)` every time, because nothing else is live to collide with it — so the free-origin allocator, the stride rule and the overlap constraint on `chunk_owning()` (first-match-wins over an UNORDERED Dictionary, so overlapping chunks answer nondeterministically) all stop existing. And the `Camera2D` smoothing reset that cost C4 a real bug at a 24-cell seam is hidden entirely, at a moment we choose. Timing has room: Pallet plus neighbours landed ~112 ms threaded against a 300-500 ms fade. If a large map ever exceeds it the fix is to hold the fade until the load reports done, not to guess a duration.

**Two things deliberately NOT done.** Caching the map you just left would halve the common case (into a house and straight back out) but reintroduces exactly the two-chunks-live placement question this decision deletes, to hide latency already hidden — measure a specific slow door first. And placing interiors near their exterior was rejected on its own merits, not just as moot: an interior HAS no exterior, only incoming warps, so it needs a reverse index across all 421 maps; the relation is a graph rather than a tree (a Pokécentre's 2F is entered from its 1F, so the chain must resolve transitively, and a forest gate has two); and an interior placed adjacent to its town would render beside it and be visible at a seam. Real bookkeeping, real ambiguity, negative runtime value.

**C5-1 — resolution. COMPLETE.** `Warp.warp_id` records a warp's OWN index, emitted by the importer because that is the only place the ordering is authoritative. **A `dest_warp_id` addresses a warp BY POSITION**, so every consumer must agree on it — and until now they agreed only because the importer appends warps contiguously and the baker emits in array order: true, load-bearing, asserted nowhere. A reorder would send every arrival in Kanto to the wrong tile and read as a content bug rather than a pipeline one. Defaults **-1, meaning not addressable**, so an unresolvable lookup fails loudly instead of landing on whichever warp happened to be first.

New `MapManager.warp_arrival(map_name, warp_id)` returns `{}` or a GLOBAL cell, reading the destination's own live `Warp` nodes rather than a copy in `MapData` — one source of truth for a warp's position, and no second place to keep in step. **Source lands the player ON the destination warp's own tile** (`SetPlayerCoordsFromWarp`, `overworld.c:685-687`, assigning `warps[warpId].x/y` with no adjacent-tile or facing offset), so entering Oak's Lab leaves you standing on the lab's door. **That does not re-trigger and needs no guard** — warps fire from `TryStartStepBasedScript` under `input->tookStep`, and arriving is not a step. ⚠️ **The guard is free ONLY while the check lives in the step path**: a per-frame "am I on a warp" poll would bounce the player between two doors forever. `warp_at(gcell)` is scoped to the OWNING chunk for the same reason a seam needs it, and treats `triggers == false` as ABSENT rather than disabled — those cells are the flanking tiles of multi-tile doorways, and reading them as present would widen every door in the region.

421/421 re-imported with the histograms byte-identical, 18 re-baked, **18/18 still reproducible**. Verified round trip on real artifacts: Pallet's warp 2 is the lab door at `(16, 13)`; Oak's Lab has THREE warps — `(5,12)`, `(6,12)`, `(7,12)` — all addressing that one slot, exactly the multi-tile doorway `warp.gd`'s own comment predicted.

**Tests.** Section **AD**, 15 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **356 → 371**. AD.01/AD.02 sweep the whole baked corridor rather than spot-checking one map, and check the indices are DENSE `0..n-1` — a gap or a duplicate is what actually breaks a positional id, not merely a missing field. AD.05/AD.06 place the chunk at a nonzero origin and assert the arrival is NOT the local cell, the exact shape of C4's teleport bug. AD.09 pins that -1 does not fall back to the first warp, the tempting bug that always resolves and always to the wrong tile. AD.14/AD.15 walk the round trip in both directions rather than trusting one, and were **proven to fail when broken** before being trusted — the totals balance identically whether a section runs or gates, so a passing count alone proves nothing here.

**[M27C C5-3 — warp dispatch] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30. You can walk into a building and back out.**

`_do_warp` is the hard boundary made real: fade out, move the player OUT of its chunk, `unload_all()`, load the destination alone at `(0, 0)`, resolve the arrival, re-parent, snap the camera, reload neighbours, fade in. **The unload is what makes the rest cheap** — with nothing else live the destination always goes at the origin, so no allocator, no stride, and no chance for `chunk_owning()`'s first-match-over-an-unordered-Dictionary to be asked an ambiguous question.

⚠️ **THE WARP CHECK LIVES ON THE STEP TWEEN'S COMPLETION AND NOWHERE ELSE.** Source fires warps from `TryStartStepBasedScript` under `input->tookStep`, and that is the entire reason arriving on a warp tile needs no guard against bouncing straight back: arriving is not a step. A per-frame "am I standing on a warp" poll would look equivalent and would ping-pong the player between two doors forever.

**A dead door is refused BEFORE anything is torn down** — an unbaked destination leaves the player standing where they are rather than stranded in an empty region. 27 of the corridor's 63 warps are in that state today, so this path runs constantly rather than being theoretical. A resolvable map with an unresolvable slot degrades to `_first_walkable()` instead, shared with spawning so a fallback arrival cannot drift from where a fresh start would put you.

`FADE_SECONDS` is **a feel value, not a ported constant** — source's own fades vary per call site and nothing depends on matching one. It only has to outlast the load it hides (~112 ms threaded against a 250 ms leg); if a large map ever exceeds it, hold the fade until the load reports done rather than raising it blindly.

⚠️ **C5-3 SHIPPED SEALED AND ROB FOUND IT BY PLAYING: "no warp at all, door grid space is blocked and nothing happens."** The step-on path was built, tested and correct, and it **can never fire for a door** — a door tile is SOLID, so the player cannot stand on it. My headless proof had set `_cell` to the door directly, which is exactly the state a real step can never reach, so the test agreed with the code and both were wrong about the game.

**Source has TWO warp geometries and the first cut ported one.** `TryStartWarpEventScript` reads the player's OWN tile after `tookStep`; `TryDoorWarp` reads the tile IN FRONT on a held direction. I had read all four trigger paths while building the `triggers` flag and carried across *which behaviours fire* without noticing they do not all fire from the same PLACE.

**Measured across all 421 maps rather than inferred:** of 1294 warps, **1015 sit on walkable tiles** (step-on works) and **279 on solid ones** — 193 of those `MB_ANIMATED_DOOR`. Source's `DIR_NORTH` restriction is safe to port exactly rather than generalise, because **all 193 doors have exactly one walkable neighbour and it is always the tile to the south** — zero with none, zero with more than one.

New `_try_door_warp()` fires on a BLOCKED step, keyed on **collision rather than the door behaviour** — consistent with C5's own decoupling, since which geometry is even POSSIBLE is decided by whether the tile can be stood on. A hand-placed warp on a wall becomes a door and one on a floor becomes a step-on warp, with nothing to remember to set. Disclosed divergence: this makes 5 solid non-door warps (4 `MB_CAVE`, 1 `MB_OCEAN_WATER`) live where source fires them from nothing — none in the corridor, and presence-decides is the call `triggers` already made.

**Tests.** Section **AF**, 5 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **387 → 392**. AF.01/AF.02 pin the MAP-DATA facts that force the distinction, so the reason survives the code moving. **AF.04 needs a SYNTHETIC position, and that is itself the finding**: aiming a non-north direction at a door is impossible in real data because every door's other three neighbours are solid, so the player is placed on one to construct the only case that distinguishes the rule from "any solid warp opens". Both breaks verified — removing the door path fails AF.03, removing the NORTH restriction fails AF.04 and AF.05. An earlier draft of AF.04 walked south from below the door and passed either way; it proved nothing, because walking away from a door does not test the rule.

**Tests.** Section **AE**, 16 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **371 → 387**, awaited rather than fire-and-forget since `_do_warp` fades. ⚠️ **One assertion was found VACUOUS by trying to break it, and the finding is worth more than the assertion was.** "The player survived the teardown" passed with the guard REMOVED: `queue_free()` is deferred, and the re-parent into the new chunk happens in the same frame, so the player currently survives by frame timing rather than by the code that exists to protect it. The guard is real but is protecting against a frame boundary nobody has introduced yet — **a threaded load in that window would be enough**. So AE.04 pins the hazard directly instead, parenting a marker into a chunk and proving `unload_all()` frees it. AE.06/AE.08/AE.15 (dead-door refusal, arrival cell, round-trip reciprocity) were each broken and confirmed failing before being trusted; the totals balance identically whether a section runs or gates, so a passing count proves nothing here on its own.

**[M27C C5-4 — leaving a building] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30.** Rob, after C5-3: *"the character comes through to the other side and stays on the warp they landed on... if you try to enter back into the house you need to step off the door then back on."* One complaint, **two missing source behaviours** behind it.

**1. The exit step.** `SetUpWarpExitTask` (`field_screen_effect.c`) picks an exit task by ARRIVAL-TILE KIND: a door gets `Task_ExitDoor`, which waits for the fade and then issues a literal `MOVEMENT_ACTION_WALK_NORMAL_DOWN`; a ladder or floor warp gets `Task_ExitNonDoor`, which moves the player NOWHERE. So the step is conditional, and the condition is the one the door-entry path already uses — **a solid arrival tile is a doorway, a walkable one is not**. That also makes it a CORRECTNESS fix rather than presentation: a player left on a solid tile is standing inside a wall. SOUTH is source's own literal (the facing-direction variant is `Task_ExitNonAnimDoor`, a different tile kind) and mirrors the north-only entry rule.

**2. Arrow warps — a THIRD trigger geometry, and the one that leaves nearly every building.** `TryArrowWarp` reads the player's OWN tile on a held direction, **before any step is attempted**, so it fires whether or not the target is walkable. That is the point: Oak's Lab exits SOUTH into the map's back wall, and every interior is the same shape. Without it you could still get out — by stepping off the exit tile and back onto it through the step-on path — which is precisely the awkwardness Rob reported, indoors as well as out.

⚠️ **The direction must be STAMPED, and the tempting shortcut is measurably wrong.** "The one blocked neighbour is the exit" fails on **64 of 345** arrow warps region-wide (zero or several blocked neighbours), so a derived guess would fire the wrong way on nearly a fifth of them. New `Warp.arrow_dir`, stamped by the importer from source's own `MetatileBehavior_Is*ArrowWarp` sets — which are **wider than the `ARROW_WARP` names alone**: water-south and the shoal entrance count as south, the abandoned-ship stairs as north.

**All three geometries now present**, which is the shape of the whole area and worth stating once: `TryStartWarpEventScript` (own tile, after a step) · `TryDoorWarp` (tile in front, walking north) · `TryArrowWarp` (own tile, pressing the arrow's direction, before any step).

421/421 re-imported, 18 re-baked, **18/18 reproducible**. Verified end to end: walk north into Pallet's lab door → inside at `(6,12)` with `arrow_dir` SOUTH → press south → out, standing at `(16,14)` on walkable ground with the door directly north → walk north again, straight back in, no shuffling.

**Tests.** Section **AG**, 10 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **392 → 402**. AG.01 pins that the outdoor door tile is SOLID, which is why the exit step is correctness rather than polish; AG.05 pins that the interior exit faces a wall, which is why a step-based check could never fire there. Both breaks verified — removing the exit step fails AG.08/AG.09/AG.10, removing arrow dispatch fails AG.07/AG.09. **AE.15 was updated, not weakened**: it asserted the player returns to the door cell, which encoded the bug; it now asserts they end up outside it on walkable ground.

**[M27C C5-4 follow-up — the escalator] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30.** Rob: *"Excellent except the escalator in the pokecenter."* Same defect as the door, different direction — **and the reason the door fix missed it is the lesson.**

That fix inferred the exit from COLLISION: *a solid arrival tile is a doorway, so step south.* Right for all 193 doors in Kanto, and it says nothing whatever about an escalator, whose tile is **walkable**. An inference that happens to be correct across one whole category is not a rule, and this is what it costs to find that out.
