**Six more opcodes closed alongside it**, each independently source-verified against `scrcmd.c`: `checkplayergender` (reads the real `PlayerIdentity.gender` via `TextBuffers.active_identity()`, matching `gSaveBlock2Ptr->playerGender`'s own 0/1 encoding exactly — no remapping needed since M27K K-b already built real gender selection); `random` (`Random() % max`, a defensive `limit<=0` guard added since C's own `%0` is undefined behavior no real script triggers); `setorcopyvar` (source keeps a real raw-storage-vs-`VarGet` distinction between this and `copyvar`, but this project's own `FlagStore.var_get` is already the one accessor every var read goes through regardless of opcode, so the two are indistinguishable here — disclosed rather than silently assumed identical); `waitse`/`playmoncry`/`waitmoncry` (the same audio-does-not-exist no-op class as the pre-existing `playfanfare` group); `bufferboxname` (a real HALT, not a no-op — mirrors `_begin_nickname`'s own PC-branch precedent exactly: there is no PC (I5-5, still deferred), so there is no real box name to buffer, and inventing one would be the same "near-miss that reads as working" this project's PC-nickname gap already refuses to risk); `fadescreenspeed`/`fadescreenswapbuffers` (the two `fadescreen` siblings named-but-excluded when that opcode shipped, same no-op reasoning now extended to both).

**A symbolic-constant table closes `addvar`/`subvar`/`checkmoney`/`addmoney`/`removemoney`'s own long-disclosed gap** — 11 real constants (`ROULETTE_SPECIAL_RATE=1<<7`, 7 flute/furniture prices, `MAGIKARP_PRICE`, `COINS_PRICE_50`/`_500`), each individually confirmed via direct source citation (`.set`/`.equ` in the owning map script, or a `#define` in `include/constants/roulette.h`). ⚠️ **Every one of them is confirmed Hoenn-only content** (Route 113's Glass Workshop, Mauville's Game Corner, the Route 4 Pokémon Center) — not merely outside the current corridor, content this Kanto project will never author a map for at all. Kept anyway since the values are cheap and sourced, and "resolves to a real number" is strictly safer than the fail-closed 0 it replaces.

⚠️ **A real, expected regression was found and fixed in `m27i_wallet_test.gd`'s own pre-existing C.05/C.05b/C.05c.** Those three assertions had `COINS_PRICE_500` as their own worked example of "an unresolvable price fails closed" — exactly the gap this session just closed, so they started failing the moment the fix landed. The same "a genuine correctness fix legitimately invalidates a stale test assumption" shape this project has hit before (Nature/Solar-Beam/ban-flag regressions, etc.): rewritten to confirm `COINS_PRICE_500` now resolves to its real value (10000) and the wallet actually charges it, with a NEW C.05d/e/f added on a deliberately synthetic constant name (`SOME_UNRESOLVED_PRICE_CONSTANT`) to keep the fail-closed path covered without the guard being invalidated again the next time a real constant is added to the table. `m27i_wallet_test` grew 30→33 (34/34 incl. its own Z.99).

New assertions added directly to `m27f_script_vm_test.gd` as Sections L (trainer-battle family, 16 assertions incl. the key already-beaten-bypass discriminator and a same-trainer `trainerbattle_single` negative control proving it's real) and M (the small opcode batch + symbolic constants, 14 assertions incl. a 200-sample statistical range check for `random` and a genuinely-unlisted-constant regression guard proving the table is additive, not a blanket resolve-everything change) — `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 135→165, **166/166** including Z.99.

**Deliberately NOT touched this session, and why**: `multichoice` beyond `MULTI_YESNO` (204 corpus uses, overwhelmingly battle-facility menus belonging to M35 — inventing a picker would be guessing at contents); `fadescreen`'s own pairing gap (128 uses, needs a real screen-transition redesign per its own doc comment, not a matching opcode); `getplayerxy` (25 uses — the VM has no player-position awareness at all, and the project's own "the VM cannot reach the scene tree, so it records and the caller applies" pattern would need new pending-state plumbing this session judged not worth building for 25 uses); `setobjectxy`/`copybyte`/`createvobject`/`turnvobject`/`setberrytree`/`register_matchcall`/`pokemart`/braille family/`setweather`/`dowildbattle` and the rest of the corpus tail — all lower-usage, cross-milestone (shops/M27I, weather/M27N, berry trees/never-fully-built, Match Call/M35), or needing real new subsystem design rather than an opcode addition. No rerunnable corpus-coverage tool was built either — the last recorded overall percentage (27.5%, Stage 2 era) stays stale; only the region-wide movement-script floor (`m27f_script_vm_test`'s own J.22/23, 1270/1372) is still tracked live.

Regression: `m27f_script_vm_test` **166/166**, `m27f_stage4_test` **63/63**, `m27i_wallet_test` **34/34**, plus all 18 other overworld suites reconfirmed clean via `run_overworld_tests.sh` (`m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `m27e_field_moves_test`/`m27e_run_test`/`m27e_surf_test`/`m27h_wild_encounters_test`/`m27i_bag_test`/`m27i_i4_bag_screen_test`/`m27i_i5_party_test`/`m27i_item_identity_test`/`m27i_text_buffers_test`/`m27k_kc_nickname_test`/`m27k_newgame_test`/`m27k_starter_test`/`m27l_save_test`/`m27o_field_poison_test`/`m27o_respawn_test`/`m27o_whiteout_test` all green, no engine ERROR lines), plus a full battle-side sweep (213 files, GRAND TOTAL 31792, 0 failures — unaffected by construction, since nothing outside `scripts/overworld/` was touched). ⚠️ **`m27h_catching_test.tscn` still hangs past its own timeout, confirmed the SAME pre-existing, unrelated issue already flagged in the `[Map scripts + coord_event trigger dispatch]` entry above** — not re-investigated here, per that entry's own disposition.

**[M27G recon] 2026-08-06. Scope of record is `docs/m27g_recon.md` — this is a summary, not a replacement. 594 region-wide functions correct to 51 reachable, then to ~20-25 real ones.**

Every prior citation of this milestone's size ("1,390 `special` + 719 `specialvar` = 2,109 uses across 569 distinct functions") measured the WHOLE region-wide compiled corpus — Kanto and Hoenn mixed indiscriminately, the same blind spot `[M27F]`'s own opcode-frequency scan already corrected once for plain opcodes, now applied to a much larger nominal surface where it matters far more. A reachability BFS from the 32 actually-baked corridor maps' own real entry points (193 placed-entity `script_label`s + each map's own `OnTransition`/`OnFrame`/`OnWarp`/`OnLoad`/`OnResume` table, walked through `call`/`goto`/every conditional's jump target) finds only **51 distinct functions, 99 total uses, reachable from content that exists in this project today.** `callnative` never fires from the corridor at all.

**A second cut removed multiplayer-gated call sites even where graph-reachable** — traced by CALLER, not just presence: `CloseLink` (13 uses) is exclusively `CableClub_EventScript_Abort*` handlers; `SavePlayerParty`/`LoadPlayerBag` only fire from the Battle Colosseum's own Cable Club entry; `HasEnoughMonsForDoubleBattle`/`HasAtLeastOneBerry` only from Cable-Club double-battle-mode/Wireless-Berry-Crush selectors. All already covered by this project's own standing multiplayer/Union-Room/Easy-Chat exclusion. **Real actionable scope: ~20-25 distinct functions, ~30-40 uses** — the number worth building against, not 594 and not even 51.

**Headline finding: the Running Shoes NPC (`PewterCity_EventScript_AideGiveRunningShoes`) is real, in-corridor content whose own script tail is `setflag FLAG_SYS_B_DASH`** — the first genuine in-game trigger for Run, which `[M27E E2]`'s own status note already flagged as granted ONLY by the debug boot ("no in-game event grants `FLAG_SYS_B_DASH` yet"). The only thing blocking this script is `DisableMsgBoxWalkaway` plus two VM opcodes this recon found along the way (`signmsg`/`normalmsg`) — all three confirmed, by direct source citation, to be LITERAL NO-OPS already in the reference itself (`script_cmd_table.inc`'s own dispatch sends both opcodes to `ScrCmd_nop1`; `DisableMsgBoxWalkaway`/`SetWalkingIntoSignVars`'s own C bodies are single commented-out lines in source, `script.c:685-692`) — not a simplification, a faithful port of an already-inert function.

**Tier 0 (trivial, source-confirmed no-op or already-built bridge, no open design question)**: the 3 opcodes/specials above, `HealPlayerParty`/`ChangePokemonNickname` (already fully implemented — the latter dispatched specially inside `ScriptVM.step()` itself, not through `FieldSpecials`, so its absence from that registry isn't a gap), `OpenMuseumFossilPic`/`CloseMuseumFossilPic` (matches the already-established `showmonpic`/`hidemonpic` field-picture-layer no-op precedent, reachable from Pewter Museum's real Kabutops/Aerodactyl fossil NPCs), `SaveGame` (a bridge to the already-built M27L save system), `CountPartyNonEggMons`/`CalculatePlayerPartyCount`/`ScriptGetPartyMonSpecies` (cheap reads off `OverworldSession.player_party()`, the same shape `getpartysize` already established).

**Tier 1 (real single-player Kanto content, genuine but bounded new work)**: `ChoosePartyMon` (a bridge to the already-built `[M27I I5]` party screen in browse mode — real corridor callers are the in-game-trade NPC and Daisy's grooming); in-game trade (`CreateInGameTradePokemon`/`DoInGameTradeScene`/`GetTradeSpecies`/`GetInGameTradeSpeciesInfo`, real single-player party-mutation logic in `trade.c`, genuinely distinct from every excluded Link-Trade item, not yet investigated past confirming its call sites — needs its own Step 0); `DaisyMassageServices` (the flow is buildable now, its real friendship-boost EFFECT is blocked on this project having no friendship system at all — a real, newly-surfaced, currently unowned gap). ⚠️ **`StartOldManTutorialBattle` looked like a quick win given `[M27H]`'s catching system already exists and turned out NOT to be** — real source has the Old Man take over the PLAYER'S OWN INPUT and play a demo battle against a wild Weedle using the player's own items, not a scripted cutscene; needs its own scoping pass (most likely a disclosed simplification skipping the interactive demo, matching this project's precedent elsewhere for GBA-spectacle sequences) and confirmation `ITEM_TEACHY_TV` exists in the roster.

**Tier 2 (real functions, already-scoped elsewhere)**: the Pokédex family → M33; `ChooseMonForMoveTutor` → M30; `DoPCTurnOnEffect`/`BedroomPC` → M27I I5-5 (deferred); `ShouldTryRematchBattle` (311 uses region-wide, the single highest-usage function found in the ENTIRE scan, 0 reachable from this corridor) → M35; `IsBadEggInParty` → M31; `GetLeadMonFriendship` → **no milestone owns a friendship system yet**, flagged not assigned.

**Tier 3 (permanently excluded, confirmed by real call-site tracing, not just presence)**: 19 functions, all Cable Club/Union Room/Easy Chat/Battle Frontier — full list in the recon doc.

**Deliberately not attempted**: scoping the remaining ~540 region-wide functions this corridor can't reach — dominated by Contest Hall/Secret Bases/the 7 Battle Frontier facilities/Trainer Hill/Trainer Tower/Pokéblocks/the Devon Corporation plotline, content that's either already excluded or unjudgeable before its own maps are ever baked. **Recommendation recorded in the doc: re-run this exact reachability walk every time a new batch of maps is baked, rather than pre-scoping the whole region now** — the same "vertical slice, not full-region import" discipline `[M27B]` already established for the map-baking side of this exact problem.

**Proposed build order**: G1 (the Tier 0 batch — unblocks Running Shoes) → G2 (`ChoosePartyMon`, unblocks Daisy's flow + is a G3 prerequisite) → G3 (in-game trade, own Step 0 first) → G4 (Old Man tutorial, own scoping session first) → re-run the reachability walk on the next corridor expansion rather than scoping the tail now.

**[M27G G1] 2026-08-06. The real Tier 0 batch, re-verified against actual corridor callers before building anything — 3 of the doc's own original 6 items turned out to be excluded, and 1 was already done.**

Re-checked every G1 candidate's own real corridor caller before implementing (the same discipline the recon doc already applied to `CloseLink`/etc.) and found the doc's own first draft was too generous: **`SaveGame`'s only 5 corridor callers are ALL Cable-Club-gated** (`CableClub_EventScript_SaveAndChooseLinkLeader`/`_EnterUnionRoom`/`_RecordCorner`/`_TryEnterColosseum`/`_TradeCenter`) — being a "shared" (not Cable-Club-prefixed) script name was not evidence of a real non-multiplayer caller, it just doesn't have one in this corridor. Same finding for **`CalculatePlayerPartyCount`/`CountPartyNonEggMons`** (`CableClub_EventScript_CheckPartyTradeRequirements`/`_CheckPartyUnionRoomRequirements` only). All three dropped from G1, left for whenever the region grows past the corridor. Separately: **`DrawWholeMapView` and `IsPlayerNotInTrainerTowerLobby`/`BufferUnionRoomPlayerName` were already implemented** before this session started (`FieldSpecials.NOOP_SPECIALS`/`SPECIALVAR_VALUES` already covered all three) — the recon doc's own Tier 2/3 placement of these was stale, corrected in place rather than silently reclassified.

**What actually shipped**: `signmsg`/`normalmsg` — two new VM opcodes, not specials (found while tracing the Running Shoes script itself) — dispatch to `ScrCmd_nop1` in source's own `script_cmd_table.inc:227-228`, a literal port of an already-inert function, not a simplification. `DisableMsgBoxWalkaway`/`SetWalkingIntoSignVars` added to `FieldSpecials.NOOP_SPECIALS` — same reasoning, both real C bodies (`script.c:685-692`) are a single commented-out line each in source itself. `OpenMuseumFossilPic`/`CloseMuseumFossilPic` added alongside them, matching the already-established `showmonpic`/`hidemonpic` no-field-picture-layer precedent (`[M27K K-a]`), reachable from Pewter Museum's real Kabutops/Aerodactyl fossil NPCs.

**`ScriptGetPartyMonSpecies`/`BufferMonNickname` needed real party context, so — matching the existing `ChangePokemonNickname` precedent — both are dispatched directly inside `ScriptVM.step()` rather than through `FieldSpecials`, which is deliberately stateless by its own design.** `ScriptGetPartyMonSpecies` (source: `field_specials.c:1641-1644`, `GetMonData(&gParties[B_TRAINER_PLAYER][gSpecialVar_0x8004], MON_DATA_SPECIES_OR_EGG, NULL)`) is party-ONLY with no PC fallback in source itself, unlike the one below — new `_party_mon_species(slot)`, degrading to 0 (no species) on an out-of-range slot, the same "unknown constant" shape `_literal` already uses. `BufferMonNickname` (source: `tv.c:3273-3278`, via `GetSelectedBoxMonFromPcOrParty`) DOES support a PC path in source — guarded with the exact same `PC_MON_CHOSEN` halt `_begin_nickname()`'s own PC branch already established, rather than silently assuming party-only. Both found via `PalletTown_RivalsHouse_EventScript_GroomMon` (Daisy's grooming) — real, reachable, non-excluded content; confirmed via its own full script that `BufferMonNickname` is needed at its tail regardless of `DaisyMassageServices`'s own still-blocked friendship effect, so it was folded into G1 rather than left for later.

**[M27G G2] 2026-08-06. `ChoosePartyMon`, bridging to the already-built `FieldPartyScreen` in browse mode — and a real, general `PARTY_SIZE` gap found only by driving the real corpus.**

`special ChoosePartyMon` is a new pause kind, `Pause.WAIT_PARTY_CHOICE`, matching `WAIT_BATTLE`/`WAIT_NAMING`'s own shape exactly: it carries a RESULT (the chosen slot), so a plain `resume()` deliberately cannot clear it — `resume()`'s own guard extended, `is_waiting()` extended — and only a new `answer_party_choice(index: int)` can. `index` is whatever the real screen's own `confirm()`/`close()` already report (a 0-5 slot, or -1 on cancel); both -1 AND an out-of-range index write `PARTY_NOTHING_CHOSEN` (0xFF, `include/constants/party_menu.h:5`) into `VAR_0x8004`, matching source's own `>= PARTY_SIZE` check (`BufferMonSelection`, `party_menu.c:8046-8052`) rather than trusting the screen's own -1 convention is the only "nothing chosen" shape a caller could hand in. Dispatched inside `ScriptVM.step()` itself (not `FieldSpecials`, which stays deliberately stateless), the same precedent `ChangePokemonNickname`/`BufferMonNickname` already set.

`overworld.gd` gained a new `_vm_party_choice_pending: bool`, checked FIRST in both `_on_party_mon_chosen`/`_on_party_cancelled` — a third disambiguation alongside `_pending_use_item`'s own two states (item-use target-picker vs. a plain browse) — routing to `answer_party_choice(index)`/`answer_party_choice(-1)` respectively before either handler falls through to its existing item-use logic. The driver's own `match _vm.pause_reason:` block opens `_party_screen.open(_vm.party)` in plain browse mode (no item name), guarded on `_vm_party_choice_pending` rather than `_party_screen.is_open` so a re-entrant frame — the screen takes a frame to actually open — cannot double-open it.

⚠️ **DRIVING THE REAL CORPUS FOUND A GENERAL, NOT HOENN-ONLY, GAP: `PARTY_SIZE` WAS UNRESOLVED.** `PalletTown_RivalsHouse_EventScript_GroomMon`'s own very next opcode after `ChoosePartyMon` is `goto_if_ge VAR_0x8004, PARTY_SIZE, DeclineGrooming` — the real "was anything chosen" check — and `_literal` had no case for `PARTY_SIZE` at all, falling through to its generic unknown-constant 0. Since `VAR_0x8004` is always `>= 0`, this made the check unconditionally true: **every one of the 57 region-wide corpus uses of `PARTY_SIZE`** (nearly all in exactly this `goto_if_ge VAR_0x8004, PARTY_SIZE, Decline*` shape — trade requests, minigame entry, grooming) always took the Decline/abort branch regardless of what was actually picked. Fixed with one new `_literal` case, `"PARTY_SIZE": return BattleParty.PARTY_SIZE` (`include/constants/global.h:82` = 6) — found only because G2's own test drove the real compiled script rather than a synthetic fixture, the same discipline that caught G1's `signmsg`/`normalmsg` gap.

**Daisy's grooming script now runs past `ChoosePartyMon` all the way to its own real remaining blocker.** Live-driven (`data/map_scripts.json`/`data/map_texts.json`): the offer's own yes/no → `WAIT_PARTY_CHOICE` → answering with a real slot → `ScriptGetPartyMonSpecies` genuinely reads that slot's real species (proving the `PARTY_SIZE` fix, not just asserting it — a still-broken build would read `VAR_RESULT == 0`, never having reached the `specialvar` at all) → the no-op `fadescreen`/`playfanfare`/`waitfanfare` chain → halts cleanly at `special DaisyMassageServices`, which needs a friendship system this project doesn't have (unowned, flagged in the recon doc's own Tier 2) — not a halt anywhere earlier than that.

New assertions added directly to `m27f_script_vm_test.gd` as Section O (11 assertions): the VM contract in isolation (`WAIT_PARTY_CHOICE` set, `is_waiting()`, a plain `resume()` NOT clearing it, a valid choice writing the real slot and letting the script continue to `DONE`, a cancel and an out-of-range index both degrading to `PARTY_NOTHING_CHOSEN`, and the pause being a genuine no-op once already resolved); then the real corpus test driving Daisy's script end to end, including the `PARTY_SIZE` discriminator. `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 174→185, **186/186 including Z.99**, all green after two real test-authoring fixes (an `_run`-vs-`_drive` mixup that can't auto-resume a `message` wait; the script's own cooldown-counter gate needing `VAR_MASSAGE_COOLDOWN_STEP_COUNTER` primed above 500 or it takes the unimplemented `GetLeadMonFriendship` branch first) — both test bugs, not production bugs.

**[M27G G3 recon] 2026-08-06. Report-only Step 0 into `trade.c`, per its own build-order note — full detail in `docs/m27g_recon.md`'s new "G3 Step 0" section. Small and bounded, not the open-ended item the old note implied.**

The four functions (`GetTradeSpecies`/`GetInGameTradeSpeciesInfo`/`CreateInGameTradePokemon`/`DoInGameTradeScene`, all `trade.c`) sit behind four shared `EventScript_*` helper scripts (`data/event_scripts.s:1531-1551`) riding entirely on already-shipped opcode infrastructure (`special`/`specialvar`/`goto_if_*` — no new opcode class, the same shape G1/G2's own additions were). ⚠️ **`DoInGameTradeScene` is NOT a lightweight fade-and-swap — it invokes the full link-trade cinematic engine** (`CB2_InitInGameTrade`/`CB2_InGameTrade`, ball-bounce + GBA screen zoom + cable sprites), the identical spectacle a real link trade runs, just with `isLinkTrade = FALSE`; confirmed via direct trace to be genuinely un-multiplayer-gated on this path (never touches `HandleLinkDataSend`/`SendBlock`), so the standing Cable-Club exclusion does not apply.

**One real trade NPC is already reachable today**: `Route2_House_EventScript_Reyley` (Mr. Mime ↔ Abra) is fully compiled into `data/map_scripts.json`/`map_texts.json` on `Route2_House_Frlg` — an already-baked map — all 4 branches (decline/wrong-species/already-traded/success) present and waiting only on the 4 specials. The other 7 real Kanto trade NPCs (Route 11, Underground Path, Cerulean, Vermilion, Cinnabar Lab ×2, Route 18) sit on unbaked maps — a map-baking question, not a G3 one; they fall out for free once baked, since the specials are generic across the table.

**Reuse confirmed for almost everything**: `ChoosePartyMon` (G2) is the exact selection screen needed; `PokemonFactory.create_battle_pokemon`'s existing `forced_ivs`/`ability_slot` params (built ahead of need, per that function's own doc comment) cover the incoming mon's construction; `nickname`/`held_item` are already real settable `BattlePokemon` fields; the M26B3 ball recall/send-out animation primitives are a real precedent for a future visual beat. **Genuinely new, but small**: an 8-entry trades data table (0 of 13 region-wide entries are FRLG-relevant beyond 8 — the first 4 are RSE-only), the 4 specials themselves dispatched inside `ScriptVM.step()` (matching the `ChoosePartyMon`/`BufferMonNickname` precedent, since `FieldSpecials` stays stateless), and — ⚠️ **a SECOND `_literal` symbolic-constant gap, the identical shape as G2's own `PARTY_SIZE` find**: `INGAME_TRADE_MR_MIME` etc. (`include/constants/trade.h:12-25`) has no case in `_literal` and would silently resolve to 0, sending every trade NPC's own `setvar VAR_0x8008, INGAME_TRADE_*` to the wrong table row — flagged now so it's fixed alongside implementation rather than found later via a silent wrong-branch bug. The one real new mechanism (not just a new dispatch case) is the party swap itself — remove the offered mon, insert the constructed traded-in mon at the same slot — since nothing in `BattleParty`/`OverworldSession` currently does that specific splice.

**Proposed split, recorded for whenever this is picked up**: **G3a** (data table + the `_literal` fix + `GetTradeSpecies`/`GetInGameTradeSpeciesInfo`/`CreateInGameTradePokemon`, `DoInGameTradeScene` reduced to a no-op/plain message for now) makes Reyley's trade fully playable on its own; **G3b** (optional, purely additive) swaps that no-op for a real ball-animation beat reusing M26B3's existing primitives rather than porting the reference's own link-trade cinematic faithfully — a disclosed simplification matching this project's established precedent for GBA-spectacle sequences elsewhere. No G3c needed. Report-only — no code, no tests, no `.tscn` changes this session.

**[M27G G3a] 2026-08-06. Reyley's trade (Mr. Mime ↔ Abra) is fully playable end to end — and driving the real corpus found a SECOND general bug, unrelated to trades, deeper than either symbolic-constant gap.**

New `scripts/gen_ingame_trades.py` → `data/ingame_trades.json`, an ARRAY (not a name-keyed dict) matching source's own `sIngameTrades[idx]` indexing — parsed directly from `src/data/trade.h:969-1215`'s raw C struct array via a brace-depth-tracked parser (needed since `.ivs = {a,b,c,d,e,f}` also uses braces, so a naive "next closing brace" scan stops there instead). **13 entries, not the recon's own "8"** — re-derived precisely from `enum InGameTradeID` (`include/constants/trade.h:8-24`): 4 RSE-only (SEEDOT/PLUSLE/HORSEA/MEOWTH, kept anyway at zero cost, matching this project's "full extraction over selective" precedent) + **9** FRLG-reachable (MR_MIME through SEEL), not 8 — the recon's own NPC-row count conflated ROWS with NPCS; Cinnabar Lab's Lounge hosts two separate trades (Electrode AND Tangela) on one map, so 8 NPCs really do cover 9 table rows. Species/item names pre-resolved to this project's own numeric IDs at generation time (reusing the already-built `species_name_to_id.json`/`item_name_to_id.json` maps), so the runtime table is pure data with no VM-side name resolution needed. Fields deliberately omitted (OT identity/mail/contest stats/sheen) — checked directly against every real corridor trade script's own text, none ever buffer or reference them.

⚠️ **THE TWO VERSION-GATED ENTRIES (`#if defined(FIRERED) ... #else ... #endif`) TAKE THE `#else` (LEAFGREEN) BRANCH, THE SAME CHOICE `[M27K K-b]`'s NAME-LIST ALREADY MADE** — and this corrected a real mistake in the recon's own informal summary table, which listed "Lickitung ↔ Golduck" (the FIRERED branch); the correct value for this project is **Slowbro** (dex 80, confirmed in the generated output), since Leaf is already the established player character. A second parser bug was caught before generating any real data: `resolve_version_branch`'s first draft assumed the `#if/#else/#endif` always wraps the WHOLE entry body (true for NIDORAN/NIDORINOA) — Lickitung's own gate wraps only its trailing `.requestedSpecies` field, so the naive version discarded the rest of the entry (nickname/IVs/etc.); fixed by substituting the `#else` branch back into the body in place rather than returning only the matched span.

**Dispatch**: `GetTradeSpecies` is aliased directly onto G1's `_party_mon_species` (confirmed behaviorally identical — source's own egg check is unconditionally false, since this project models no eggs). `GetInGameTradeSpeciesInfo` is new — indexes the table by `VAR_0x8005`, buffers the REQUESTED species' name into slot 0 and the GIVEN-AWAY species' name into slot 1 (source's own argument order), returns the requested dex. `CreateInGameTradePokemon` is new — builds the incoming Pokémon via `PokemonFactory.create_battle_pokemon`'s existing `forced_ivs`/`ability_slot` params (built ahead of need for M24, confirmed reused for real here) at the OFFERED mon's own level, then does a **direct in-place replace** (`party.members[slot] = incoming`), matching source's own `SWAP(*playerMon, *partnerMon, sTradeAnim->tempMon)` (`TradeMons`, `:3100`) — simpler than the recon's own "remove-then-append" framing, since source never actually removes a slot at all. Friendship resets to a flat 70 (`TRADE_FRIENDSHIP`, a real source CONSTANT, not per-row data). Held item resolves via a new `_resolve_trade_held_item` that checks `ResourceLoader.exists` first, degrading to no item held (not a noisy warning) for every real-but-unimplemented item this project has no `.tres` for — Fab Mail (Jynx)/Tiny Mushroom (Nidoran)/Stardust (Tangela) — while Farfetch'd's own item resolves to a genuinely FUNCTIONAL held item: **`ITEM_STICK` is confirmed a real alias for `ITEM_LEEK`** (id 393, already implemented since `[M18g]`'s crit-stage item work), so that one trade ships with a real mechanical held item for free. `DoInGameTradeScene` added to `FieldSpecials.NOOP_SPECIALS`, matching the `fadescreen` no-op precedent — the real work is already done by `CreateInGameTradePokemon`.

⚠️ **THE `_literal` FIX WAS EXACTLY AS SCOPED — `_INGAME_TRADE_IDS`, a new dict (kept separate from `_SYMBOLIC_CONSTANTS`, since these are general row indices, not Hoenn-only price values), all 13 constants. But driving the real corpus found a SECOND, deeper, genuinely PRE-EXISTING bug, unrelated to either symbolic-constant gap: `_conditional`'s own 3-arg form (`goto_if_eq`/`_ne`/`_lt`/etc.) resolved its SECOND operand through `_literal` directly — constants only — never checking whether it was itself a variable.** Reyley's own script does `goto_if_ne VAR_RESULT, VAR_0x8009, NotRequestedMon` — a genuine variable-vs-variable comparison, source's own real `VarGet`-uniform semantics — and `_literal("VAR_0x8009")` has no case for it, falling through to 0. `VAR_RESULT` (63, a real matching species) `!= 0`, so the check took the WRONG branch on every CORRECT trade. Confirmed general, not trade-specific, via a full corpus scan: **17 region-wide 3-arg conditionals compare two variables**, of which **7 are this exact in-game-trade shape** (Dontae/Garett/Clifton/Norma and 3 more, on top of Reyley) — meaning this fix alone was necessary for 7 of the 8 real trade NPCs' own success path to ever be reachable, not just Reyley's. Fixed by resolving `args[1]` via `_resolve_number` (the existing var-then-literal duality) instead of `_literal` directly; `setvar`'s own value argument is deliberately UNTOUCHED (a `setvar` target in source is always a literal — `copyvar` is the var-to-var op), so the fix is scoped to conditionals only.

New assertions added directly to `m27f_script_vm_test.gd` as Section P (32 assertions): the `_literal` fix for `INGAME_TRADE_*` (general, spot-checked at both ends of the range plus the RSE-only first entry); the data table directly (a real row, an out-of-range degrade); **the `_conditional` fix in isolation** (var-vs-var equal doesn't branch, var-vs-var different DOES branch — the discriminator proving it isn't just "always fall through" — and a regression guard confirming the overwhelmingly-common var-vs-literal shape is completely unaffected); `GetTradeSpecies`/`GetInGameTradeSpeciesInfo` via real opcode dispatch (never a private function call, matching every earlier section's own discipline), including the argument-order discriminator (slot 0 buffers the REQUESTED name, slot 1 the GIVEN-AWAY name) and the out-of-range degrade; `CreateInGameTradePokemon`'s full construction (species/level-from-offered-mon/nickname/forced-IVs/friendship-reset-to-70, with an untouched-other-slot discriminator), plus the held-item resolve/degrade pair (Leek resolves, Fab Mail degrades to null); `DoInGameTradeScene`'s no-op; and — the strongest tests — **all four of the real compiled `Route2_House_EventScript_Reyley` script's own branches driven end to end**: success (party genuinely mutated, flag genuinely set), wrong species offered (NEITHER slot mutated, flag not set), decline (never even reaches `ChoosePartyMon`), and already-traded (never pauses on anything at all). `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 185→217, **218/218 including Z.99**, all green after the one real production bug (the `_conditional` fix) found and fixed via this session's own first test run.

Regression: `m27f_script_vm_test` **218/218**, plus all 19 other non-hanging overworld suites reconfirmed clean via `run_overworld_tests.sh` (`m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514 down through `m27o_whiteout_test` 33/33, no engine ERROR lines) — a full sweep specifically because the `_conditional` fix touches a core, widely-shared dispatch function, not scoped narrowly like a pure-addition session would be. `m27h_catching_test.tscn` skipped, the same already-documented pre-existing hang. Full battle-side sweep not run — nothing outside `scripts/overworld/`/`scenes/overworld/` was touched, and full sweeps are Rob's own manual step per this file's standing convention.

**G3a is done. G3b (the real visual beat, deferred per Rob's own explicit call) and the other 7 trade NPCs (blocked on map-baking, not on this milestone) remain open**, both already recorded on this file's own M27 roadmap row.

**[Field-script authoring fork — `field_script_source/`] 2026-08-06. Prompted directly by Rob wanting to hand-edit dialogue/scripts; the same architectural move M27M already made for map tiles ("the scene becomes the source of truth"), applied here to field script logic and dialogue text. Full working guide: `docs/field_script_authoring.md`.**

**Why this was needed at all**: `data/map_scripts.json`/`data/map_texts.json` are read LIVE at runtime (`overworld.gd:1919`, no bake step, confirmed via direct citation before assuming) but are FULLY GENERATED — `scripts/gen_map_scripts.py`/`scripts/gen_map_texts.py` re-derive them wholesale from `reference/pokeemerald_expansion` every run, so any hand edit to the JSON directly would be silently discarded the next time either generator ran. There was no durable path to hand-author either script logic or dialogue before this.

**What shipped**: `field_script_source/` (project root, newly TRACKED — not gitignored, unlike `reference/pokeemerald_expansion` itself, since this fork is genuinely authored content going forward, not regenerable build output the way `assets/maps/` is) — a byte-for-byte copy of every one of the reference's own **1025 `.inc`/`.s` files** (~7.4 MB), in the identical relative layout under `field_script_source/data/`, so `gen_map_scripts.py`'s own `iter_inc_files()` (`os.walk(os.path.join(REF, "data"))`) needed zero logic changes — only the `REF` constant itself, now `os.path.join(PROJECT, "field_script_source")` in both generators rather than the reference checkout. **One fork covers both scripts AND dialogue for free**: `.string` text blocks live inside the same `.inc` files the opcodes come from (confirmed directly — `PalletTown_ProfessorOaksLab_Text_ThoseArePokeBalls::` sits at line 1535 of that map's own `scripts.inc`, well below its script labels), so there was never a second tree to fork.

⚠️ **VERIFIED BYTE-FOR-BYTE IDENTICAL OUTPUT BEFORE ANY HAND EDIT WAS MADE** — both generators were run against the fresh fork and diffed against the previously-committed `data/map_scripts.json`/`data/map_texts.json`: **zero differing lines, `git status` reported no change to either tracked file.** The fork started as a proven-clean baseline, not an unverified copy — the same discipline M27B's own "re-verified with the current code" precedent already established for a bake-pipeline change.

A `.gdignore` marker was added at `field_script_source/`'s own root proactively — `reference/pokeemerald_expansion` itself had already paid the cost once for skipping this (12,651 `.import`-metadata files generated by Godot scanning a large non-Godot tree, per this file's own "Do not use" table above), so this new 1025-file tree got the same precaution from the start rather than discovering the gap the hard way again. Confirmed via a real `--import` pass: zero `.import`/`.uid` files generated anywhere under the new tree.

Both generator scripts' own doc comments now carry an explicit, load-bearing warning: **never repoint `REF` back at `reference/pokeemerald_expansion`** — doing so would silently discard every hand edit made to the fork on the very next regeneration. This file's own "Ground truth / reference" section gained a matching exception note, since that section is specifically "which checkout to use for what" and this is now a real, permanent carve-out from its own default rule.

Regression: `m27f_script_vm_test` **218/218** (unaffected — confirms the `REF` redirect changed nothing observable), plus all 19 other non-hanging overworld suites reconfirmed clean via `run_overworld_tests.sh` — a full sweep, matching the same discipline the immediately-preceding `_conditional` fix used, given this touches the two generators every baked map's scripts and dialogue ultimately trace back through. `m27h_catching_test.tscn` skipped, the same already-documented pre-existing hang. Nothing committed to git, per standing instruction — `field_script_source/` exists on disk, untracked-but-trackable, ready whenever Rob chooses to commit it.

## M27I — Field UI & menus *(scoped 2026-07-31; I1 built)*

**Step 0 first, reported before building.** `M27I` as a block is start menu / Bag / Party / Summary / PC / options / region map. The immediate need is narrower: **Brock's win path runs its whole reward chain and stops at `checkitemspace`**, the only wall the corridor has actually hit.

⚠️ **`giveitem` IS NOT A PRIMITIVE.** It is a macro over `setorcopyvar` x2 + `callstd STD_OBTAIN_ITEM`, and that standard script does real work: `additem` -> buffer the pluralised name -> `checkitemtype` -> buffer the POCKET name -> branch on success -> fanfare + "obtained", or "no room". This project's compiler kept it unexpanded as an opcode, so implementing it means reproducing that script's DECISION STRUCTURE natively — the precedent Stage 2 set for `trainerbattle_single`.

⚠️ **ARGUMENTS ARE OFTEN VARIABLES, NOT ITEM CONSTANTS.** Every arg goes through `VarGet` — `additem VAR_0x8009`, `checkitemspace VAR_TEMP_0`, **64 region-wide**. An implementation assuming `ITEM_*` literals breaks on exactly the scripted give-and-branch chains this exists to serve.

**Measured scope** — region-wide 729 item-opcode uses / 267 distinct items; **corridor 137 uses / 69 items**. Shops are nearly absent from the corridor (`pokemart` 2, money 3+3), so they are genuinely deferrable.

**Phases**: **I1** item identity · **I2** text buffers + `{STR_VAR_n}` substitution · **I3** the bag + the opcodes (**unblocks Brock**) · **I3b** wallet + money opcodes (unblocks M27O's O3) · **I4** the bag screen · **I5** party/Summary/PC. **I2 is a real phase, not a rider on I3**: the message box has ZERO substitution today, and `STD_OBTAIN_ITEM` itself buffers two strings, so a `giveitem` without it prints raw placeholders.

**[M27I I1 — item identity] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31.**

New `scripts/gen_item_name_map.py` -> `data/item_name_to_id.json`: **976 constants over 875 distinct ids, so 101 are alias spellings.** Mirrors `gen_move_name_map.py`, which solved the identical problem for moves. Idempotent (byte-identical on re-run).

⚠️ **THE ALIASES ARE LOAD-BEARING AND THE CORRIDOR USES BOTH SIDES.** `ITEM_ITEMFINDER = ITEM_DOWSING_MACHINE`, `ITEM_OAKS_PARCEL = ITEM_PARCEL` — same class as `[M27B Step 4]`'s `MOVE_FAINT_ATTACK = MOVE_FEINT_ATTACK`. Break-tested: parsing only `= <int>` leaves **43 corpus items unresolvable**.

**TMs and HMs are a preprocessor zip, not constants** (`ENUM_TM(n, id)` over `FOREACH_TM`), so rather than emulate the preprocessor the generator reads the ordered move list and pairs entry n with `ITEM_TM<n>` — source documents the result itself (`ITEM_TM_FOCUS_PUNCH = ITEM_TM01`).

⚠️ **A REAL DATA GAP FOUND BY I1'S OWN GUARD: `items.json` CONTAINS NO TM OR HM AT ALL.** Genuine holes at ids 582-631 and 682-689 — **all 58, TM39 (Brock's own reward) included**. Not corruption: the M15 pipeline routed TM/HM identity into `tmhm_map.json`, keyed by TM NUMBER rather than item id, and the two files never met. `get_item_identity()` bridges them (`tm_number = id - ITEM_TM01 + 1`; an HM's index is 50 + its number). Break-tested: without the bridge **85 corpus items resolve to an id and then have no record** — the failure moves one step later rather than away.

**Lookup lives on `PokemonRegistry`**, which already owned both files, rather than a second loader over the same JSON. New `item_id_of()` / `get_item_identity()` / `item_constants()`. `ItemManager` gains the three missing pocket ordinals (`POKE_BALLS`/`TM_HM`/`KEY_ITEMS`) — battle only ever needed to tell a berry from a non-berry; a bag needs all five — plus `pocket_from_name()`, since `items.json` stores a pocket as a string and every consumer wants the ordinal. The two pre-existing constants were already correct against source.

**The headline guard is E.02/E.03**: **every one of the 267 item constants the corpus names resolves to an id AND to a real identity record.** An unresolvable item is a script that cannot run, found at play time rather than here.

**Tests**: new `m27i_item_identity_test` **31/31**. Regression: `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `item_registry_test` 309/309, `m18_patch1_test` 21/21.

**Not built by I1, deliberately**: no bag, no opcodes, no behaviour — identity only.

**[M27I I2 — text buffers and placeholder expansion] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31.**

Source keeps exactly three script string buffers (`sScriptStringVars[] = { gStringVar1, gStringVar2, gStringVar3 }`, `scrcmd.c:102`) and every `buffer*` command writes one. New `scripts/overworld/text_buffers.gd` holds them; the VM gains `bufferitemname`/`bufferitemnameplural`/`bufferstdstring`/`buffernumberstring`/`bufferspeciesname`.

⚠️ **EXPANSION HAPPENS AT PRINT TIME, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE PROPERTY.** Source expands in the print path (`StringExpandPlaceholders`), not the load path. A script buffers, prints, re-buffers and prints again — the second print must show the second value. Expanding when the `message` opcode RUNS would freeze the first, and it is invisible until a script reuses a slot, which most do: **`STR_VAR_1` is written 176 times across the corpus and read 1369.** So `overworld.gd` expands at `_box.open`, not in the VM. Break-tested: handing the box raw pages fails F.02/F.03/F.04.

⚠️ **UNKNOWN MARKERS EXPAND TO EMPTY, WHICH IS SOURCE'S RULE, NOT A CONVENIENCE.** `GetExpandedPlaceholder` returns `gText_ExpandedPlaceholder_Empty` for any id past its table. Break-tested: letting unknowns survive leaks **32 distinct marker kinds** into player-facing text — `{POKEBLOCK}`, `{FONT_NORMAL}`, `{PLAY_BGM ...}`.

⚠️ **`{KUN}` IS GENUINELY EMPTY IN ENGLISH** — `gText_ExpandedPlaceholder_Kun` and `_Chan` are both `_("")` (`strings.c:8-9`). It is a Japanese honorific slot the English build deliberately blanks, not a missing string. **400 corpus uses**, so reading it as a gap would put a stray word in 400 places.

**Placeholders are already in brace form** (`{STR_VAR_1}`, `{PLAYER}`) because M27F Stage 1's extractor resolved source's two-byte `PLACEHOLDER_BEGIN` encoding at extraction time — so this is string substitution, not a byte-stream walk.

⚠️ **BOTH SLOT SPELLINGS ARE REAL**: scripts mostly write `STR_VAR_1`, but the corpus also carries a bare `0`. Handling only the named form drops those silently. Same shape as I1's alias finding, and the same shape as the item args themselves — **`_resolve_item`/`_resolve_number` run every argument through the variable store**, because 64 corpus args are variables rather than constants.

**`{PLAYER}` is 1193 uses and there is no player-identity system** — M27K owns naming. `"LEAF"` matches the overworld sprite AND the battle side's own `_PLAYER_BACK_PIC`, so all three halves agree until then rather than each inventing an answer.

New `scripts/gen_std_strings.py` -> `data/std_strings.json`: all **39** of `gStdStrings[]`, generated rather than hand-typed (two entry forms — inline `COMPOUND_STRING` and `gText_*` references resolved out of `strings.c`) for the same reason `metatile_behavior.gd`'s constants are.

**Disclosed simplifications**: the plural rule is suffix-only (source uses a real per-item plural name when one exists; this project has no plural column — 8 corpus uses, 0 in the corridor), `bufferspeciesname` resolves a dex number but not a `SPECIES_*` constant (no species name map yet; 6 corridor uses, none gating anything), and `bufferpartymonnick`/`bufferdecorationname` are unbuilt.

**The headline guard is E.02**: expanded across **all 11,451 real text entries, no marker survives anywhere** — a survivor is a raw `{...}` shown to a player.

**Tests**: new `m27i_text_buffers_test` **42/42**, three breaks verified. Regression: `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `m27i_item_identity_test` 31/31.

**Next**: I3 — the bag and the item opcodes, which is what unblocks Brock.

**[M27I I3 — the bag and the item opcodes] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31. BROCK'S REWARD CHAIN RUNS TO THE END.**

The wall the whole vertical slice was stopped against: Brock's win path ran its reward chain and halted on `checkitemspace`. Driven on the REAL compiled script now — `PewterCity_Gym_EventScript_DefeatedBrock` runs **28 ops and ends DONE**, not UNKNOWN_OP, with **TM39 in the bag and `FLAG_BADGE01_GET` set**.

New `scripts/overworld/bag.gd`, held on `OverworldSession` beside `FlagStore` — a battle is a real scene swap, and a bag held on the overworld would be discarded with it.

⚠️ **IT IS A SLOT MODEL, NOT A `{item: count}` DICTIONARY, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE POINT OF `checkitemspace`.** Source's bag is a fixed array of slots per pocket, so "is there room" is a question about SLOTS. A dictionary answers yes forever and makes every bag-full branch in every script dead code. Break-tested: removing the slot limit fails B.14 and D.05.

**Capacities are source's own** (`global.h`): ITEMS 30 · POKE_BALLS 16 · TM_HM 64 · BERRIES 46 · KEY_ITEMS 30, stack cap 999.

⚠️ **TM/HM AND BERRIES TAKE ONE STACK PER ITEM AND REFUSE MORE.** `BagPocket_AddItem` branches those two pockets out specifically — if a count cannot fit one slot it returns FALSE, where every other pocket spills into a second. Treating all five alike would let 1000 Berries into a pocket that must say no. Break-tested: fails C.03/C.04.

⚠️ **ADD AND REMOVE ARE ALL-OR-NOTHING, WHICH IS SOURCE'S CONTRACT, NOT A SIMPLIFICATION.** Source computes into a scratch buffer and only writes real slots once the whole count fits. A partial add leaves a script that branched on "you did not get it" holding some of it.

⚠️ **AND THE OBVIOUS TEST FOR THAT WAS VACUOUS.** B.15 fills a pocket EXACTLY, which cannot overflow anyway — the fill loop simply finds no room — so deleting the up-front space check left B.15 green. The real hazard needs a PART-FULL stack and no free slot: a naive add tops that stack up, then fails, having already banked 499. **B.16 was written specifically because injecting the mistake did not fail the test that looked like it covered it.** Same lesson as `[M27F Stage 3b]`'s K.15.

**`giveitem`/`finditem`/`giveitem_msg` reproduce `STD_OBTAIN_ITEM`'s DECISION STRUCTURE** — add, buffer the pluralised name, resolve the pocket, buffer its name, then branch to "Obtained the ..."/"put away in the ... POCKET" or "The BAG is full". Same approach `[M27F Stage 2]` took for `trainerbattle_single`, and for the same reason: the real script calls `playfanfare`/`showitemdescription`, which have no equivalent here, while its branching IS the behaviour. **The text is not invented** — every page is source's own string, already in `map_texts.json` from Stage 1.

They **pause on WAIT_MESSAGE** like `message` does. A `giveitem` that ran silently would skip the whole obtained beat.

**The four primitives** (`additem`/`removeitem`/`checkitem`/`checkitemspace`) plus `checkitemtype` all set `VAR_RESULT` and run **both** arguments through the variable store — `additem VAR_0x8009`, `checkitemspace VAR_TEMP_0` are real corpus forms, 64 region-wide. `checkitemtype` writes the POCKET rather than a boolean, which is what the obtain flow switches on.

⚠️ **THE UNKNOWN-OPCODE FIXTURE MOVED FOR THE THIRD TIME AND IS NOW PINNED.** `applymovement` (Stage 3) -> `checkitemspace` (here) -> `frontier_set`. Each move was a real correctness change invalidating a stale assumption, but picking the next roadblock is picking a fixture with an expiry date — `[M27C]` already paid for that when a "known unbaked" map got baked and took ten assertions down. `frontier_set` belongs to a PERMANENTLY EXCLUDED subsystem (Battle Frontier facilities), so it is real data nothing will implement out from under the test.

**[M27O O1 — the respawn point] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31.**

`setrespawn` now stores where a whiteout will send the player. New `scripts/overworld/respawn_point.gd`, held on `OverworldSession` beside the bag and the flags — it has to survive the scene swap a battle performs, and a respawn point forgotten by the very fight that caused the whiteout would be worse than none.

⚠️ **THE HEAL POINT AND THE RESPAWN POINT ARE DIFFERENT PLACES, AND SOURCE KEEPS TWO TABLES FOR IT.** A heal location's `map`/`x`/`y` is the OUTDOOR tile the Pokémon Centre stands on — what Teleport and fly use. Its `respawn_map` is INSIDE: the Centre, or **for Pallet the player's own house**, which is the one entry where a "respawn == Pokémon Centre" assumption is simply wrong. Collapsing them drops the player outdoors after a whiteout instead of in front of a nurse. Break-tested: fails A.06/B.02/B.03/B.04.

⚠️ **`respawn_x`/`respawn_y` ARE OPTIONAL AND THEIR DEFAULT IS REAL.** Most entries omit them because every Centre has the same interior, and source's template fills `DEFAULT_POKEMON_CENTER_X 7` / `_Y 4`. Reading a missing coordinate as 0 puts the player in the wall. The generator reads both defaults out of the template rather than hardcoding them. Break-tested: fails A.05.

⚠️ **A CORRECTION MADE MID-STEP-0, WORTH RECORDING BECAUSE IT NEARLY RESHAPED THE PHASE.** A first pass concluded the heal-location table was Hoenn-only and Kanto had none — which would have meant inventing the data. It was a bad filter: it looked for an `FRLG` suffix in the map name, and Kanto entries carry none (`MAP_PALLET_TOWN`, not `MAP_PALLET_TOWN_FRLG`). **20 of the 42 entries are Kanto**, and all four the corridor sets are among them. Checking rather than acting on the first result is what saved a phase built on fabricated data.

New `scripts/gen_heal_locations.py` -> `data/heal_locations.json`: 42 entries with map constants already resolved to this project's own names through the `MapConstants` table `[M27B]` generated, so nothing downstream re-derives that mapping. Idempotent.

**An unknown location is REFUSED, not stored.** Keeping it would turn into an unresolvable warp at whiteout time — the worst possible moment, with the player already fainted and nowhere to go. The opcode names it in `diagnostic` instead, the same degrade-and-report contract every other opcode here follows. Break-tested: fails C.04/D.03/D.04.

**The starting respawn resolves from `start_map`** rather than being hardcoded, so moving the start for a playtest moves the respawn with it instead of stranding a Pewter session back in Pallet; a start map with no heal location of its own falls back to the story's first. That is a **stand-in for M27K's new-game flow**, not a mechanic — source sets the first respawn from its opening sequence.

**Headline guard E.02/E.03**: every one of the **39 distinct locations the corpus sets** is known AND resolves to a real respawn map. A script setting a respawn the player can never be sent to is a bug that only appears the first time someone loses.

⚠️ **Only 3 of 42 respawn maps are baked today** (the corridor's own). That is expected — the rest are outside the vertical slice — but it means O2 must handle "respawn map not baked" as a real case rather than assuming the destination exists.

**Tests**: new `m27o_respawn_test` **24/24**, three breaks verified. Regression: `m27i_bag_test` 47/47, `m27i_text_buffers_test` 42/42, `m27i_item_identity_test` 31/31, `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

**[M27O O2 — the loss path] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31. Losing a battle now costs something.**

Live-driven end to end: parked script on WAIT_BATTLE, force a LOSS, and the player wakes at **PewterCity_PokemonCenter_1F_Frlg (7, 4)** — script abandoned, trainer NOT flagged beaten, reward flag NOT leaked.

Source's own sequence is `DoWhiteOut` (`overworld.c:392`): run the money script, heal the party, reset state, warp to the last heal location.

⚠️ **A DEFEAT ALONE DOES NOT WHITE OUT.** `CB2_EndTrainerBattle`'s branch is `IsPlayerDefeated && ... && !NoAliveMonsForPlayer()` — with live Pokémon you simply return to the field. That second half is **currently unreachable-but-equivalent** here: there is NO PERSISTENT PARTY (`build_debug_player_party` builds a fresh full-health one per battle), so a defeat means the party was wiped in that battle and "no alive mons" is true by construction. It becomes a real distinction the moment a party survives between battles — M27K/M27L — and the gate is documented where it belongs rather than silently omitted.

⚠️ **THE PARKED SCRIPT MUST NOT RESUME, AND THAT IS THE REAL PENALTY.** `CB2_WhiteOut` calls `ScriptContext_Init()`, wiping the script state outright — a trainer's post-battle branch does not run after you black out. Resuming it would hand out the reward for a fight you lost. New `_abandon_script()` is distinct from the normal finish path, which reports where a script stopped; a script dropped by a blackout did not stop at a coverage gap, so saying so would be noise.

**The destination is the RESPAWN point, not the heal point** — at this project's config `OW_WHITEOUT_CUTSCENE` is GEN_LATEST, so `SetWarpDestinationToLastHealLocation` takes its `IsWhiteoutCutscene()` branch and warps INSIDE. O1's own headline, now consumed.

**The party heal is deliberately absent, with its position preserved.** Source heals between the money loss and the warp; with no persistent party there is nothing to heal and a call would be theatre. The comment marks the spot so M27K/M27L drop it in rather than rediscovering where it goes. The money loss is O3, still blocked on a wallet.

⚠️ **`_apply_battle_result` REPORTS rather than performs, and that shape was forced by a real hazard.** The battle-return spawn path runs BEFORE `_add_camera`/`_add_fade`, so a whiteout there would fade an overlay and re-centre a camera that do not exist yet. It now returns whether one is owed; the overlay path performs it immediately, the spawn path records `_pending_whiteout` and `_ready` performs it once the scene is whole. The `_in_battle` guard is still held across the call — clearing it first is what let a SECOND battle start in D5.

⚠️ **THE REGRESSION SUITE CAUGHT A REAL ORDERING BUG I INTRODUCED, AND IT IS WORTH RECORDING.** Extracting the shared teardown out of `_do_warp` put `manager.warp_arrival(dest, ...)` BEFORE `manager.load_chunk(dest, ...)` — so every warp resolved its destination against a chunk that was not loaded, got `{}`, and silently fell back to `_first_walkable`. **Seventeen warp assertions failed at once** (AE/AF/AG/AH/AI/AJ). The helper is now split into `_teardown_and_load` and `_place_player` precisely so the load cannot drift ahead of the resolution again, and the reason is recorded at the split. Nothing about the whiteout itself was wrong; the damage was entirely to the door warps it was sharing code with.

**Tests**: new `m27o_whiteout_test` **22/22** — which outcomes are defeats (a DRAW is one), the flag staying unset on every defeat outcome, the destination being the respawn rather than the heal point, and the deferred-whiteout wiring. Regression: `m27a_step_resolver_test` **514/514** (after the fix), `m27o_respawn_test` 24/24, `m27i_bag_test` 47/47, `m27i_text_buffers_test` 42/42, `m27i_item_identity_test` 31/31, `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

**[M27I I3b — the wallet] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31. Money and coins, and O3 is unblocked.**

New `scripts/overworld/wallet.gd` on `OverworldSession` beside the bag, plus `checkmoney`/`addmoney`/`removemoney`/`checkcoins`/`addcoins`/`removecoins`, and the three money-box display commands accepted as no-ops (`hidemoneybox` alone is 42 corpus uses — treating them as unknown would halt scripts that are otherwise fully runnable).

⚠️ **THERE ARE NOW THREE DIFFERENT REMOVAL SEMANTICS IN THIS PROJECT, AND THEY ARE ALL SOURCE-CORRECT.** `Bag.remove` is ALL-OR-NOTHING; `Wallet.spend` CLAMPS TO ZERO and never fails (`RemoveMoney`); `Wallet.remove_coins` is ALL-OR-NOTHING again (`RemoveCoins`). Making them consistent would be tidier and wrong — the whiteout payout depends on `spend` clamping, because it asks for badge-scaled money and expects to take everything you have when that is less. Break-tested: making `spend` refuse fails A.06.

⚠️ **THE COIN RESULT IS INVERTED.** Source sets `VAR_RESULT` to **FALSE on SUCCESS** for both coin commands (`if (AddCoins(..) == TRUE) gSpecialVar_Result = FALSE`). Reading it the natural way round makes every Game Corner "did that work" branch take the wrong path. Break-tested: fails C.08/C.09.

**Money and coin arguments are read differently, in source and in the corpus**: money is a raw `ScriptReadWord`, coins go through `VarGet`. Stated honestly, though — `_resolve_number` is literal-first, so for numeric data the two agree and injecting the wrong one breaks nothing. The literal read is kept for fidelity and the test asserts what actually protects it rather than pretending to a difference it cannot demonstrate.

⚠️ **A REAL COMPILER GAP FOUND BY THAT ASSERTION, AND IT IS NOT A WALLET PROBLEM.** Rewriting the vacuous guard into "every money arg is a literal" failed immediately: **6 are file-scoped assembler constants** — `COINS_PRICE_500`, `COINS_PRICE_50`, `MAGIKARP_PRICE` — declared with `.equ`/`.set` INSIDE the map script that uses them, which `gen_map_scripts.py` does not resolve. **32 such constants exist region-wide**, and `REQUIRED_CAUGHT_MONS` is declared THREE times with three different values (30/40/50), so the fix belongs in the compiler WITH FILE SCOPE — a global table would be wrong for that one. Flagged, not fixed: it is a shared-pipeline change plus an 8.8 MB regeneration, outside a wallet phase.

⚠️ **AND THE DEGRADE HAD TO FAIL CLOSED.** Reading an unresolved price as 0 makes `checkmoney` report AFFORDABLE and the following `removemoney` charge nothing — **free goods**. It now reports "cannot afford" and names the constant in `diagnostic`. Guessing from the name would be worse still: **`COINS_PRICE_50` is 1000**, so the suffix is the COIN count and a name-derived value would be wrong by 20x. Break-tested: failing open fails C.05/C.05b.

**Save shape clamps on load** rather than trusting the file, so a hand-edited save cannot produce an impossible wallet.

**Tests**: new `m27i_wallet_test` **31/31**, three breaks verified. Regression: `m27o_whiteout_test` 22/22, `m27o_respawn_test` 24/24, `m27i_bag_test` 47/47, `m27i_text_buffers_test` 42/42, `m27i_item_identity_test` 31/31, `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514.

**M27O O3 IS NOW UNBLOCKED, and its scoping needs one correction**: the whiteout money loss is NOT in `EventScript_WhiteOut` — that script is only state resets. It lives in `Cmd_getmoneyreward` (`battle_script_commands.c:5850`), which handles the win prize AND the loss payout, so it belongs on the battle-return path rather than in `_do_whiteout`. Formula confirmed: `sWhiteOutBadgeMoney[badge_count] * highest_party_level`, clamped to what the player holds.

**[M27O O3 — the money] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31. M27O IS CLOSED except the deferred field-poison path.**

Live-driven both ways: **5000 -> 4808** on a loss (16 x 12 = 192, one badge, level 12) and **4808 -> 6708** on a win (+1900), with the trainer flagged beaten only on the win.

⚠️ **A CORRECTION TO O3'S OWN EARLIER SCOPING, MADE AT STEP 0.** The whiteout money loss is NOT in `EventScript_WhiteOut` — that script is `call ResetEliteFour` / `goto ResetMrBriney` / `end`, state resets only. It lives in **`Cmd_getmoneyreward`** (`battle_script_commands.c:5850`), which owns the win prize AND the loss payout in one place. So both halves are applied together on the battle-return path rather than split across the win path and `_do_whiteout` — which is what the earlier scoping would have had me build.

**The formula**, at this project's `B_WHITEOUT_MONEY = GEN_LATEST`: `sWhiteOutBadgeMoney[badge_count] * highest_party_level`, table `[8, 16, 24, 36, 48, 64, 80, 100, 120]` indexed 0-8 badges, badges counted over `gBadgeFlags`' own eight. **Not** the older "half your money" rule, which is what `<= GEN_3` would give.

**Clamped to what the player holds** (`if (!IsEnoughMoney(..)) money = GetMoney()`). `whiteout_payout` returns the REAL amount taken rather than the amount asked for — `Wallet.spend` clamps anyway, but the difference matters the moment anything reports the figure to the player. A broke player loses 0 rather than going negative; a level-0 party is floored at 1 rather than making a loss free.

⚠️ **THE WIN PRIZE HAD BEEN COMPUTED SINCE M24b AND CONSUMED BY NOTHING.** `money_awarded`/`last_money_awarded` shipped with that milestone, tested at exactly 1900, and no consumer ever existed — there was no wallet to put it in. I3b built one; this banks it. A two-line `prize_money()` accessor on the battle screen keeps the field from reaching into `_bm`.

**`BattleOutcome` carries the prize and the highest party level**, because the field has NO PERSISTENT PARTY — it builds one per battle, so the level is captured at battle setup, the only moment it is known for certain. Source reads it straight off the party inside `Cmd_getmoneyreward`.

**A GDScript gotcha this project has already paid for recurred**: `var asked := WHITEOUT_BADGE_MONEY[...] * ...` failed to parse — indexing an untyped Array yields Variant, which `:=` cannot infer from. Same class as `[M27F Stage 3]`'s own `locked_moved`. Fixed with an explicit `: int`, and the reason recorded at the line.
