**[M27O O4 — field poison] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. M27O IS NOW FULLY CLOSED.**

Live-driven in the real scene: a poisoned Bulbasaur at 5 HP walking west out of Pewter lost **exactly 1 HP on steps 4, 8, 12 and 16**, the counter cycling 1/2/3/0 throughout, and on reaching 1 HP was **cured** with *"Bulbasaur survived the poisoning. The poison faded away!"* in the real message box, the world frozen until dismissed.

⚠️ **THE RULE BUILT IS GEN 4, AND IT IS DELIBERATELY NOT THIS PROJECT'S `GEN_LATEST` DEFAULT.** Rob's explicit call, stated here because every other config in this project follows `GEN_LATEST` and a later session will reasonably assume this one does too. `OW_POISON_DAMAGE`'s own comment (`include/config/overworld.h:9`) reads *"In Gen4, Pokemon no longer faint from Poison in the overworld. In Gen5+, they no longer take damage at all"* — and at `GEN_LATEST` the whole system is **compiled out**: `UpdatePoisonStepCounter` AND its single call site both sit inside `#if OW_POISON_DAMAGE < GEN_5` (`field_control_avatar.c:761, 862`). So the *newest* behaviour is no field poison whatsoever, and what Rob described is reachable at exactly one value, `OW_POISON_DAMAGE == GEN_4`.

⚠️ **NO FAINT HOOKUP IS NEEDED, AND THAT IS SOURCE RATHER THAN A SIMPLIFICATION.** `Task_TryFieldPoisonWhiteOut` only whites out when `AllMonsFainted()` holds, and that tests `HP == 0` (`field_poison.c:35-43`). At `>= GEN_4` nothing reaches 0 from poison, so `EventScript_FieldPoison`'s own `goto_if_eq VAR_RESULT, FLDPSN_WHITEOUT` is dead code at this config. Rob's instinct was exactly right.

**The mechanics, each pinned by a test.** Damage is a **flat 1 HP**, not a maxHP fraction — source's own `--hp`, and the natural wrong guess given every in-battle residual here. **Toxic ticks at the same flat rate as ordinary poison**: `GetAilmentFromStatus` collapses both onto `AILMENT_PSN`, so the escalation this project models in battle is deliberately absent out of it. Every **4th** step (`(*ptr)++; (*ptr) %= 4`), cleared at every battle entry as source does (`battle_setup.c:262, 298, 986`). Ordinary ticks are **silent** — source returns FALSE for both `FLDPSN_NONE` and `FLDPSN_PSN`, so no script runs and no message prints. The mon is **cured, not healed**: it stays on 1 HP.

⚠️ **A MON ALREADY AT 1 HP RE-ANNOUNCES EVERY TICK, and the CURE is what breaks the loop, not the damage.** Source's `hp == 1 || --hp == 1` short-circuits, so a parked mon counts again on every pass. Reproduced exactly, and pinned — a caller that ran the damage without the cure would print forever.

⚠️ **THE REAL BLOCKER WAS THE PARTY, NOT THE STEP COUNTER — `[M27O]`'S OWN SCOPING UNDERSTATED IT.** There was no persistent overworld party: `build_debug_player_party()` made a fresh full-health team per battle and nothing wrote HP or status back, so there was nothing to poison and no way for poison to get in. **Rob's call: give the overworld one.** `OverworldSession.party` now holds the same (still DEBUG) team across battles, mutated in place — M27K's starter choice replaces it wholesale and M27L saves it; only where it lives and how long changed.

**That made two things load-bearing that earlier entries could correctly call otherwise.** New `BattleManager.restore_party_after_battle` is the equivalent of source's copy-back — source keeps `gPlayerParty` (what survives) and `gBattleMons` (volatiles) apart, while this project conflates them, so discarding the volatiles has to be an explicit pass or a +6 Swords Dance follows the player into the next fight. It reuses `_switch_out_clear` rather than re-enumerating ~60 fields, because two hand-kept copies of one rule is the drift `check_bake_diff` already cost this project once. And **`[M27O O2]`'s whiteout heal is no longer theatre** — that entry was right at the time, but with a persistent party a whiteout that did not heal would drop the player at the Centre with the same wiped team and start the next battle on a fainted lead. The slot O2 left is exactly the right one.

**Two source gates are absent because nothing can trip them yet**, recorded so they are found rather than rediscovered: `MAP_TYPE_SECRET_BASE` (no secret bases, no map-type concept) and the forced-movement skip (ice, currents, spin tiles — **M27E**, unbuilt). When forced movement lands, `_poison_step` needs that guard.

**Ordering is source's own**: `CheckForTrainersWantingBattle` runs at the very top of `ProcessPlayerFieldInput`, before it even tests `input->tookStep`, so a trainer who just spotted you takes precedence over the step scripts poison lives in.

⚠️ **A LIVE-DRIVE FINDING THAT IS NOT A BUG, AND THE READING THAT WAS WRONG FIRST.** The probe reported the message box refusing to close after five A presses, and a direct `advance()` call not closing it either — which reads exactly like a soft-lock. It is not: instrumenting the box showed `typing == true` at that moment, so `advance()` legitimately consumed the call as a typewriter skip (source lets you skip), and the presses never reached it at all — **a synthetic just-pressed edge is 0-frames observable from a driver**, measured. Driving `advance()` to completion closed it in 2 calls and the player then walked 4 cells, proving the freeze was the box and not a wall. **Worth recording for every future probe: `Input.action_press` drives HELD state fine — which is why walking has always been drivable — and cannot produce a `just_pressed` edge at all**, so `try_interact`, the VM's own `WAIT_BUTTON` and this drive are all undrivable that way.

**Tests**: new `m27o_field_poison_test` **57/57**, with all four headline guards break-tested and confirmed to catch their regressions (a maxHP fraction instead of flat 1 fails B.01/B.04/B.14; firing on step 1 instead of 4 fails A.02/A.04/A.05; dropping the `hp == 1` short-circuit fails D.01/D.02; a restore that also heals fails E.06/E.07/E.09). Regression: `m27o_whiteout_test` 33/33, `m27o_respawn_test` 24/24, `m27i_wallet_test` 31/31, `m27i_bag_test` 47/47, `m27i_text_buffers_test` 42/42, `m27i_item_identity_test` 31/31, `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `switch_test` 64/64, `m24b_test` 61/61, `m26_trainer_category_party_test` 130/130, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

**M27O IS CLOSED — O1, O2, O3 and O4 are all done.** Two consequences of the persistent party worth carrying forward: a battle's damage and status now persist, so healing matters and the Pokecentre has a real job (M27I I5); and `[M27O O2]`'s own note that source's `NoAliveMonsForPlayer()` gate is "unreachable-but-equivalent here" is **no longer true** — a party can now survive a defeat, so that gate has become a real distinction rather than a construction.

**[M27F Stage 4 — the yes/no prompt, and healing] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. The Pokecentre works.**

Live-driven in the real Pewter Centre: talk to the nurse **over the counter**, prompt appears with the cursor on YES, and a party of `3/32 poisoned | 0/33 FAINTED | 3/33 poisoned` comes back **fully healed, status cleared, the fainted one revived**, script running to completion.

**Why this was next**: `[M27O O4]`'s persistent party made damage and status carry between fights and left **no way to heal except whiting out** — a gap that session opened. The nurse was standing there and could not work.

⚠️ **THE HEADLINE, AND IT WOULD HAVE INVERTED SILENTLY: KANTO'S NURSE DOES NOT USE `yesnobox` AT ALL.** Reading `pkmn_center_nurse.inc` gave the Hoenn script — `yesnobox`, then `goto_if_eq VAR_RESULT, YES`. Kanto's is its own file (`pkmn_center_nurse_frlg.inc`) and uses **`multichoice 19, 8, MULTI_YESNO`** + `switch VAR_RESULT` + `case 0 -> heal`. **The two have OPPOSITE polarity**: `yesnobox` writes **1 for YES** (`Task_HandleYesNoInput`), while `multichoice` writes the **LIST INDEX**, and `MultichoiceList_YesNo` is `{Yes, NO}` — so **0 is YES**. Behind one identical-looking prompt. **Found by the test, not by reading**, which is the whole argument for driving the real corpus rather than a fixture. Resolved by putting the polarity in the VM (`answer_yes_no`), where the opcode that paused is known — a caller picking either convention would heal on NO across half the region.

⚠️ **A SECOND SILENT INVERTER, ALREADY LIVE BEFORE THIS SESSION: `_literal` RESOLVED `YES` TO 0.** **753 corpus args** are `YES`/`NO`/`MULTI_B_PRESSED` and every one of them read as 0, so `goto_if_eq VAR_RESULT, YES` compared against 0 — a NO answer taking the YES branch at every yes/no site in Kanto. Fixed: `YES = 1` (`event.inc:2133`), `NO = 0`, `MULTI_B_PRESSED = 127` (`script_menu.h:8`).

⚠️ **A THIRD, CAUGHT IN MY OWN DESIGN BEFORE IT SHIPPED.** The specialvar allowlist was first written as a blanket "answer 0", which carries **both** nurse scripts through and is WRONG on exactly the negated predicates: `PlayerNotAtTrainerHillEntrance` and `IsPlayerNotInTrainerTowerLobby` both `return TRUE` unless you are standing in that one facility (`field_specials.c:2143, 5408`), so 1 is the honest answer in a Pokecentre. Both branches reach the same place either way — which is precisely why a convenient-but-false value would have survived testing and been wrong somewhere else. Now a value PER ENTRY, each with its reason. **Two of the five are 1.**

**The widget** (`YesNoBox`) is source-shaped: cursor **defaults to YES** (`DisplayYesNoMenuDefaultYes`, `initialCursorPos = 0`), does **not wrap** (`Menu_ProcessInputNoWrap*`), and **B folds onto NO** for `yesnobox` while `multichoice` has its own distinct 127. ⚠️ It ports source's **5-frame input debounce** (`Task_HandleYesNoInput`'s `tRight < 5`) — without it the same A press that dismissed the question is still down and instantly confirms YES, which reads as "the prompt does not work", not as a timing bug. Held as SECONDS, per `[M26G4]`'s refresh-rate finding.

**The specials carve-out** (`FieldSpecials`) is deliberately **NOT M27G**, same shape as `[M26B4-0]`'s: `HealPlayerParty` for real (`script_pokemon_util.c:38` — max HP, status cleared, PP restored, exactly what `heal_party` already did for the whiteout), two no-ops, five allowlisted specialvars. ⚠️ **AN UNKNOWN SPECIAL STILL HALTS.** Degrading would hide the other ~2,100 call sites and make `[M27F]`'s coverage figures meaningless. **`multichoice` likewise implements ONLY `MULTI_YESNO`** and halts on the other 204 uses rather than guessing at facility menus.

**A sizing correction for M27G**: this row estimates "a few hundred" distinct special functions. Measured — **1,390 `special` + 719 `specialvar` = 2,109 uses across 569 distinct functions**, plus 62 `callnative` across 28 more. M27G is bigger than written down.

⚠️ **AN UPSTREAM DATA BUG, REPRODUCED RATHER THAN FIXED.** `pkmn_center_nurse_frlg.inc:20` is literally `case 1 EventScript_PkmnCenterNurse_Goodbye_Frlg` — **a missing comma in pokeemerald-expansion itself**, the only malformed `case` in all 2,027 in the corpus. Not this project's compiler. Harmless here: the malformed arg degenerates to value 0, which never matches VAR_RESULT 1, so answering NO simply ends without the goodbye line rather than healing. Recorded so nobody "fixes" the compiler for it.

**Probe finding worth carrying forward**, measured while driving this and `[M27O O4]`: **`Input.action_press` from a driver cannot produce a `just_pressed` edge at all** (0 frames of 4), though HELD state drives fine — which is why walking has always been drivable. So `try_interact`, the VM's `WAIT_BUTTON` and this prompt are all undrivable that way; drive the widget/VM API instead.

**Tests**: new `m27f_stage4_test` **52/52**, including the real Pewter nurse end-to-end in both directions (YES heals, **NO leaves the party unhealed** — without which the prompt is decorative). Five headline guards break-tested: a blanket-0 allowlist fails C.07; **swapped polarity fails D.04/D.05, i.e. it heals on NO**; a NO-default cursor fails A.02; no debounce fails six A-section assertions; a degrading unknown special fails B.04/B.05. Regression: `m27o_field_poison_test` 57/57, `m27o_whiteout_test` 33/33, `m27o_respawn_test` 24/24, `m27i_wallet_test` 31/31, `m27i_bag_test` 47/47, `m27i_text_buffers_test` 42/42, `m27i_item_identity_test` 31/31, `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

**[M27F Stage 4 follow-up — the nurse heals on contact] 2026-08-03.** Rob's call: drop her confirmation. Talking to the nurse has exactly one purpose, so the prompt was a keypress between the player and the only thing they came for.

⚠️ **A DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE FROM SOURCE, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT "FIXED" BACK.** Source really does ask ("Would you like me to heal your POKéMON back to perfect health?") and this really does not. A later session reading `Std_MsgboxYesNo` WILL find the prompt and try to restore it.

**Keyed on the SCRIPT LABEL, never a global setting** (`ScriptVM.AUTO_CONFIRM_LABELS`, both nurses listed). A blanket auto-confirm would silently answer all **425** yes/no sites — shops, tutors, trades — every one of which is a real choice with a real cost. Break-tested: ignoring the label fails F.07 plus four E-section assertions.

**The polarity is now in ONE place** (`_write_yes_no`), shared by the real prompt and the auto path, so the `yesnobox`-writes-1 / `multichoice`-writes-0 split cannot drift between them. Break-tested: making the auto path ignore polarity fails F.04 **and D.04/D.05** — the Kanto nurse silently stops healing.

**Live-driven**: `2/34 psn | 2/31 psn | 0/35 FAINTED` -> all three full, status cleared, revived, **`EVER PROMPTED: false`**.

⚠️ **ONE CONSEQUENCE LEFT AS-IS, FLAGGED RATHER THAN SILENTLY PATCHED**: the script still PRINTS *"Would you like me to heal your POKéMON back to perfect health?"* and then heals without waiting — a rhetorical question. Suppressing it means suppressing authored dialogue, which is a content decision rather than the prompt removal Rob asked for. One line in the same allowlist shape if wanted.

**Tests**: `m27f_stage4_test` 52 -> **59/59**. ⚠️ Two assertions were REWRITTEN, not deleted, because the change legitimately invalidated them: D.02 asserted the nurse *reaches* a prompt (now asserts she never does), and D.05 answered NO to prove the prompt was not decorative (now asserts she heals with **nobody answering anything** — strictly stronger for the new design, and it would hang if the prompt returned). Regression: `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `m27o_field_poison_test` 57/57, `m27o_whiteout_test` 33/33, `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `m27i_bag_test` 47/47.

**Still open on the slice**: the bag SCREEN (M27I I4 — you win TM39 and cannot look at it), wild encounters (M27H), and the party/Summary screens (M27I I5).

**[M27F Stage 4 follow-up 2 — the nurse question removed] 2026-08-03.** With the confirmation gone the script still PRINTED *"Would you like me to heal your POKéMON back to perfect health?"* and then healed anyway. Rob's call to drop it.

**New `ScriptVM.TEXT_OVERRIDES`, keyed on the TEXT SYMBOL** rather than the script, so a line reads the same way everywhere it is used. ⚠️ **This is the FIRST place this project overrides reference dialogue** — `[M27]`'s own premise is that geometry and systems are imported while content and meaning are authored, so a table like this was always going to exist; this is it starting, with two entries (Kanto's and Hoenn's greetings). The greeting stays, the question goes.

**Tests**: new Section G (4 assertions) — the override REPLACES rather than appends, the greeting survives, the question is gone. `m27f_stage4_test` 59 -> **63/63**.

**[M27I I4 — the field Bag] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. You can finally look at what you are carrying.**

Live-driven in the real overworld: START -> BAG shows **`Potion x4`** / **`Poké Ball x10`** / **`TM39 x1`** across real pockets with real descriptions, and the two empty pockets say so. **TM39 is Brock's own reward** — `[M27I I3]` put it in the bag and nothing had ever displayed it.

**New `FieldStartMenu`, because I4 needed a way IN.** Source opens the bag from the START menu (`StartMenuBagCallback`), and a dedicated hotkey would have been a second mechanism to retire later. ⚠️ **ITS SHORT ENTRY LIST IS SOURCE'S OWN CONDITIONAL RESULT, NOT A STUB**: `BuildNormalStartMenu` adds POKéDEX only on `FLAG_SYS_POKEDEX_GET`, POKéMON only on `FLAG_SYS_POKEMON_GET`, and those flags are genuinely unset — so the entries appear on their own the moment M33 / I5 set them, with no code change here. **Three entries source adds UNCONDITIONALLY are deliberately omitted** (player name -> M27K, SAVE -> M27L, OPTION -> unscoped): a dead entry the player cannot distinguish from a broken one is worse than no entry.

**New `FieldBagScreen`, deliberately NOT a reuse of `ItemSelectScreen`.** That screen (`[M25h-1.4]`) is the BATTLE bag — three hardcoded items, one pocket, calling back into a `BattleScreen` parent — and reworking it is **M26E1/E2**'s job; touching it now would risk the battle screen for no gain. Source itself draws the line: the field bag opens with ALL pockets (`GoToBagMenu(ITEMMENULOCATION_FIELD, POCKETS_COUNT, ...)`) where battle's is restricted, which is why pocket switching exists here and not there.

⚠️ **POCKETS WRAP, THE ITEM LIST CLAMPS.** Both wrapping would make a held Down key cycle forever with nothing telling the player it had. Pocket names and order are source's own (`gPocketNamesStringsTable`), and **a row is a STACK, not an item** — the bag caps a stack at 999, so 1000 Potions is genuinely two rows, which is what I3 stores and what a full bag depends on. **Key items and HMs show no count**, matching source's own separate `gText_NumberItem_HM` format.

⚠️ **A DISPLAY BUG THE LIVE DRIVE CAUGHT AND NO TEST WOULD HAVE.** `items.json` carries source's own **literal `\n` two-character sequences** — the reference hand-wraps its description box at a fixed GBA width — so descriptions rendered with visible backslash-n in them. Unwrapped to spaces rather than converted to real newlines, since this box is a different width and autowraps itself. Pinned by D.01b.

⚠️ **A VACUOUS GUARD, CAUGHT ONLY BY ITS OWN INJECTION — the fourth time this project has hit this shape.** C.07/C.08 asserted the item list CLAMPS, and passed against a deliberately WRAPPING implementation: the fixture had ONE row, where `wrapi(0+1, 0, 1)` and `clampi(0+1, 0, 0)` are both 0, so the two competing rules could not disagree. Rebuilt on a two-item pocket and re-injected the wrap to confirm it now fails. **A fixture where the competing rules agree is not a guard**, and it is worth restating: this is the same lesson as `[M27F Stage 3b]`'s K.15 and `[M27I I3]`'s B.15.

**Tests**: new `m27i_i4_bag_screen_test` **48/48**. Four guards break-tested: pockets clamping fails C.04/C.05, an HM showing a count fails D.03, an unconditional POKéDEX fails E.01/E.04, and the rebuilt clamp guard fails C.07/C.08 (it did not before). Regression: `m27f_stage4_test` 63/63, `m27i_bag_test` 47/47, `m27i_item_identity_test` 31/31, `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `m27o_field_poison_test` 57/57, `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

⚠️ **DISCLOSED, NOT BUILT**: the bag is READ-ONLY. There is no use / give / toss, no item-use from the field at all — a TM cannot be taught, a Potion cannot be drunk. Using an item needs a party screen to use it ON, which is **I5**, so this is a real ordering dependency rather than an omission. **Chrome is plain Godot `Panel`**, not the real `bag_frame.png` this project already has: M26E1 owns the visual rework and would redo it. **M27I status: I1, I2, I3, I3b and I4 are done; I5 (party/Summary/PC) is what remains.** [I5-2/I5-3 have since landed — see below; I5-3a has since landed too; I5-4 (Summary) and I5-5 (PC) remain.]

**[M27I I5-2 + I5-3 — the field party screen, and using an item on it] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. The read-only bag can now do something.**

Live-driven in the real overworld: START → BAG (`Potion x3`, real description) → USE → the party opens as a **target picker** titled *"Use Potion on which POKéMON?"* → confirm on a poisoned `Bulbasaur 4/34 PSN` → **24/34, one Potion gone, bag row now `x2`**. This closes the gap `[M27I I4]` opened deliberately and `[M27O O4]` made matter — that session gave the overworld a persistent party, so damage and status now carry between fights and something has to heal them outside a Pokécentre.

⚠️ **ITEM USE STARTS IN THE BAG, NOT THE PARTY — and that is source's flow, not a shortcut.** `ItemUseOutOfBattle_Medicine` opens the party menu as a target picker, and `docs/m26_e3_recon.md`'s own out-of-scope list says the same thing in this project's words: *"item flow lives in the bag screen"*. So `FieldPartyScreen` only ever reports which slot was chosen; the caller decides what that means. Confirmed by the live drive that source **returns you to the bag** afterwards rather than closing everything, so you can use a second Potion without re-navigating — reproduced.

⚠️ **IT SHOWS ALL SIX SLOTS, FAINTED INCLUDED — AND THAT IS SOURCE, NOT A DIVERGENCE FROM IT.** [An earlier draft of this entry called it *"a deliberate divergence"*, which is misleading in a way that matters — it invites a later session to "restore" a filter. Rob challenged the claim; source was checked and he was right. Corrected here rather than left to mislead.] Verified directly: **`RenderPartyMenuBoxes` (`party_menu.c:1236-1243`) walks slot 0 through `PARTY_SIZE` unconditionally — there is no eligibility filter anywhere in the draw path.** Every ineligible case prints a REASON instead of vanishing, in two different places depending on when eligibility is knowable:

- **at selection time** for the battle switch (`:7544-7583`) — fainted → *"{X} has no energy left to battle!"*, already on the field → *"{X} is already in battle!"*, egg → *"An EGG can't battle!"*, already picked in a multi → *"{X} has already been selected."*, trapped → *"{X} can't switch out!"* — each sets a message then `return FALSE`, so the slot was drawn, WAS selectable, and got rejected out loud;
- **at draw time** for menus that know eligibility up front (choose-half / contest / move-tutor / evolution-item, `:1101/1125/1148/1198`) — the box prints **"NOT ABLE"** (`gText_NotAble`, `strings.c:341`) rather than disappearing.

⚠️ **THE REAL DIVERGENCE IS ON THE BATTLE SIDE, AND IT IS ALREADY SCOPED**: `switch_select_screen.gd:189` skips active-or-fainted members, which source never does. `docs/m26_e3_recon.md` §2 already records this as a known gap (*"it hides active/fainted mons instead of showing them with legality messages like source does"*) and its §5 design change #2 plans the fix — so this is a confirmation of an existing M26E3 item, not a new finding. Break-tested here anyway: adding the filter to the field screen fails A.01/A.02 (and unbalances Z.99, because the row indices shift).

⚠️ **SHOWING FAINTED ROWS MAKES THEM SELECTABLE, WHICH IS ITS OWN RISK — the mechanics are already correct.** `ItemManager.bag_item_heal` refuses a target at 0 HP, so a Potion aimed at a fainted slot is refused AND not consumed — the same observable outcome as source, whose own `RemoveBagItem` sits on the SUCCESS branch only (`party_menu.c:4922`). Pinned by D.06a and break-tested (dropping the `current_hp <= 0` check fails it). Fainted rows are marked `FNT` and stay selectable deliberately, so a Revive can target one later without redesigning the screen.

⚠️ **FIELD USABILITY IS DERIVED, AND THAT IS A DISCLOSED APPROXIMATION.** Source carries `.fieldUseFunc` per item — Potion has one, X Attack has **none**, verified directly — but `items.json` holds only description/hold_effect/pocket/price, so this keys on `battle_usage` (`RESTORE_HP` / `CURE_STATUS`) instead. It agrees with source on everything the corridor can reach; a future items-pipeline pass should pull the real field-use column rather than widen the heuristic. **Source OMITS an action it does not support rather than greying it out**, so an unusable item simply offers CANCEL and no "you can't use that" text had to be invented.

⚠️ **THE ITEM IS CONSUMED ONLY IF IT DID SOMETHING.** A Potion on a full-HP Pokémon, or a Full Heal on a healthy one, is refused rather than eaten. Break-tested by dropping the `if worked:` gate — fails D.03/D.04/D.07, the last of which is the one worth having: it proves cancelling clears the pending item, so the *next* browse does not silently spend it.

**The list CLAMPS** (the bag's pockets wrap, its item list does not — same reasoning: a held direction with nothing signalling the wrap cycles forever). Break-tested with a **three-slot** fixture, because that is the whole lesson of `[M27I I4]`'s vacuous guard — with one row `wrapi` and `clampi` give identical answers and the test proves nothing.

**`FieldStartMenu` gained POKéMON with no new gating code** — `build_entries` already mirrored `BuildNormalStartMenu`'s conditional shape, so the entry appears the moment `FLAG_SYS_POKEMON_GET` is set and not before. ⚠️ **Consequence worth stating: browse mode is currently unreachable in play** — nothing sets that flag until M27K's starter choice, so today the party screen is only reached through the bag's USE. Both paths were driven live anyway (browse shows *"Choose a POKéMON."*, cursor moves, closing consumes nothing).

**Tests**: new `m27i_i5_party_test` **43/43**. Five guards break-tested and confirmed to catch their regressions: filtering fainted slots fails A.01/A.02, wrapping instead of clamping fails B.02/B.03, offering USE unconditionally fails C.07, consuming unconditionally fails D.03/D.04/D.07, and letting a Potion heal a fainted target fails D.06a/D.07. Regression: `m27i_i4_bag_screen_test` 48/48, `m27i_bag_test` 47/47, `m27i_item_identity_test` 31/31, `m27i_wallet_test` 31/31, `m27f_stage4_test` 63/63, `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `m27o_field_poison_test` 57/57, `m27h_wild_encounters_test` 49/49, `m27h_catching_test` 49/49, `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `m22_item_action_test` 67/67, `item_test` 77/77, `item_registry_test` 309/309, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

**[M27I I5-3a — the item-use flow announces itself] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03.** Closes the gap the entry above flagged. Live-driven: heal → *"Bulbasaur's HP was restored by 20 point(s)."*, refusal → *"It won't have any effect."* (and the Potion count stays put), cure → *"Squirtle became healthy."*

⚠️ **THE CURE MESSAGE IS KEYED ON THE ITEM, NOT ON WHICH STATUS WAS CURED — and the plausible reading is the wrong one.** `GetMedicineItemEffectMessage` (`party_menu.c:4764`) switches on `GetItemEffectType(item)`, so a Full Heal (`ITEM_EFFECT_CURE_ALL_STATUS`) always prints *"became healthy"* even when the only thing it cured was poison. Its `statusCured` argument is consulted by exactly ONE case — freeze-vs-frostbite, splitting two ailments that share a single item effect type, which this project's roster cannot reach. Break-tested by keying on the ailment instead: fails G.05.

⚠️ **A CORRECTION TO THIS ENTRY'S OWN EARLIER CLAIM THAT SOURCE "RETURNS YOU TO THE BAG AFTERWARDS".** It is conditional, and decided per use: `Task_DisplayHPRestoredMessage` (`:5249`) and the cure branch (`:4946`) both hand off to `Task_ReturnToChooseMonAfterText` when `menuType == PARTY_MENU_TYPE_FIELD && CheckBagHasItem(item, 1)` — i.e. **you stay in the party menu while you still hold the item** — and to `Task_ClosePartyMenuAfterText` otherwise; a REFUSAL returns unconditionally (`:4910`), which the same count check covers since a refused item was never consumed. The earlier claim described only the last-one-used case, which is the one the first live drive happened to exercise. So "two Potions in a row" now needs no second trip through the bag. Deliberately NOT reproduced: source also resets the prompt to *"Choose a POKéMON."* on the way back (`:2065`), so a second use is prompted as if you were merely browsing — that reads as a bug, and this screen keeps naming the item.

⚠️ **TWO STRUCTURAL FIXES WERE FORCED BY THIS, NOT OPTIONAL EXTRAS.** (1) **The message box's input block sat BELOW the menus in `_process`** — fine while the poison notice was the only script-less box, since that fires while walking with nothing open, but item use announces its result with the BAG still open underneath, so `_drive_bag_screen` would have eaten every press and the box could never be dismissed. Moved above; source has the same precedence, its message window taking input while the menu freezes. (2) **`MessageBox` drew at layer 50, UNDER the bag (70) and party screen (75)** — invisible exactly when it matters. Raised to 80, with `YesNoBox` moved 60 → 85 **in lockstep**, since leaving it behind would have flipped the nurse prompt behind its own question. Section F now pins the whole field stack (start 65 < bag 70 < party 75 < message 80 < yes/no 85 < battle 100 < fade 200); break-tested by restoring layer 50, which fails F.02.

**H1 — the data.** New `gen_wild_encounters.py` -> `data/land_encounters.json`, **192 maps**, every species resolved, idempotent. ⚠️ **A CORRECTION TO THIS ROADMAP'S OWN M27/M29 ROWS**: they describe Kanto's encounters as "imported (264 of 388 entries)", which reads as done — `wild_encounters.json` was the **RAW reference dump**, 1.0 MB, in the tree since M15 with **no consumer**. ⚠️ **SPECIES RESOLUTION IS IMPORTED FROM `gen_trainer_data.py`, NOT REIMPLEMENTED.** That module owns `normalize()`, the Nidoran gendered-form aliases and the collision assert, and `[M27B Step 4]` is the record of what a second spelling costs — `Nidoran-female`/`-male` both collapsed to `NIDORAN` and one silently overwrote the other, invisible until a Kanto roster used them.

**LAND ONLY, and the split is measured**: of the 7 corridor maps carrying any table, only **5 carry a LAND one** (Routes 1/2/3/22, Viridian Forest). **Pallet Town and Viridian City are WATER-ONLY** — surfing, M27E — so "has a table" would wrongly include them. ⚠️ This corrects `docs/m27_next_step_recon.md`'s own "7 of 32 baked maps carry a land encounter table", written before the split was measured.

**H2 — the roll**, ported from `WildEncounterCheck`. ⚠️ **THE x16 IS LOAD-BEARING**: `encounterRate *= 16` against a 2880 denominator, so Viridian Forest's table rate of 14 is **7.8% a step, not 0.5%** — dropping it makes grass feel dead rather than broken, which is the worse failure. **Lead-ability modifiers are part of that same function**, not a separate gate, so Illuminate/Arena Trap/No Guard double and White Smoke/Quick Feet halve; porting the roll without them ports half a function.

⚠️ **`MB_CAVE` IS A LAND-ENCOUNTER TILE AND A GRASS-ONLY READING MISSES IT.** Source's set is `IsEncounterTile && !IsSurfableWaterOrUnderwater` — 9 behaviours, not 1. The corridor has **46 cave cells** (Diglett's Cave North Entrance), inert only because that map carries no table; a grass-keyed trigger would silently never fire the moment B1F is baked.

⚠️ **THE 40% NEW-METATILE GATE APPLIES ONLY ON A CHANGE.** `AllowWildCheckOnNewMetatile` is consulted when the behaviour differs from the previous step, so path-to-grass pays it and grass-to-grass does not. Applying it every step is the plausible misreading and makes grass ~60% quieter than the reference. The previous tile's behaviour is therefore captured **BEFORE** the move — reading it after would compare a tile with itself and remove the gate entirely.

**H3 — the mount.** `_begin_battle` split into a shared `_mount_battle`, with `begin_wild_battle` alongside it. ⚠️ **THE EMPTY TRAINER KEY IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT A PLACEHOLDER**: `BattleOutcome.should_set_defeated_flag` already reads `trainer_key != ""`, and `[M27O O3]`'s prize money is 0 because `Cmd_getmoneyreward` only pays for a trainer — so both fall out of the empty key rather than needing a wild-specific branch. A later session must not "helpfully" give wild battles a synthetic key. Encounters fire LAST in the step callback (warp -> sight -> poison -> wild), matching `ProcessPlayerFieldInput`'s own order, and are additionally gated on the poison message box not being open.

⚠️ **REPEL AND THE POST-BATTLE IMMUNITY WINDOW ARE ABSENT BY ROB'S DECISION, 2026-08-03, AGAINST this session's own recommendation.** Recorded at the code site: a later session finding `RestartWildEncounterImmunitySteps` must NOT restore it without asking.

Its field effect is exactly this halving, so the behaviour is now correct and simply unreachable. Deliberately a LITERAL id rather than a new `AbilityManager.ABILITY_STENCH` constant: **adding that constant is what closing M17's last item looks like**, and doing it as a side effect of an encounter roll would quietly answer a question that is Rob's.

**[M27H H4/H5 — catching and fleeing] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. M27H IS CLOSED.**

⚠️ **A CORRECTION TO WHAT I TOLD ROB WHEN SCOPING THIS.** I said `attempt_catch` "exists and has never run outside a test", implying the mechanics were there. The SEAM was — signal, dispatch, call site — but the function itself was `return false  # M27 will replace this`. `docs/m26_b7_recon.md` §0.4 already recorded that ("an always-false stub"); I should have read it before characterising H4. **H4 was writing the formula, not wiring one.**

⚠️ **AND A CATCH WAS UNREACHABLE IN PLAY AT ALL** — the battle Item menu holds three hardcoded entries (Potion/Full Heal/X Attack) and **no Poké Ball**, so nothing could throw one. A fourth entry now exists, inheriting that list's own already-disclosed simplification (hardcoded, unlimited); real bag integration is M26E1/E2's.

**H4 — the formula**, ported from `ComputeCaptureOdds` (`battle_script_commands.c:9912`). ⚠️ **THE ORDER OF THE TERMS IS THE FORMULA** — every step is integer division, so it cannot be tidied algebraically. ⚠️ **THE BADGE MALUS IS A LOOP, NOT ONE MULTIPLY**: at `B_MISSING_BADGE_CATCH_MALUS = GEN_LATEST` it applies x4/5 **once per missing badge whose level threshold the target exceeds**, so 0 badges is strictly worse than 4, not equal.

**Two properties found by measurement when my own assertions failed, both real:** the Gen 9 low-level bonus `(36-2L)/10` is **exactly x1.0 at level 13** — 13, 14 and 20 all give the same odds (measured 15 each, against 24 at level 10), so 13 is its NEUTRAL BOUNDARY rather than its last helpful level; and **a catch rate of 1 gives odds of exactly 0 at any HP**, by integer truncation, i.e. mathematically uncatchable with a plain ball. Both are source behaviour; both had me asserting the opposite first.

⚠️ **THE SHAKE COUNT IS EMITTED NOW, FOR M26B7.** That recon flags `catch_attempted` as "carrying no shake count" and proposes adding a presentation seam for exactly this — emitting it here means the catch ANIMATION has nothing to retrofit. 0-2 is a break-free, 3 is a capture.

**A full party REFUSES BEFORE THE ROLL** (Rob's decision, 2026-08-03), so a ball never wobbles and then loses the Pokémon anyway. That pairing is what makes deferring the PC safe rather than merely convenient. ⚠️ **A CATCH IS NOT A WIN**: `CAUGHT` is its own outcome, in neither the win nor the defeat set — conflating it with WON would pay prize money and could set a defeated-trainer flag. The capture ends the battle **through the phase machine** (the wild side has nothing able to act) rather than via a new "battle over" flag, which is how source gets there too.

**H5 — fleeing**, from `TryRunFromBattle` (`battle_util.c:540`). ⚠️ **EQUAL SPEED SUCCEEDS** — the roll is gated on `speed < opponent`, so a naive `>` would make same-speed encounters roll for nothing. Slower rolls `(own*128)/theirs + run_tries*30` against 0-255, with `run_tries` accumulating so a slow Pokémon is never trapped indefinitely. Ghost-types always escape (`B_GHOSTS_ESCAPE >= GEN_6`, the same rule that already exempts them from trapping).

⚠️ **`[M25b]`'S RUN PLACEHOLDER COULD NOT SIMPLY BE REPOINTED, exactly as flagged when H5 was scoped**: one button serves both battle kinds, and running from a TRAINER is a forfeit that whites the player out while running from a wild Pokémon costs nothing. The wild branch is separate; the simulator's own scene-swap Run is untouched.

**Smoke Ball gets its first purpose.** `[M24b]` shipped it data-only with a note that no flee mechanic existed to consume it; this is that mechanic. ⚠️ **Run Away is the mirror case and is NOT implemented** — `[M17n-1]` gave it no constant at all on the grounds that it is "out of battle-engine scope", which was true then and is no longer: its real effect is guaranteed escape. Flagged, not fixed — an ability-roster decision like Stench's.

⚠️ **ONE DISCLOSED GAP: A FAILED ESCAPE DOES NOT SPEND THE TURN.** Source does. This project has no "do nothing" action to queue, so the player simply picks again — which makes fleeing free, only slower. Closing it needs a real skip-turn action in the TURN MACHINE, not in the escape code, which is why it is recorded rather than bodged.

**M27H IS CLOSED** — H1 through H5. ⚠️ **NOT screenshot-verified**: the formula and the round trip are driven by tests and the H1-H3 live drive, but a catch has not been watched on screen. It would show nothing anyway until **M26B7** builds the animation.

**[M27H H4 fix — caught Pokemon were unusable] COMPLETE — 2026-08-04.** Reported from play: "I can't use any Pokemon I catch — they join the party fainted."

⚠️ **THE CATCH ENDS THE BATTLE BY FAINTING THE OPPONENT, AND `caught_pokemon` IS THAT SAME OBJECT.** `BattlePokemon` is `RefCounted`, so there is no copy: the two lines that zero the opponent's HP to reach `_phase_battle_end_check`'s fully-fainted state zero the very Pokemon the player is about to receive. Source has no such collision — `FinalizeCapture` REMOVES the battler from the battle rather than fainting it, so nothing it hands back was ever marked dead. Fainting is this project's own documented stand-in for that removal, and the stand-in leaked into the prize.

**Fixed by recording `caught_hp` before the mutation and handing the Pokemon over through a new `take_caught_pokemon()`**, which undoes THREE things — only the first of which was obvious: the zeroed HP and `fainted` flag; the battle VOLATILES (confusion, a Substitute, a rampage lock); and the STAT STAGES. ⚠️ The last two matter because the caught mon is appended to the party AFTER `restore_party_after_battle` has already cleaned the player's own party, so nothing else would ever strip its battle state — a wild mon that used a boosting move would arrive permanently buffed. `status` and `toxic_counter` are deliberately KEPT: source's capture cures nothing, and a Pokemon caught asleep stays asleep.

**Tests**: `m27h_catching_test` 49 -> **53/53** (D.06-D.09). Nothing had ever asserted the caught Pokemon's own state — the suite checked that the outcome CARRIES a Pokemon, never that it was usable — which is exactly how this shipped. Three injections, all confirmed failing: handing back the raw field (D.06/D.07), healing instead of restoring (D.07/D.08, the discriminator that separates "restore" from "heal"), and skipping the volatile strip (D.09). Regression: all 20 overworld suites green, 5 battle suites plus both `--autoplay` runs green.

⚠️ **[M27H H4 follow-up — the fix above DID NOT REACH THE PLAYER, reported from play a second time] FIXED — 2026-08-04, same day.** Rob: *"Caught pokemon still enter my party as fainted, I can't use them at all because switch hides feinted pokemon."*

**The diagnosis and the mechanism were right; the fix was wired to nothing.** `take_caught_pokemon()` was built, tested and documented — and `battle_screen_shared.gd`'s own `caught_pokemon()` accessor, the ONE path the overworld actually calls (`overworld.gd:1532`), still read the raw `_bm.caught_pokemon` field. The doc comment directly above that line already said *"Goes through `take_caught_pokemon()`, NOT the raw `caught_pokemon` field"* — **the comment describing the fix was written and the call was not**, so the file asserted its own correctness while shipping the bug. One line: `return _bm.take_caught_pokemon() if _bm != null else null`.

⚠️ **AND THE SUITE PASSED 53/53 BOTH BEFORE AND AFTER, WHICH IS THE REAL LESSON.** D.06-D.09 call `take_caught_pokemon()` **directly**; E.05 only checked `bs.has_method("caught_pokemon")`. So the tests covered the function and the *existence* of the accessor, and nothing anywhere called the accessor — the exact seam between them was the only place the bug could live. This is rule (7) in a new dress: not a vacuous assertion, but a **complete guard on the wrong side of a one-line seam**. A guard that tests the callee instead of the call site cannot see a caller that never calls.

New E.05b-E.05e drive the real accessor on a bare `battle_screen_singles.tscn` instance with `_bm` assigned by hand (`@onready`, so null off-tree — the same shape E.04 already uses): the handed-over mon is alive at its real 7 HP, keeps its status (restore, never heal), has its stat stages stripped, and a null `_bm` still degrades to null. `m27h_catching_test` 53 -> **57/57**. Injection re-verified: restoring the raw-field read fails **E.05b and E.05d** and nothing else — E.05c/E.05e correctly stay green, since status and the null path are unaffected either way. `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 52 -> 56; Z.99 caught a miscount (I claimed 5 new assertions, wrote 4) on the first run, which is what that balance check is for.

Also fixed while here: the `[M27O O4]` doc block for `restore_party` had drifted above `caught_pokemon()`, so the file read as though the party-restore rationale belonged to the catch accessor. Moved back to its own function; no behaviour change.

Regression: all **20 overworld suites** green (`m27a` 514/514, `m27e_surf` 74/74, `m27h_catching` 57/57, `m27h_wild_encounters` 56/56, `m27l_save` 105/105, …), plus `m22_item_action_test` 67/67, `m23_6_battle_setup_test` 111/111, `m26_trainer_category_party_test` 130/130, `message_pacing_test` 67/67, `m26b_debug_log_test` 62/62, `m26_b3_6c_test` 168/168, `item_test` 77/77, `switch_test` 64/64, and both `battle_screen_*.tscn --autoplay` runs 1/1.

## M27E — Field moves & traversal *(absorbs the retired M32; scoped 2026-08-03)*

⚠️ **SCOPE OF RECORD FOR THE MEASUREMENTS IS THIS ENTRY.** Measured across all **421 imported Kanto maps**, not estimated: `MB_OCEAN_WATER` **31,501 cells / 64 maps**, `MB_SHALLOW_WATER` 747/16, `MB_POND_WATER` 635/11, `MB_FAST_WATER` 2,831/12, `MB_WATERFALL` **80/4**, `MB_ICE` 189/2, `MB_THIN_ICE` 9/1, `MB_NORTHWARD_CURRENT` 142/1, `SOUTHWARD` 45/1, `EASTWARD` 42/2, `WESTWARD` **0**, `MB_STRENGTH_BUTTON` 15/8. Object events: **55 cuttable trees / 24 maps**, **97 breakable rocks / 15**, **58 pushable boulders / 16**. ⚠️ **Surf is ~32,900 cells across ~70 maps and Waterfall is 80 across 4 — these are not comparable pieces of work**, and the block's sub-tiering should follow that rather than the alphabet.

⚠️ **DIVE HAS ZERO CELLS ON ZERO MAPS, AND IS IN SCOPE ANYWAY — Rob's call, 2026-08-03.** `MB_DEEP_WATER`, `MB_NO_SURFACING`, `MB_SEAWEED_NO_SURFACING` and `MB_INTERIOR_DEEP_WATER` are all **absent from the region**; Dive is Hoenn's HM08 and FRLG has none. I recommended cutting it and Rob kept it. `MB_MUDDY_SLOPE` is also 0 and is not in scope. **M27's own row says "surf/dive/waterfall", which is Hoenn's list, not Kanto's** — corrected here.

**More already exists than the row implies.** **Encounter content is fully imported**: of Kanto's 262 encounter maps, **water_mons on 98, fishing_mons on 98, rock_smash_mons on 29** — Surf/fishing/smash need TRIGGERING, not data. **All art is pulled** by `[M27D D1]` without anyone noting it: `misc_surf_blob`, `people_leaf_green_surf_run`, `people_red_red_surf_run`, `people_red_red_fish`, plus `cut_grass`, `splash`, `ripple`, `bubbles`, `water_surfacing` and `field_move_streaks`. **HM items are in the roster.** `StepResolver` already reads `behavior_at` for directional blocking, so Surf is a new rule set on a built surface, not new infrastructure.

⚠️ **FLASH NEEDS AN IMPORTER CHANGE, AND IS THE ONLY PIECE THAT DOES.** `requires_flash` is **not imported at all** — `assets/maps/*.json` carries `name/layout/width/height/atlas/events/connections/border_*` and no map type. Only **2 Kanto maps** need it (`RockTunnel_1F_Frlg`, `RockTunnel_B1F_Frlg`); the other 7 in the reference are Hoenn. Fixing it means a `gen_map_import.py` change plus a full `assets/maps/` regeneration, which lands on the already-open 8.8 MB tracked-size question.

**Proposed sub-tiers, in dependency order**: **E1 Surf** (the block, effectively — water state, second collision rule set, mount/dismount, the blob, water encounters) · **E2 Cut / Rock Smash / Strength** (one shared shape, "an ability used on an object event", 210 objects over 55 maps) · **E3 Waterfall / currents / ice** (forced and directed movement) · **E4 Fishing** · **E5 Flash** (behind the importer change) · **E6 Dive** (no site in Kanto).

**[M27E E0 — the field-move gate] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03.**

⚠️ **THE BADGE IS THE WHOLE GATE. NO PARTY MEMBER NEEDS TO KNOW THE MOVE — Rob's design call, 2026-08-03.** Hold the Cascade Badge, face a cuttable tree, press A, and the tree comes down whether or not anything you own has ever heard of Cut. **This is a deliberate, large divergence from source**, and what it deletes is listed at the class so a later session reading `party_menu.c` does not restore it as missing work: `SetUpFieldMove_*`, the party-menu entry point, the "Which POKéMON?" selection, the per-Pokémon usability scan, and the `{STR_VAR_1} used CUT!` message that names the user. **None of it is unfinished — it is designed out.** Rob's framing: *keep the overworld behaviours, separate them from the actual moves.* A.02 pins the whole design in one assertion — a store holding one badge and **no party at all** can cut.

⚠️ **THE MAPPING IS SERIES CANON, AUTHORED HERE, AND COULD NOT HAVE BEEN PORTED.** Checked directly: **the reference has no badge→HM field gating in C anywhere.** `FLAG_BADGE0*_GET` appears only in `event_data.c`'s `gBadgeFlags` table and `battle_util.c`'s obedience ladder, and the Route 22/23 `BoulderBadgeGuard` scripts are map CHECKPOINTS, not field-move gates. So the eight pairs are written from the games' canon rather than lifted from source — stated plainly, because an audit against the reference will find no counterpart and should not read that as drift. **Rob expects to edit it**, so it is one flat dictionary nothing reads any other way. The badge ORDER *is* ported and verified from each Kanto gym's own script (Pewter 01 · Cerulean 02 · Vermilion 03 · Celadon 04 · Fuchsia 05 · Saffron 06 · Cinnabar 07 · Viridian 08). **Dive's entry is the one invention**: it takes the Earth Badge, which canon leaves free because the eighth governs obedience rather than a field move.

**The flourish is generic, on Rob's call**: source prints `{STR_VAR_1} used CUT!` and there is no such Pokémon here, so naming one would put a lie in the message box. C.02 pins that no placeholder survives. The refusal deliberately **does not name the badge or the gym** — a hint source never gives, which would flatten the region's own progression (C.05).

⚠️ **A BADGE-ONLY GATE MEANS AN EMPTY PARTY CAN STILL ACT**, and since `[M27L L5]` an empty party is a real starting state. Academic in play — you cannot win a gym with nothing — but reachable, so B.08 pins it rather than leaving it to surprise someone. **HM handouts are unchanged for now and will likely be relocated later** (Rob, 2026-08-03); HM items keep their battle role and lose their field one.

**Tests**: new `m27e_field_moves_test` **21/21**. Four guards break-tested: any-badge-unlocks-all fails A.03, a shared badge fails 5 (A.02/A.04/B.02/B.03/B.04), a restored `{STR_VAR_1}` fails C.02, naming the badge in the refusal fails C.05.

**[M27E E1a — surfing: the traversal rules] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03.**

⚠️ **SURFING INVERTS THE COLLISION RULE RATHER THAN RELAXING IT.** On foot, water stops you because its collision bit is set — so surfing cannot simply *ignore* collision, or the blob would ride through walls. It makes **SURFABLE tiles passable despite the bit** and leaves everything else exactly as strict. B.04/B.05 are the pair that pin this: a wall (land WITH a collision bit) stays impassable while surfing, and the real `no_collision` debug toggle passes it — so the two are demonstrably different mechanisms. Break-tested: degrading surfing to `no_collision` fails B.04.

⚠️ **DISMOUNTING NEEDS NO RULE, AND THERE DELIBERATELY ISN'T ONE.** Because only *surfable* tiles get the override, the only non-water tile reachable from the water is one that was already walkable — which is the shore. B.03 asserts stepping ashore works with nothing written for it.

⚠️ **`MB_SHALLOW_WATER` IS NOT SURFABLE, AND IT LOOKS LIKE AN OMISSION.** Source is explicit — it carries no `TILE_FLAG_SURFABLE` in `sTileBitAttributes` (`metatile_behavior.c:25+`) because it is water you **wade** through on foot. Adding it "for consistency" would let the player mount a blob in a puddle and would put 747 already-walkable cells across 16 Kanto maps at risk. The 17-entry set is **extracted verbatim from source rather than reasoned from the names**, which is what caught this. Break-tested: adding shallow water fails C.03 and C.05.

**Of the 17, Kanto uses 8**: OCEAN_WATER (31,501 cells), FAST_WATER (2,831), CYCLING_ROAD_WATER (751), POND_WATER (635), the three used currents (229 between them) and WATERFALL (80). The other 9 are Hoenn's — including `MB_DEEP_WATER`/`MB_NO_SURFACING`/`MB_SEAWEED*`, the same absence that leaves Dive siteless.

`surfing` defaults **off** (A.03) — break-tested, since defaulting on would make every existing map swimmable the moment it shipped.

**Also this session, Rob's call**: the field-move flourish is now **`{PLAYER} used CUT!`** rather than the first draft's subjectless *"Used CUT!"*. Source names the Pokémon (`{STR_VAR_1}`); the badge-only gate leaves no such Pokémon, so the subject moves to the only actor left. Returned **unexpanded** and expanded at print time like every other message, so a rename takes effect immediately (C.02b).

**Tests**: new `m27e_surf_test` **16/16**; `m27e_field_moves_test` 21 → **22/22**. Three guards break-tested. Regression: `m27a_step_resolver` 514/514 green, which matters most here — this changed `resolve()` itself.

⚠️ **WHAT REMAINS OF E1, AND IT IS THE MAJORITY OF THE PLAYER-FACING WORK**: the mount/dismount interaction (facing water + A + the Soul Badge, with source's own "Would you like to SURF?" prompt), the surf blob sprite and the player's `surf_run` frames (both pulled, neither wired), water wild encounters (data already imported — `water_mons` on 98 Kanto maps), and the state actually living on the overworld rather than only on the resolver. **E1a is the RULES only** — nothing in play can surf yet.

**[M27E E1b — mounting and dismounting] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. ⚠️ NOT LIVE-DRIVEN; see the caveat at the end of this entry.**

⚠️ **THE SURF STATE IS ON `OverworldSession`, AND THAT ONE IS NOT OPTIONAL.** A wild encounter can start while surfing — `water_mons` is imported for **98 Kanto maps** — and a battle is a real scene swap. Held on the overworld, the flag would clear the first time something attacked, dumping the player **onto water on foot, on a tile the step resolver refuses in every direction**. That is the same soft-lock `[M27C]`'s `start_cell` guard exists to prevent, arrived at from a new angle. `_resolver.surfing` is therefore pushed on **every boot**, not just on a mount, so a battle return or a loaded save arrives still on the water.

⚠️ **THE SURF CHECK RUNS BEFORE `Interaction.resolve`, AND HAS TO.** Water carries no script and no entity, so `resolve` returns empty and the press would be eaten with nothing to show. Source reaches surfing through the ordinary interact path too (`EventScript_UseSurf`), not a dedicated key.

**`can_mount` returns a REASON, not a bool** — four independent refusals (already surfing / not water / no badge / OK), because *"you cannot surf here"* and *"you cannot surf yet"* are different sentences and collapsing them would make the caller re-derive which to print. It is **pure and takes the faced behaviour** rather than reaching into `MapManager`, so it is testable without standing up a map. ⚠️ **Only NO_BADGE speaks**; facing land or already surfing fall through silently, or every press at a wall would answer a question about surfing.

⚠️ **SHALLOW WATER OFFERS NO PROMPT** (D.06) — it is walkable, so a prompt there would offer to surf onto a tile you can already stand on. This is `[E1a]`'s non-surfable finding paying off a second time.

**Dismount is decided by where you LANDED, not by a key** — source has no "get off". Since `[E1a]` only lets a surfing player reach a tile that was already walkable, *"landed somewhere unsurfable"* **is** *"landed ashore"*. Checked on the INPUT step path only; scripted `applymovement` is an authored cutscene and source's own forced walks off water do their own dismount.

**Text is verbatim** from `data/text/surf.inc` — including the first line of the prompt (*"The water is dyed a deep blue…"*), which reads like scene-setting and is easy to drop. `Text_CurrentTooFast` is kept unused against E3's currents (`MB_FAST_WATER` is 2,831 cells over 12 Kanto maps). The ride message is **`{PLAYER} used SURF!`**, expanded through `TextBuffers` rather than `_expanded_pages()` — that one reads the VM's pending pages and there is no VM behind this.

**Tests**: `m27e_surf_test` 16 → **28/28**. ⚠️ A parse error in `overworld.gd` (an untyped `Vector2i` inference, this project's own recorded GDScript gotcha) took `m27a_step_resolver` to 461/462 and `m27l_save_test` to 92/93 with their totals DROPPING — the aborted-function signature. Caught by the sweep, fixed; both back to **514/514** and **105/105**.

⚠️ **THE WIRING IS NOT LIVE-DRIVEN, AND THIS IS THE ONE REAL GAP.** The suites cover the pure decision logic — `can_mount`, `should_dismount`, the behaviour set, the resolver rules — but the end-to-end path (face water → press A → prompt → YES → mount → ride → step ashore → dismount) has **never been run in the real scene**. Every previous tier this session that skipped a live drive had a defect the suites could not see: K-b's keyboard was unreachable from the keyboard, and L4's boot path needed three driver rewrites. **Assume the same here until it is driven.** That is the first thing the next session should do, before any new E-tier.

**[M27E E1c — the blob, the surf sprite, and the ride out] COMPLETE — 2026-08-04. ⚠️ ALSO NOT LIVE-DRIVEN — E1b's caveat above now covers both tiers; Rob is driving them together.**

⚠️ **THE BLOB SHEET IS THE FIELD-EFFECT ONE, AND THE OBVIOUS PICK IS THE WRONG ONE.** `[M27D D1]` pulled both: `object_events/misc_surf_blob.png` (192x32, 6 frames) and `field_effects/surf_blob.png` (96x32, 3 frames). The blob is a FIELD EFFECT — `FLDEFF_SURF_BLOB`, template `gFieldEffectObjectTemplate_SurfBlob` (`field_effect_objects.h:249`) — whose pic table names the 3-frame one. The 6-frame sheet's own symbol, `gObjectEventPic_SurfBlob`, has **no graphics-info consumer anywhere in source** (grepped): an unused leftover. It is bigger, so it looks like the richer asset, and it is inert. Recorded at the constant so a later session does not "upgrade" to it.

**Three frames, not four**: SOUTH/NORTH/WEST, with `sSurfBlobAnim_FaceEast` being `ANIMCMD_FRAME(2, .hFlip = TRUE)` — the same mirrored-EAST convention the people sheets already use, so it needed no new mechanism.

⚠️ **THE PLAYER'S SURF SHEET HAS 14 FRAMES AND SOURCE USES THREE OF THEM.** `people_leaf_green_surf_run.png` is 224x32, and `ObjectEventGraphics` derived `frames` from sheet width, so the table said 14. But `sPicTable_GreenSurf` (`object_event_pic_tables.h:1449`) is twelve entries that reference **only raw frames 0/1/2** — every logical walking frame maps back onto the three facing poses, i.e. **surfing shows no walk cycle at all in source**; the sheet's frames 3+ are FRLG's unrelated RUN art. Left at 14, `WalkAnim` would have indexed those run frames as the surf walk cycle. New `FRAME_OVERRIDES` in `gen_object_event_sprites.py` emits **3** for both surf ids, which is the honest port of the pic table AND makes `WalkAnim.animates()` false — reproducing the facing-only behaviour through the existing rest path with **zero special-casing in the anim code**. Break-tested: restoring 14 fails F.01/F.03.

**The bob is ported whole** from `UpdateBobbingEffect` (`field_effect_helpers.c:1310`): `y2 += velocity` every 4th frame, velocity flipping every 16th — a 0..-4px triangle on a 32-frame period. ⚠️ **The step uses the OLD velocity on frame 16 and flips after**, which is what keeps it a clean triangle rather than overshooting. Near a shore the interval mask goes 3 -> 7 (source's own `intervals[] = {0x3, 0x7}`, selected by `SynchronizeSurfPosition`'s scan for an `ELEVATION_DEFAULT` neighbour), which is **slower AND shallower** — -2 at frame 16, not -4. That halving is the discriminator E.09 asserts; a mask that never changed would read -4.

⚠️ **BOB_PLAYER_AND_MON: THE PLAYER RIDES THE SAME OFFSET.** Source assigns `playerSprite->y2 = sprite->y2` — both sprites bob in lockstep, and that shared offset is the entire reason it reads as *riding* rather than two sprites that happen to overlap. Reproduced by the blob driving the body sprite alongside itself. Break-tested: dropping it fails E.10 and nothing else, so the guard is specific.

⚠️ **MOUNTING RIDES OUT ONTO THE WATER — IT IS NOT JUST A FLAG AND A SPRITE.** Source's `EventScript_UseSurf` jumps the player onto the water tile as part of the mount; without the step the blob appears under a player still standing on the shore. Routed through the ordinary `_try_step`, so it is a real step (sight checks, step scripts) exactly as source's jump is. **Disclosed divergence**: a glide, not the jump arc — this project has no jump animation. `_mount_surf` was split into the awaiting shell and a synchronous `_ride_out()` purely so the effect is testable without stepping through a yes/no prompt.

⚠️ **THE DISMOUNT IS DEFERRED TO THE END OF THE STEP, AND THE ANIM KEYS ON THE BLOB RATHER THAN THE FLAG.** `_try_step` flips `surfing` at step START (it updates `_cell` before the tween runs), so removing the blob there sinks it under a player still visibly mid-water. Held until the tween lands. That leaves a real window where the flag is false while the surf texture is still on the sprite — so `_player_graphics_id()` keys on the blob's presence, not the session flag; keying on the flag would run walk-cycle indices against the surf sheet's run frames during the glide. Break-tested separately: dropping the defer fails F.07/F.08, keying on the flag fails F.08 alone.

**Rebuild-safe**: `_build_player_node` calls `_update_surf_visuals()`, the same reason `_ready` re-pushes the resolver flag — a battle return or a loaded save rebuilds the player with `surfing` already true, and without it they arrive standing on water in walking clothes.

⚠️ **A VACUOUS GUARD, CAUGHT ONLY BY INJECTION — the same rule-(7) trap this file keeps recording.** The first cut of this suite went 52/52 on its first run, and deleting the mount's own `_try_step` left it **fully green** while stranding the player ashore. Six other injections all failed correctly; that one proved the suite had no opinion about the single most visible thing the tier does. New Section G drives `_ride_out` against a REAL `MapManager` holding a synthetic land/water/water strip and asserts the player ends on the WATER cell. Re-injected: fails G.03, exactly one assertion.

**[Debug boot, same session]** `overworld.tscn`'s F6 boot moved to `PalletTown_Frlg (7, 16)` — a real beach tile facing open ocean — with two new gated exports, `debug_flags` (currently the Soul Badge, since E0 gates Surf on `FLAG_BADGE05_GET` and the 32-map slice stops at Pewter/badge 01) and `debug_party`. Both apply ONLY on a fresh F6 boot: a new game sets `pending_new_game`, and a battle return or CONTINUE arrives with a non-empty `resume`, so neither can inherit scaffolding. ⚠️ **`debug_party` exists because `[M27L L5]` left the debug boot with NO PARTY AT ALL** — L5 correctly retired the lazily-built team as the default, but the debug boot never runs Oak's script, so it inherited an empty party and nothing put one back. Reported from play as a black-screen hang on a wild encounter; see the flagged defect below. Four `m27a_step_resolver_test` fixtures that set `start_map` explicitly but left `start_cell` implicit needed the same treatment — they had been relying on the scene default being out of bounds in Pallet and falling back to `_first_walkable`, and a real beach tile broke AO.02 (whose southern neighbour is now ocean, so the ELEVATION check answered before the object-event one). The identical lesson `[M27F]` already recorded for `start_map`, one level deeper.

**[M27E E1d — the shoreline is an elevation change] COMPLETE — 2026-08-04.** Fixes defect (1) below, which E1c flagged and could not have found from a fixture.

⚠️ **THE ELEVATION MISMATCH IS THE MECHANISM, NOT AN OBSTACLE — the framing E1c reported was exactly backwards.** Measured: all 66 surfable cells in the corridor are elevation 1, land is 3, and the shoreline carries no transition tiles, so EVERY crossing in either direction is a mismatch by the ordinary rule. Source leans on precisely that. The mount's own precondition is the mismatch read one way (`IsPlayerFacingSurfableFishableWater`, `field_player_avatar.c:1645` — mismatch AND player at ELEVATION_DEFAULT AND faced tile surfable), and the dismount is the same mismatch read the other (`CheckForObjectEventCollision:966` converts `COLLISION_ELEVATION_MISMATCH` into `COLLISION_STOP_SURFING` when `CanStopSurfing:1000` agrees: surfing, DESTINATION at elevation 3, nobody standing on it). New `Outcome.STOP_SURFING`, appended so serialised ints do not shift, returned from one `_mismatch()` helper covering both call sites — the destination being otherwise enterable is guaranteed by position, since a mismatch is only ever reported for a cell whose collision and directional rules already passed.

⚠️ **AND THE MOUNT IS A JUMP, WHICH IS WHY IT MUST NOT GO THROUGH THE RESOLVER.** The ordinary rules refuse land -> water and source refuses it too; `SurfFieldEffect_JumpOnSurfBlob` (`src/field_effect.c`) issues `GetJumpSpecialMovementAction`, a held movement bypassing collision and elevation exactly as this project's scripted `applymovement` already does. E1c routed the mount through `_try_step`, which passed a flat-elevation suite and could never move the player on a real map. New `_jump_onto_water()`; it deliberately skips the step-completion scripts, since you land on water where source has none.

⚠️ **A SECOND BUG THE SAME DRIVE EXPOSED: E1c's DEFERRED DISMOUNT WAS UNREACHABLE IN PLAY.** `_update_surf_visuals` holds the blob until the step lands by testing `_moving`, and that assignment sat BELOW the dismount check — so the flag was still false, the blob came off at step start, and only E1c's own F.07 (which set `_moving` by hand) ever exercised the deferral. `_moving = true` moved above the check.

**Tests**: `m27e_surf_test` 56 -> **66/66**. New Section H drives the shoreline rule on REAL elevations (land 3 / water 1): the mount step still refused, water-to-water ordinary, water-to-land STOP_SURFING **and allowed**, plus the three discriminators `CanStopSurfing` actually turns on — not while on foot, not onto elevation 4, not onto an occupied tile — and H.01 pins the fixture against the baked Pallet artifact so it cannot drift into fiction. Section G's own fixture was rebuilt with real elevations, which is what makes G.03/G.05 meaningful. Five injections, all confirmed to fail: no STOP_SURFING (3), ungated by surfing (1), any-elevation landing (2), mount back through `_try_step` (3), `_moving` reordered (1).

⚠️ **G.06 WAS VACUOUS ON ITS FIRST WRITING AND ONLY INJECTION SHOWED IT.** The mount jump leaves `_moving` true — its tween never completes on an off-tree node — so the deferral fired for that reason rather than the one under test, and reintroducing the ordering bug left the suite GREEN. Fixed by clearing `_moving` first, which is the only starting state that can tell the two orderings apart. Third time this arc; the preventive form remains rule (7)'s: write the injection before the assertion.

**[M27E follow-up — the empty-party black screen] FIXED — 2026-08-04.** Closes the defect E1c flagged and E1d left open.

`begin_wild_battle` guarded the OPPONENT party and nothing guarded the PLAYER's, so it returned **true** with a 0-member party, the overlay mounted, and `BattleManager` threw `Out of bounds get index '0'` on the active slot followed by a cascade of `Invalid access ... on a base object of type 'Nil'` — an unrecoverable black screen, since the field is left behind an overlay that never becomes usable. Reported from play on a wild encounter; reproduced headlessly.

⚠️ **THE GUARD IS ORIGINAL DESIGN, NOT A PORT, AND THE SEARCH THAT ESTABLISHED THAT IS THE USEFUL PART.** Source has no equivalent: `WildEncounterCheck` reads `gParties[B_TRAINER_PLAYER][0]` unconditionally (`wild_encounter.c:349`) because the state is unreachable there — Oak blocks Route 1 until you hold a starter, and a wipe always whites you out. It is reachable HERE (a debug boot has no party since `[M27L L5]`; a new game has none until Oak's script runs), so "source does not guard this" is a fact about source's own guarantees, not a licence to leave it unguarded.

**Fixed in two places, deliberately.** `_mount_battle` refuses at the very top — before `pending_trainer_key`, `_in_battle` or `battle_starting` — which makes a black screen structurally impossible for EVERY battle path, trainer and scripted included, not just wild. `_wild_step` additionally refuses before the roll, so a battle that cannot happen does not consume RNG or read the lead's ability. The mount guard is the safety net; the encounter guard is politeness, and it is only safe to be politeness because the net is there. `push_warning`, not `push_error` — this is a handled degrade, and `run_overworld_tests.sh` fails a run on ERROR lines, so a test exercising the guard would otherwise fail the whole suite.

**`_party_can_battle()` answers two states with different origins**: an EMPTY party, and an ALL-FAINTED one (unreachable in correct play, but a guard that trusted that would be trusting exactly what already broke). `is_fully_fainted()` covers both — its loop is vacuously true when empty — but relying on that silently would be too clever, so the empty case is tested explicitly.

⚠️ **A DISCLOSED CONSEQUENCE**: a trainer who spots you with no usable party now walks over and nothing happens. That is strictly better than a black screen and only reachable in a state correct play cannot produce, but it is a visible dead end rather than a graceful one — worth revisiting if a real path to an empty party ever ships.

**Tests**: `m27h_wild_encounters_test` 49 -> **53/53**. E.14/E.15 are the defect itself (a REAL opponent still refused, and nothing mounted); E.16 is the discriminator that separates "refuses when it should" from "refuses always"; E.17 pins the all-fainted case. Placed adjacent to the pre-existing E.13 (which guards the OPPONENT party) so the asymmetry that caused this cannot quietly return. Three injections, all confirmed failing: removing the mount guard (E.14/E.15), narrowing it to empty-only (E.17), and a blanket refusal (E.16). Verified end to end off-tree: the exact scenario that black-screened returns false with nothing mounted, a debug party is accepted, an all-fainted one is not.

⚠️ **NOT separately guarded**: `_wild_step`'s early return shares `_party_can_battle()` with the mount guard, so removing it alone fails nothing — it is an optimisation over a covered path, not an independent claim, and is recorded as such rather than given a test that would only restate E.16.

**Tests**: `m27e_surf_test` 28 -> **56/56**, with all seven guards break-tested (wrong sheet -> E.01; frame count -> F.01/F.03; body lockstep -> E.10; shore mask -> E.09; draw order -> E.11/F.05; deferred dismount -> F.07/F.08; ride-out -> G.03). Regression: **all 20 overworld suites green through `run_overworld_tests.sh`** (which fails on engine ERROR lines), `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32 reproducible — the sprite generator re-ran with zero asset churn, only the two frame counts in the generated table.

**[M27E E1e + H5 fix — three findings from Rob's live playthrough] COMPLETE — 2026-08-04.** The first real drive of the surf work, and it found one art error and two genuine bugs.

**1. THE BLOB WAS EMERALD'S, NOT KANTO'S — Rob's call, and he was right.** Two blob sheets exist and are visibly different designs: `field_effects/surf_blob.png` (96x32, 3 frames, dark navy with a heavy black outline) and `object_events/misc_surf_blob.png` (192x32, 6 frames, lighter purple with a pale blue crest and foam). E1c chose the first because it is the one `gFieldEffectObjectTemplate_SurfBlob` actually uses — correct as a PORT, wrong for a Kanto project. ⚠️ **Consequence worth stating: the Kanto sheet has NO anim table in source** (`gObjectEventPic_SurfBlob` is referenced by nothing outside its own INCGFX), so the frame mapping is now READ OFF THE ART rather than ported — six frames as three facings x two bob frames, (0,1) SOUTH / (2,3) NORTH / (4,5) WEST, EAST mirrored. **If a facing looks wrong on screen, that table is the first suspect.** The spare bob frames are deliberately unused: source's blob bobs by y-offset alone, which is already ported, and animating the sprite too would invent a second motion on top of a real one.

**2. THE MOUNT JUMP IS NOW A REAL ARC.** E1d disclosed this as missing ("a glide, not the jump arc"). `sJumpY_High` ported verbatim (`event_object_movement.c:10876`) — source's mount is `InitJumpSpecial` -> `InitJump(..., JUMP_DISTANCE_NORMAL, JUMP_TYPE_HIGH)`, so the HIGH table, 16 frames peaking at -12px. Ported rather than fitted, matching this project's practice for source tables. ⚠️ **The arc drives the BODY SPRITE, not the player node**, because the blob is a CHILD of the player and arcing the node would hop the water along with you — source creates the blob at the DESTINATION while the player arcs onto it. New `_mount_jump_active` holds the blob back until landing, the mirror of `_surf_exit_pending` on the dismount side, so the two never fight over `position.y`. `_finish_mount_jump()` is split out of the tween callback so the landing is reachable off-tree, where `create_tween()` cannot run. Verified live: peak exactly -12px, blob absent during flight, present after, body settling on its captured base.

⚠️ **THE ARC TABLE NOW EXISTS TWICE, AND THIS COPY IS THE ONE TO RETIRE — flagged 2026-08-09, not fixed.** `[M27R Step 1]` needed the same `sJumpY_High` for the movement-action jump family (`jump_2_down` and the 11 siblings, 56 region-wide uses) and ported it again as `MovementRunner.JUMP_Y_HIGH`, alongside `JUMP_Y_NORMAL` which this surf path never needed. So the sixteen numbers are hand-kept in two places: inlined here in `overworld.gd`'s mount/dismount arc, and as a real constant on the runner.

**That is the two-hand-kept-copies shape this project has already paid for once** — `check_bake_diff`'s own normalisation rules drifted from `map_baker`'s and produced a permanent false positive, and the fix was to make one the authority. Same call here: the surf path should read `MovementRunner.JUMP_Y_HIGH` rather than its own literal. Deliberately NOT done in the same session that introduced the second copy — the surf arc is live-verified behaviour (peak exactly -12px) and repointing it is a change to working code for tidiness, which belongs in its own pass with its own regression run rather than riding on an unrelated tier. **Whoever next touches either arc should collapse them.**

**3. RUNNING FROM A WILD BATTLE WHITED THE PLAYER OUT — and the cause was two bugs cancelling into a constant.** `try_flee` refuses outright unless `is_wild_battle` (`battle_manager.gd:411`), so Run never rolled; it fell through to FORFEITED, which `IsPlayerDefeated` counts as a defeat, which whites you out and charges the payout. ⚠️ **`is_wild_battle` was derived from two signals BOTH read after `BattleSetupContext.clear()` had already reset them**: `opp_trainer_key == ""` was therefore `"" == ""`, vacuously TRUE for every battle including trainer ones, while `OverworldSession.has_pending_return()` was vacuously false — because the overlay design deliberately saves no position ("the overworld STAYS ALIVE underneath"), a signal that stopped existing while still compiling. **Two errors that multiplied to a constant `false`, which is why no battle was ever wild and why fixing only one half changed nothing** — the first fix attempt did exactly that and moved nothing. Now captured BEFORE the clear, alongside every other context field, with a new explicit `BattleSetupContext.is_overworld_battle` the overworld sets on its own battles. `overlay_mode` cannot stand in: the overworld assigns it AFTER `add_child()`, and `add_child` is what fires `_ready`.

**Tests**: `m27e_surf_test` 66 -> **67/67** (the Kanto sheet, the paired-frame facings, and the jump's own blob timing split into G.04/G.04b); `m27h_wild_encounters_test` 53 -> **56/56** (E.18-E.20, including the discriminator that separates "marks overworld battles" from "is simply always true" — the exact shape of the bug being replaced). Three injections, all confirmed failing: attaching the blob during the jump (G.04), reverting to the Emerald sheet (E.01), and dropping the overworld's own marker (E.18). Regression: all 20 overworld suites green, 8 battle-side suites green (`battle_screen_shared.gd` and `BattleSetupContext` are shared with the simulator), both `--autoplay` runs 1/1, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

⚠️ **STILL NOT DRIVEN: the jump and the Kanto blob have been verified by probe and by test, not watched on screen.** The frame mapping in particular is inferred from the art.

**[M27E E1f — four findings from the second live playthrough] COMPLETE — 2026-08-04.**

**1. The used-SURF message needed a keypress.** New optional `auto_close_after` on `MessageBox.open`, used for field-move announcements only. ⚠️ A deliberate divergence from source, which waits for a press on essentially every message (`msgbox` defaults to MSGBOX_DEFAULT) — Rob's call: an announcement is not a conversation, and requiring a dismissal puts a keypress between the player and the thing they just asked for. Guarded on the page set being unchanged so a reopened box cannot be shut by a stale timer.

**2. THE RIDER SAT 8px TOO LOW — and the figure is DERIVED, not tuned by eye.** Rob asked whether a sheet exists that shows this correctly. It does: FRLG ships `green_surf.png`, a **32x32, 9-frame COMBINED sprite** with character and blob drawn as one piece — unreferenced in source, exactly like the Kanto blob, because the expansion uses Emerald's split architecture. Measured against it: Leaf's hat crown sits at y4 in the combined art and y12 on the standalone 16x32 sheet this project draws, so the rider was **exactly 8px low**. ⚠️ **The combined sprite is deliberately NOT used instead**, even though it would fix the height for free — it cannot be taken apart, and finding #4 requires the blob to stay on the water while the player leaves it.

**3. No jump when ending surf.** `Task_StopSurfingInit` (`field_player_avatar.c:1997`) issues the SAME `GetJumpSpecialMovementAction` the mount does, so the arc is now shared by both via `_add_jump_arc`.

**4. THE BLOB FOLLOWED THE PLAYER ASHORE — and source says exactly why it should not.** `UpdateBobbingEffect` runs `sprite->x = playerSprite->x` **only inside `if (bobState != BOB_JUST_MON)`**, and the dismount's first act is `SetSurfBlob_BobState(..., BOB_JUST_MON)`. So the blob keeps bobbing where it is while the player jumps off, and is destroyed only when the jump LANDS (`Task_WaitStopSurfing`). Ported as `SurfBlob.stay_behind()`. ⚠️ **`top_level` alone is not enough and getting it wrong teleports the blob**: it makes `position` mean GLOBAL while the bob keeps writing a LOCAL base, so the first frame after the switch snaps the blob toward the world origin. Rebased so the wave continues from where it is.

⚠️ **A VACUOUS GUARD THAT TOOK THREE ATTEMPTS, AND THE THIRD IS THE LESSON.** The rebase above was injected three times before a test caught it. First an x-only assertion (the teleport is vertical). Then x+y — still green, because **section G's chunk sits at origin (0, 0), so local and global Y are EQUAL there and the correct and broken implementations agree**. Rule (13) in a new dress: a fixture where two competing rules coincide cannot tell them apart, and this one coincided by virtue of its own map origin. Pinned by E.15/E.16 on a rider whose parent is deliberately far from the origin; G.10 now states plainly that it cannot see the vertical half.

**Tests**: `m27e_surf_test` 67 -> **74/74**. Four injections, all confirmed failing: no rider lift (G.08), blob keeps following (G.09/G.12), no rebase (E.15), and the earlier three. ⚠️ **A parse error in the first cut hung the whole run rather than failing it** — `ow` is untyped, so `:=` could not infer through it, the script failed to load, nothing called `quit()`, and the suite timed out instead of reporting. This project's own documented GDScript gotcha; explicit types now, with the failure mode recorded at the site. Regression: all 20 overworld suites green, message-box consumers green, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

⚠️ **NOT DRIVEN: none of these four have been watched on screen.** The rider lift is derived from art measurement, and the dismount jump and blob freeze are verified by probe and test only.

**[M27E E2 — the run mechanic] COMPLETE — 2026-08-04.** Rob's side project: hold SHIFT and the player runs at double speed with source's own run animation.

⚠️ **THE RUN FRAMES DID NOT EXIST IN THIS PROJECT, AND THE REASON IS A GENERATOR GAP, NOT MISSING ART.** `sPicTable_GreenNormal` spans **two pic symbols** — 9 walking frames from `leaf/green_normal.png` followed by **11 run frames living inside `leaf/green_surf_run.png`** (indices 9-19 -> that file's raw frames 3-13; its frames 0-2 are the SURF poses). `gen_object_event_sprites.py` resolved each id through `first = re.search(r"\((gObjectEventPic_\w+)", ...)` — the FIRST symbol only — which is right for 384 ids and silently truncates these. So the art was pulled by `[M27D D1]` and half of it was unreachable.

**The generator now composites entry-by-entry.** ⚠️ **THE TABLE IS AN ORDER, NOT A CONCATENATION** — GreenNormal's second half starts at frame 3, and `sPicTable_OldMan2` both REPEATS and REORDERS (0,1,2,0,0,1,1,2,2 then one OldWoman frame), so appending whole files would be wrong for both. Verified pixel-for-pixel: walk frames 0-8 match `green_normal.png`, run frames 9-19 match `green_surf_run.png` frames 3-13, and all three run frames per facing are distinct real art.

⚠️ **A STEP-0 MISS OF MY OWN, CAUGHT BY THE OUTPUT: I MEASURED ONE FILE AND GENERALISED.** I reported "exactly 3 multi-symbol tables" from `object_event_pic_tables.h` alone; the generator reads **four** files, and the berry-tree tables are multi-symbol too. The first regeneration silently rewrote `berry_trees_dirt_pile.png` from 16x16 to a 144x16 composite. Rule (6) in a new dress — a measurement scoped to one file reported as a fact about the source.
