**That accident surfaced a REAL hazard and the guard is the valuable part.** A berry tree draws from a 16x16 dirt pile, a 32x16 sprout AND a 96x32 tree, while its declared frame size clamps to the FIRST of those — so compositing it would have cropped every grown stage to its top-left 16x16 corner and looked entirely plausible in a directory listing. Compositing is now refused unless **every** source agrees on the declared frame geometry, and the refusals are **REPORTED** (`mixed geom` in the run output) rather than silently dropped. Net: **3 ids composited** (both player normals + OldMan2), **3 refused and named** (the berry trees). ⚠️ **The berry-tree refusal is a real, still-open finding, not a fix**: a tree still emits only its dirt pile and never its grown stages. Closing it needs PER-ENTRY frame geometry, which is its own task.

⚠️ **AND THE WHOLE COMPOSITE STILL DID NOTHING FOR THE PLAYER AT FIRST, BECAUSE THE PLAYER IS NOT THAT ID.** `PLAYER_GRAPHICS_ID` was **`OBJ_EVENT_GFX_LEAF`**, whose pic table is a bare `overworld_ascending_frames(...)` macro — 9 frames, one file, and **no run art anywhere in source**. Found by probing the gate rather than by reading, after the tests failed. This was the wrong half of a real split: `pics/people/leaf.png` is a STANDALONE cameo sprite, while `pics/people/leaf/` is the FRLG **player set** (green_normal / green_bike / green_surf / green_surf_run / green_fish / green_item). **Rob's call, after seeing both sprites rendered side by side: switch to `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_GREEN_NORMAL`.** That also closes an inconsistency older than running — the player was already SURFING as `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_GREEN_SURF` while WALKING as a different character's sprite. Pinned by E.08 precisely because it is a one-word change that silently disables the entire feature.

**Mechanics, each ported as a ratio rather than a constant.** Speed is **exactly double** — source's `MOVE_SPEED_FAST_1` step table is 8 entries against the walk's 16 (`sStep2Funcs`/`sStep1Funcs`, counted) — so 0.16s becomes 0.08s, preserving the invariant the way `_player_step_ticks` already does for the walk. The animation is `sAnim_Run*Frlg` (the **FRLG** variants; `IS_FRLG` selects between two genuinely different tables): neutral/legA/neutral/legB at **5,3,5,3**. ⚠️ **UNEVEN, AND THAT IS THE ASSERTION** — an even 4/4 split keeps the cadence and loses the gait, which is why the cycle is timed in SECONDS against the step rather than integer ticks (at ~4.8 frames a tile, 5:3 would round to 3:2). ⚠️ **The run neutral is a RUN-SPECIFIC frame, never the standing pose the walk rests on.**

**Gate**, from `field_player_avatar.c:906-919`, in source's own order: not surfing (source returns one block earlier, at `PLAYER_AVATAR_FLAG_SURFING`, having already moved at `PlayerWalkFast` — a surfing player is not "a runner who is refused", the question is never asked), not a ledge hop, key held, `FLAG_SYS_B_DASH`, `IsRunningDisallowed`, and finally the sheet itself. ⚠️ **THE TILE CHECKED IS THE ONE YOU ARE LEAVING** (`currentMetatileBehavior`), so it reuses `prev_behavior`, already captured before `_cell` moves. Deliberately not modelled, each with a reason rather than by omission: underwater (no such state), `ObjectMovingOnRockStairs`/`PlayerRunSlow` (no rock-stair behaviour imported), follower/dowsing (neither system exists).

⚠️ **THE MAP-HEADER HALF OF SOURCE'S GATE IS DEAD AT THIS CONFIG AND NEEDED NO IMPORTER CHANGE.** `IsRunningDisallowed` (`bike.c:1370`) is `(OW_RUNNING_INDOORS == GEN_3 && !gMapHeader.allowRunning) || <behaviour>`, and `OW_RUNNING_INDOORS` is **GEN_LATEST** — so running indoors is simply allowed. Worth stating because the obvious reading is that a per-map `allowRunning` flag must be imported; it must not, and `gen_map_import.py` is untouched. The behaviour set is `MB_NO_RUNNING` / `MB_LONG_GRASS` / `MB_HOT_SPRINGS` / the four Pacifidlog logs — note `MB_LONG_GRASS_SOUTH_EDGE` is a SEPARATE behaviour that source does NOT list, so the edge tile stays runnable and is pinned as a discriminator.

**Input is SHIFT, read as a raw held key.** Source uses B; B is already `ui_cancel`, which opens the START menu in the field. `project.godot` has no `[input]` section at all and the battle screen's F3 toggle already reads a raw keycode, so this matches the established shape — **M26C8 owns real input mapping** and is where it should become rebindable. `_can_run` is split into a thin key-reading wrapper over `_can_run_with(behavior, outcome, run_held)` so every condition is testable deterministically; ⚠️ **the key read itself is therefore NOT covered**, which is deliberate and stated — K-b's naming screen was unreachable FROM THE KEYBOARD precisely because its driver called past the input layer, and L2 repeated it.

**Reachability**: no in-game event grants `FLAG_SYS_B_DASH` yet, so the debug boot grants it (pinned by F.05, or running would be dead code in play).

**Tests**: new `m27e_run_test` **45/45**. Four injections, each failing exactly what it should and nothing else: reverting the player sprite to `LEAF` fails E.01/E.07/E.08/E.09; an even 4,4,4,4 cadence fails B.04 **and D.06** (the sample that sits inside entry 0 only because it is 5 units long — D.01-D.05 pass under both); a gate ignoring the held key fails E.02; dropping long grass fails C.02. Z.99 caught two miscounts of my own before either could ship. Regression: **all 21 overworld suites green**, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32 reproducible, and the sprite regeneration left **only the 3 intended sheets** changed (berry trees restored byte-for-byte once the geometry guard landed).

⚠️ **DISCLOSED — NOT LIVE-DRIVEN.** The cycle maths, the gate, the cadence and the composited sheet are all test- and pixel-verified, but **nobody has held Shift and watched it**. The two things most likely to read wrong on screen are the ones a test cannot see: whether 0.08s/tile actually feels like running rather than skating, and whether the 5:3 gait reads correctly at that speed. This project's own record is unambiguous that driving finds what suites cannot.

⚠️ **ONE DELIBERATE CONSEQUENCE, FLAGGED RATHER THAN LEFT TO SURPRISE SOMEONE**: `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_OLD_MAN_2` is now composited too (9 -> 10 frames), which is source-faithful — its table references only frames 0,1,2 plus one OldWoman frame, so that NPC becomes effectively facing-only where it previously used the raw sheet's real step frames. **One consumer, one map** (`PewterCity_Museum_2F_Frlg`), and unwatched.

⚠️ **[M27E E2 — SUPERSEDED THE SAME DAY: THE COMPOSITE IS GONE.]** Everything above shipped in commit `a7fcf13a` and was then **reverted at Rob's direction**, 2026-08-04. Kept rather than rewritten (this file does not edit history), but **do not act on the composite half of it** — the generator no longer composites anything, and `gen_object_event_sprites.py`'s multi-symbol handling, `_write_composite`, the geometry guard and the `mixed geom` report are all removed.

**Rob's objection, and it was right**: *"Seems like it shouldn't be [a composite] with the run sheet available."* The run frames are already a usable sheet on disk — `leaf/green_surf_run.png`, raw frames 3-13, sitting beside the three surf poses at 0-2. Materialising a stitched 20-frame copy of a file we already ship is manufacturing an asset to justify machinery.

**What replaced it**: the run frames are read from that sheet directly and `WalkAnim` **swaps the sprite's texture for the duration of a run**. New `setup_run(run_graphics_id)` binds the run sheet; `_draw` takes a `run` flag and swaps only when the cycle actually crosses between sheets; `setup()` caches the walk texture and clears the applied-sheet marker, so the surf path (which assigns `sprite.texture` directly) can never leave this class believing a stale sheet is mounted.

**Consequences of the switch, all in the good direction:**
  * **zero asset churn** — all three sheets are byte-identical to their pre-composite state again, verified against `b5ada413`;
  * **the OldMan2 side effect is gone** — that NPC is back to its own 9-frame sheet, so the flagged one-map behaviour change no longer exists;
  * **the berry-tree hazard is moot** — nothing composites, so nothing can crop a grown stage. ⚠️ The underlying finding still stands and is now *purely* informational: `gen_object_event_sprites.py` still emits only the FIRST file of any multi-symbol pic table, so a berry tree still renders its dirt pile and never its grown stages. **That is a real, open, pre-existing gap this feature no longer touches.**

⚠️ **THE COST IS A -6 INDEX SHIFT, AND IT IS THE ONE PLACE AN ERROR CAN HIDE.** Source's `sAnim_Run*Frlg` quote 9/12/15 because its pic table stitches the two files; reading the file directly makes every index 6 lower (3/6/9, legs 4/5, 7/8, 10/11). Pinned **twice** — arithmetically against source's own numbers (B.06) and by **pixels** (A.05-A.07: no run index may land on a surf pose, and the three neutrals must be distinct real art). `MIN_RAW_FRAMES_TO_RUN = 12` is compared against the **sheet's measured width**, never a graphics id's declared `frames`, because `FRAME_OVERRIDES` deliberately reports 3 for the surf ids and an id-based check would refuse the very sheet the run frames live on.

⚠️ **`PLAYER_GRAPHICS_ID` STAYS `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_GREEN_NORMAL`, AND UNDER THE SWAP IT MATTERS MORE, NOT LESS.** The run frames are swapped in from Green's sheet, so if the player still walked as `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_LEAF` they would visibly change **character** every time Shift went down. Pinned by E.08.

⚠️ **A REAL MISTAKE MADE AND CAUGHT DURING THE REVERT, worth recording**: reverting the generator to `b5ada413` reverted it **one commit too far** — `FRAME_OVERRIDES` was added by E1c, which was part of `a7fcf13a` rather than before it — silently restoring `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_GREEN_SURF` to 14 declared frames and making the surf sheet animate again. **Caught by `m27e_surf_test` (74 -> 71/74)**, not by inspection; a checkout-based revert reverts everything in that file, including things the session never touched.

**Tests**: `m27e_run_test` 45 -> **46/46**, re-based to raw indices. Three fresh injections: unshifted pic indices fail **8** assertions across A/B/D (bounds, tables and cycle independently); pointing the run sheet at the walk sheet fails E.01/E.07/E.08/E.09; reverting to the cameo sprite fails E.08. Regression: all **21 overworld suites** green, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

**Still NOT live-driven** — unchanged from above, and the swap adds one thing worth watching that a test cannot see: whether the texture change at run start/stop is invisible in motion.

## M27K — Game flow

**[M27K K-a — the starter] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. Kanto's own Oak's Lab script gives you a real Pokémon.**

⚠️ **STEP 0 SPLIT THIS MILESTONE IN TWO.** I proposed M27K as one tight scope (title + naming + starter). It is two halves with different characters: **the starter is already authored as imported data** — Oak's Lab carries **153 labels**, and `EventScript_ChoseStarter` reads end to end — while the title/naming/new-game flow is **C rather than map script** (`title_screen.c` 873 lines, `main_menu.c` 2313, `naming_screen.c` 2650), so it is a port of code rather than of data. Only K-a is built here.

⚠️ **CORRECTION, ROB 2026-08-03 — I CLAIMED THE NEW-GAME SPEECH IS "BIRCH'S, NOT OAK'S (263 REFERENCES TO 0)". THAT WAS WRONG, AND THE WAY IT WAS WRONG MATTERS MORE THAN THE FACT.** I grepped exactly ONE FILE (`src/main_menu.c`), where the figure is real, and reported it as a conclusion about the whole reference — the error M36's own standing rule (6) already names: *"grep found nothing" is a statement about the PATTERN, not the source.* A file-scoped measurement was presented as a source-scoped finding. **The reference ships FRLG's Oak intro in full**: `src/oak_speech.c` (**2193 lines**, a complete implementation — `Task_OakSpeech_WelcomeToTheWorld`, `..._ThisWorld`, `..._ReleaseNidoranFFromPokeBall`, `..._IsInhabitedFarAndWide` …), the whole `gOakSpeech_Text_*` string set (welcome / this world / I study Pokémon / tell me about yourself / your name / so your name is {PLAYER} / your rival's name / let's go, plus `AskPlayerGender`), and `data/text/new_game_intro_frlg.inc` (243 lines) carrying **FRLG's own preset name list** — RED, GREEN, LEAF, FIRE, GARY, ASH, KENE, TORU, KAZ. **So K-b is substantially MORE portable than I said**: the speech beats, the naming screen and its presets, and gender selection are all real portable material. What stays authored is the *story content* this project replaces FRLG's with, and the title art — not the mechanism.

⚠️ **A SPECIES CONSTANT WAS WORTH 0, AND 295 CORPUS ARGS ARE ONE.** `ScriptVM._literal` had no `SPECIES_*` branch, so `setvar PLAYER_STARTER_SPECIES, SPECIES_BULBASAUR` stored **nothing** — the starter script could not name its own Pokémon, and the same silence covered 70 `setvar`, 25 `bufferspeciesname`, 24 `setwildbattle` and 19 `givemon` args. Exactly the shape of the YES/NO inversion `_literal` already documents: wrong at every call site at once, and quiet. Fixed **in `_literal`** rather than in `givemon`, so `setvar`/`compare`/`copyvar`/`switch` all agree — which is what source does, since every arg goes through `VarGet` and a species constant is just a number by then. Break-tested: removing the branch fails B.01/B.02 **and** D.02/D.03/D.04.

**New `scripts/gen_species_name_map.py` → `data/species_name_to_id.json`** — 1672 constants over 1574 ids, idempotent. The third instance of this bridge after `gen_move_name_map.py` and `gen_item_name_map.py`, deliberately built the same way rather than a third way. ⚠️ **98 entries are `SPECIES_X = SPECIES_Y` aliases, and TWO are in this project's own roster** — `SPECIES_CASTFORM` and `SPECIES_DEOXYS`. Measured, not assumed: an int-only parse of the enum loses 99 constants of which **exactly those two** are implemented here — the identical failure `gen_item_name_map.py` hit with `ITEM_ITEMFINDER`. **Every constant is emitted, not just the 386 implemented**, so a Gen 4+ name resolves to a real id and then fails at the ROSTER lookup — keeping "unknown constant" (a pipeline bug) apart from "known but out of scope" (a boundary), the same two-step as I1's `item_id_of`/`get_item_identity`.

⚠️ **THE ROSTER-COVERAGE GUARD FOUND A PREFIX COLLISION, NOT A GAP.** Five corpus constants failed to resolve: `SPECIES_GFX_CHANGE_*`. They are **not species** — they are graphics-change modes from a different enum entirely (`include/constants/battle_anim.h:690`), and every use is a `createvisualtask` inside a battle-ANIMATION script, which is M36's domain rather than a map script. Excluded **by name with the citation** so the carve-out is a stated fact rather than a quietly loosened assertion, and A.09a pins that those five are the only exclusions.

**New opcodes**: `givemon` (real), `removeobject` (recorded for the caller to apply — the VM cannot reach the scene tree, the same split Stage 2 uses for battles), and no-ops for `showmonpic`/`hidemonpic` (no field picture layer) and `playfanfare`/`waitfanfare`/`playse`/`playbgm`/`fadedefaultbgm`. ⚠️ **Audio is a project-wide absence, not a stub for this scene** — already recorded against M36-S; a fanfare is a real beat here and it is genuinely silent. Listed explicitly so each stays a known no-op rather than an `UNKNOWN_OP` halt.

⚠️ **A FULL PARTY REFUSES THE GIFT AND SAYS SO.** Source's own macro asks for the first empty slot and reports failure in `VAR_RESULT`; with no PC (I5-5, deferred past the slice) there is nowhere else to put it, so refusing is the honest shape — the same call `[M27H H4]` made for a full-party catch. An out-of-roster species is refused separately, with a diagnostic naming the roster so it does not read as a parse error. Both break-tested (C.05/C.06 and C.07/C.08).

**Live-driven on the real script**: pick the Bulbasaur ball → `PLAYER_STARTER_SPECIES = 1`, `RIVAL_STARTER_SPECIES = 4` → `ChoseStarter` → **Bulbasaur Lv5 in the party**, `FLAG_SYS_POKEMON_GET` set, the ball object removed. Picking Squirtle gives **Squirtle Lv5** and pairs the rival differently — a discriminator, since a starter choice that always handed out Bulbasaur would satisfy every other check. ⚠️ **THIS IS WHAT MAKES THE PARTY SCREEN REACHABLE IN PLAY** — nothing in production set that flag before, so `[M27I I5-2]`'s browse mode was dead code from the player's side.

⚠️ **WHERE IT STOPS, AND WHY THAT IS THE RIGHT SEAM**: accepting the nickname prompt halts on `fadescreen` into `ChangePokemonNickname` — **the naming screen**, which is the mechanism **K-b needs for player naming too**. Declining runs clean through and parks on `waitmovement` (the rival walking over to take his), which is correct rather than a halt. So the one thing between K-a and a complete opening is a screen that serves both halves.

**Tests**: new `m27k_starter_test` **37/37**. Three guards break-tested: dropping the `_literal` branch fails 5 assertions across two sections, ignoring the roster fails C.05/C.06, and letting a full party accept a 7th fails C.07/C.08. Regression: `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `m27f_stage4_test` 63/63, `m27i_i5_party_test` 58/58, `m27i_i4_bag_screen_test` 48/48, `m27i_bag_test` 47/47, `m27i_item_identity_test` 31/31, `m27o_field_poison_test` 57/57, `m27h_wild_encounters_test` 49/49, `m27h_catching_test` 49/49, `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `m20a_data_test` 387/387, `m23_3_converter_test` 56/56, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

⚠️ **NOT BUILT — K-b**: title screen, save-slot selection (needs M27L), **player naming** (which would retire the `"LEAF"` placeholder now hardcoded in `TextBuffers.PLAYER_NAME`, `_PLAYER_BACK_PIC` and the overworld sprite — three copies agreeing by luck), gender selection, the rival's name, and the opening speech. [An earlier draft called this *"design work rather than a port"* on the strength of the wrong Birch measurement corrected above. With `oak_speech.c` and the FRLG preset names in hand, most of it IS a port; what is genuinely authored is the story this project tells in place of FRLG's.] **The naming screen is the shared mechanism** — it serves player naming, the rival's name AND the starter's own nickname branch, which is where K-a stops, so building it once closes three things.

**[M27K K-b — the new game: Oak's speech, gender, and both names] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. The player has an identity.**

Live-driven end to end in the real scene: the five speech beats → *"Are you a boy? Or are you a girl?"* → **GIRL** → the naming screen opens on the **female** preset list → NEW NAME → keyboard → **AOB** → *"Right… So your name is AOB."* → the rival's own list → **GARY** → *"His name is GARY!"* → *"AOB! Your very own POKéMON legend is about to unfold!"*. Afterwards `{PLAYER}` reads `AOB` and `{RIVAL}` reads `GARY`.

⚠️ **THIS RETIRES A HARDCODE THAT LIVED IN THREE PLACES AGREEING BY LUCK.** `"LEAF"` was written into `TextBuffers.PLAYER_NAME`, `battle_screen_shared.gd`'s `_PLAYER_BACK_PIC`, and the overworld sprite `[M27D D1]` chose — three copies with nothing keeping them in step. New `PlayerIdentity` owns the answer; the constants survive **only as fallbacks**, because a boot that skips the speech (a test, a debug F6 into the corridor) must still render a name rather than a blank, and `display_name()` returns the same strings so both halves agree. `{PLAYER}` is **1193 corpus uses** and until now every one read a constant.

⚠️ **GENDER IS ASKED BEFORE THE NAME, AND THAT ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING.** `sMaleNameChoices`/`sFemaleNameChoices` (`src/oak_speech.c:593-650`) are genuinely different lists, so a name chosen first would be chosen from the wrong one — the live drive shows the female list (OMI…) after picking GIRL. B.01 pins that the lists actually differ, which is what makes the ordering matter rather than being decoration.

⚠️ **SOURCE SHOWS A PRESET MENU, NOT A KEYBOARD — and opening on the keyboard would be a different game.** FRLG offers the preset list with **NEW NAME** at its head (`gOtherText_NewName`) and only that row reaches the keyboard; most real playthroughs never type at all. Break-tested: opening in keyboard mode fails 12 assertions — but ⚠️ **that count flatters the guard and the real figure is 3.** Checked when Rob pushed on it: only **C.01** (it opens on the preset list), **C.02** (NEW NAME at the head) and **C.03** (the presets under it) prove the claim. C.06/C.07/C.09 are the same fact restated through another surface — `move`/`confirm`/`typed` all behave differently once the mode is wrong. The six `D.*` failures are **fixture drift, not evidence**: the D section reaches the keyboard by calling `confirm()` on NEW NAME, so the break makes that setup call type "A" instead and the whole section runs one keypress out of step. Recorded because quoting a raw failure count without checking what produced it is the same family as this file's own vacuous-guard problem — a number that includes cascade reads stronger than the evidence behind it. The **rival has its own list** (`sRivalNameChoices`, 8 entries), not the male one — reusing the male list would offer a set source does not.

⚠️ **THE VERSION-GATED HEAD IS A CHOICE, RECORDED AS ONE.** Source's tables open with `#if defined(FIRERED)` — FireRed leads RED/FIRE/ASH/KENE/GEKI, LeafGreen leads GREEN/LEAF/GARY/KAZ/TORU. This project takes **LeafGreen's**, because `_PLAYER_BACK_PIC` is already Leaf and a game whose sprite is Leaf offering "RED" first would be incoherent.

⚠️ **THE CAP IS 12, DELIBERATELY NOT SOURCE'S 7 — Rob's call, 2026-08-03, after asking whether 7 was necessary or merely inherited.** It was inherited, and checking before changing it found **nothing in this project requires it**: no fixed-width slot displays the player's name anywhere (the only consumers are `{PLAYER}` substitutions in message text), this project saves structured data as JSON (`team_storage.gd`), which has no field width, and nothing reserves glyphs for it in the message box. Source's `PLAYER_NAME_LENGTH` (`global.h:159`) exists because the GBA sizes a fixed save-block field to it. **12 matches `POKEMON_NAME_LENGTH`** (`global.h:156`), which nicknames will want anyway, so the project carries ONE name bound rather than two. [An earlier draft of this entry justified 7 with *"M27L will want the same bound"* — that was wrong, and is corrected rather than left standing as a reason for a number nothing requires.] Verified after the change that an 11-character name renders correctly in the authored dialogue (*"So your name is BARTHOLOMEW."*, *"His name is MAXIMILIAN!"*), which was the one real risk in raising it, since FRLG's lines are hand-wrapped around short names.

⚠️ **THE CAP IS ENFORCED TWICE, ON PURPOSE.** The screen **refuses the keypress past the cap** so the limit is visible; `PlayerIdentity.sanitize` **truncates** whatever it is handed so a caller bypassing the screen cannot mint an unstorable name. Two jobs, not a duplicated check. Break-tested separately (D.06). One stale literal (`length() == 7`) was caught by the cap change and now reads the constant, so the next change to it cannot leave one behind.

⚠️ **B BACKSPACES ON THE KEYBOARD, IT DOES NOT CANCEL** — source's own binding, and the reason matters: cancelling out of naming mid-new-game would leave the player unnamed with no way back in. Break-tested: making B cancel fails 5 assertions.

**Text is verbatim** from `data/text/new_game_intro_frlg.inc`, and the **beat order is source's own task chain** (welcome → this world → inhabited far and wide → I study Pokémon → tell me about yourself → gender → your name → rival → let's go). Reordering reads fine and is a different scene. Gender reuses `YesNoBox` rather than a second two-option widget — source asks it as one question with two answers, and that widget already carries the 5-frame debounce (which caught the live driver out before it caught a player).

⚠️ **STILL NOT PORTED**: the tiled background scene (needs a real tile/tilemap decode, not a plain copy — a separate, larger, undecided task), the shrink-into-the-overworld exit (a plain fade stands in — see below). The Controls Guide and Pikachu Intro screens that source's real entry point (`StartNewGameSceneFrlg`) also drives are deferred too, Rob's own call — recon (`docs/m27k_oak_speech_visuals_recon.md`) found neither has ANY logic or text in this project, a bigger gap than the portrait/Nidoran theatre alone, and neither was picked up.

**Ground platform: BUILT, 2026-08-05.** Three `TextureRect`s sliced from `platform.png`'s own 3 vertically-stacked 32×32 frames (a plain-copy indexed PNG, confirmed before pulling — same handling as the portraits), placed side by side as one strip, drawn first so everything else layers on top. Deliberately NOT source's own 3-separate-sprite placement at literal GBA screen coordinates — matches `YesNoBox`'s own established call to not port literal GBA tile coordinates onto this project's 1024×768 canvas. Tied to `show_solo()`/`pick_gender()` (show it) and `hide_all()` (hide it) rather than a separate toggle. ⚠️ **A real bug found and fixed while testing**: `_platform_frame()` always returns a non-null `AtlasTexture` wrapper even when the underlying sheet fails to load (a brand-new asset with no `.import` sidecar yet logs an ERROR and loads null) — a first-draft assertion checking `tile.texture != null` was vacuously true regardless. Fixed by forcing the missing import (`godot --headless --editor --quit`, the same fix class already on record from M26c-2/M25e) and by correcting the assertion to check `tile.texture.atlas != null` instead. Attempting to break-test this a second time (removing the `.import` sidecar again, then hiding the source PNG entirely) did NOT reproduce the original failure either time — Godot's own `.godot/imported/` cache persists the compiled texture independent of source/sidecar presence once warm — recorded honestly as a real bug found and fixed, not a cleanly re-provable break-test.

**Ball-release beat: BUILT, 2026-08-05 — but a RANDOM roster species, not source's fixed Nidoran♀, Rob's own explicit call.** Fully random across all 386, legendaries included — there is no legendary/mythical tag anywhere in `pokemon.json`/`PokemonSpecies` to filter against even if a curated pool had been chosen instead, confirmed before asking rather than assumed. `OakSpeechOverlay.release_random_pokemon()` reuses the exact ball-sheet asset the battle send-out animation already loads, but NOT the battle screen's own tuned `_play_send_out` sequence — confirmed not reusable outside battle without its `BattleParty`/slot-array scaffolding. Built standalone instead: ball appears closed → wobbles → opens, a species sprite (from `SpriteRegistry.get_front(dex)`, the same loader battle uses, confirmed all 386 roster species have a real pulled sprite) grows in → holds → both fade out. Species picked the same way `RandomTeamGenerator.generate_team()` already does — a random entry from `PokemonRegistry.get_all_species()`, not an assumed-contiguous dex range. ⚠️ **NO CRY PLAYS** — a fresh grep confirmed zero audio playback exists anywhere in the live project (a real per-species cry `.wav` library sits vendored and unwired at `assets/Essentials_v19.1/Audio/Cries/`, genuinely available later), and standing up this project's first-ever audio system for one incidental beat was judged out of scope, matching the existing `playfanfare`/`playse` no-op precedent from `[M27K K-a]`. The reverse ball-recall animation source plays later was also not built — release only. **Wired at source's own real placement**: the opening `_say` batch was split so the beat plays between `OAK_THIS_WORLD` and `OAK_INHABITED`, matching `Task_OakSpeech_IsInhabitedFarAndWide` pairing the release with that specific line. Live-driven via a disposable probe (deleted after): ball becomes visible immediately closed, the released sprite reaches full scale/opacity with a real non-null texture, both fade and hide cleanly, and the message box resumes with exactly `OAK_INHABITED`/`OAK_I_STUDY`/`OAK_ABOUT_YOURSELF` right after.

**Portraits: BUILT, 2026-08-05.** `scripts/gen_oak_speech_assets.py` pulls Oak's/the player's/the rival's portraits — all real, small (64×96, already-indexed PNGs, ~13.8KB total, confirmed a plain copy suffices, no GBA tile/palette decode needed) — into `assets/sprites/oak_speech/`. New `OakSpeechOverlay` (`scripts/overworld/oak_speech_overlay.gd`) shows Oak throughout his own lines, the chosen portrait for name confirmation, and the rival's for rival naming, fading out at the close. Built in code, not a `.tscn`, matching every sibling field widget's own established convention for this specific subsystem (`MessageBox`/`YesNoBox`/`NamingScreen`/... — confirmed none of them are `.tscn` either, before assuming this project's more general "prefer scene-tree-visible UI" guidance applied here).

⚠️ **THE GENDER QUESTION NOW DIVERGES FROM SOURCE ON PURPOSE — Rob's own explicit call, not a K-b simplification left standing.** Source shows no portrait during the choice (a bare 2-item text menu). This project shows Red and Leaf **side by side, directly clickable** — picking a look IS the answer. `YesNoBox` no longer drives this question at all, replaced by `OakSpeechOverlay.pick_gender()` (same BOY/GIRL polarity preserved). **This is the first mouse-clickable widget in this project's overworld field-UI subsystem** — every sibling widget is keyboard/gamepad-only via `overworld.gd`'s central `move()`/`confirm()` `_unhandled_input` dispatch, confirmed by a full grep before writing this. `move()`/`confirm()` are exposed on `OakSpeechOverlay` too, matching the sibling shape, but deliberately **not** wired into that dispatch yet — Rob's own "mouse now, keyboard/gamepad in future" sequencing, stated as a deferral so it doesn't read as the same class of oversight `[M27L L2]` already found and fixed for this exact question's prior `YesNoBox`.

⚠️ **A REAL ENVIRONMENT LIMITATION FOUND WHILE LIVE-DRIVING, NOT A BUG**: headless `Viewport.push_input()` does NOT route `InputEventMouseButton`/`InputEventMouseMotion` to `Control` nodes in this project's Godot build — confirmed via a disposable probe (deleted after) that pushed a real click at the button's own correct global rect and got neither `gui_input` nor `mouse_entered` to fire. Same CLASS of gap as `[M27L L2]`'s already-documented `Input.action_just_pressed()` headless limitation, now confirmed to extend to mouse/GUI routing too — worth remembering for any FUTURE button-based field widget. Fell back to calling the button's own handler directly (this project's established workaround for the whole class) to prove the LOGIC end to end: gender resolves to GIRL, the solo portrait swaps to `leaf.png`, the rival portrait shows during rival naming, a real rival name gets chosen. **Real mouse-click routing itself is standard `TextureButton` engine behavior, not custom code — unverified in this session for lack of a display, not untrustworthy**, flagged honestly rather than claimed as proven.

**Tests**: `m27k_newgame_test` gained section F (8 assertions — button-visibility defaults, `show_solo`/`hide_all`, the platform's 3 tiles each carrying a real loaded sheet [checking `.atlas`, not just `.texture`, per the bug above] and hiding correctly, `pick_gender()`'s full async round trip driven via `call_deferred` since GDScript requires `await` at a coroutine's own call site, the resolved BOY/GIRL polarity) and section G (3 assertions — nothing showing before the ball-release beat, both ball and released sprite hidden again once it ends awaiting the full ~2s real sequence, a real texture assigned), `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 47 → 58, 59/59 including the harness's own Z.99 self-count. K-b's original 47 assertions never actually drove `_yes_no` through `run_new_game()` end to end (confirmed before touching anything), so none needed changing. Regression: `m27k_starter_test` 37/37, `m27f_script_vm_test` 136/136, `m27l_save_test` 105/105, `m27f_stage4_test` 63/63, `m27o_field_poison_test` 57/57, `m27o_whiteout_test` 33/33, `m27i_i4_bag_screen_test` 53/53, `m27i_bag_test` 47/47, `m27k_kc_nickname_test` 59/59 — all unchanged. Nothing committed to git, per standing instruction.

**Tests**: new `m27k_newgame_test` **47/47**. Four guards break-tested and confirmed to catch their regressions: opening on the keyboard fails 12, letting the 8th character through fails D.06, making B cancel fails D.04-D.08, and ignoring identity in `{PLAYER}` fails E.01-E.03.

**[M27K K-c — the nickname branch] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. The starter script now runs to its own ending.**

Live-driven in the real scene: pick the Bulbasaur ball → *"Is this the one?"* YES → *"Bulbasaur's nickname?"* → the keyboard → **SPUD** → OK → the field party screen reads `▶ SPUD  Lv5  20/20`.

⚠️ **K-a's SCRIPT HALTED BEFORE THE SPECIAL, AND NOT WHERE ANYONE WOULD LOOK.** `EventScript_ChoseStarter` steps 15-19 are the nickname yes/no, YES goes to `EventScript_GiveNicknameToStarter` → `Common_EventScript_NameReceivedPartyMon`, and **that script's FIRST opcode is `fadescreen`**, which the VM did not implement. So the branch died one op before `special ChangePokemonNickname` ever ran — which is why K-a's own test runner answers NO, and why "wire one special" understated the work. **5 corpus call sites**, so it is not a one-off: the starter, the Celadon Eevee, the Game Corner prizes and the three Cinnabar fossils.

⚠️ **`fadescreen` IS A NO-OP, AND *NOT* BECAUSE NO FADE SYSTEM EXISTS — `_fade_to` does, and warps and the battle round trip both use it.** It is a no-op because **the fade it starts is never closed by another opcode**: `Common_EventScript_NameReceivedPartyMon` is `fadescreen FADE_TO_BLACK` / `special` / `return`, and the fade back is done by the naming screen's own return callback (`CB2_ReturnToFieldContinueScriptPlayMapMusic`), which is engine plumbing this project has no equivalent for. A faithful-looking fade here would leave the screen **black for good**, so the no-op is the safe reading rather than the lazy one. **128 corpus uses, 106 of them `FADE_TO_BLACK`** — a future session implementing it properly must pair it with the SCREEN TRANSITION, not with a matching opcode, because in 106 places there is no matching opcode to pair with. `fadescreenspeed` (8) and `fadescreenswapbuffers` (34) are deliberately excluded.

⚠️ **A NICKNAME HAS NO PRESET LIST, WHICH IS THE EXACT OPPOSITE OF K-b's SCREEN — and both are source.** `sMonNamingScreenTemplate` (`naming_screen.c:2172`) is a bare keyboard serving both `NAMING_SCREEN_NICKNAME` and `NAMING_SCREEN_CAUGHT_MON`; only the PLAYER/RIVAL templates get presets. Hence `open_keyboard()` as a **second entry point** rather than a flag on the first. Its cap is `POKEMON_NAME_LENGTH` = **12** (`global.h:156`), which is source-exact here — K-b's raise to 12 means the project now carries one bound rather than two, exactly as that entry predicted.

⚠️ **OK ON AN EMPTY ENTRY IS ACCEPTED FOR A NICKNAME AND REFUSED FOR A PLAYER NAME.** `SaveInputText` (`naming_screen.c:1921`) copies the typed buffer into the destination **only if some character is neither space nor EOS**, and `ChangePokemonNicknameWithCallback` (`pokemon.c:6892`) pre-seeds that destination with the current nickname — so OK-with-nothing-typed leaves the mon named after its species, which is how you back out once the keyboard is already up. A player name has no such seed, so the same behaviour would mint a blank player. Break-tested: making the nickname refuse an empty entry fails A.08-A.09.

⚠️ **A REAL K-b DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED: THE KEYBOARD WAS UNREACHABLE FROM THE KEYBOARD.** K-b bound accepting to `ui_text_submit`, and **a probe of the actual InputMap found `ui_accept` and `ui_text_submit` are BOTH bound to Enter** (this project declares no `[input]` section, so Godot's defaults ship). `_drive_naming` tests `ui_accept` first in an elif chain, so pressing Enter always typed a character and **a typed name could never be submitted at all**. K-b's live drive missed it because the driver called `accept()` directly instead of pressing keys — a driver that reaches past the input layer cannot test the input layer. Fixed with source's own design rather than a fourth binding: **an OK key on the grid** (`naming_screen.c` has one) that you move onto and press A on, which removes the collision instead of working around it. Break-tested: clamping the cursor one cell short so OK is unreachable fails A.06-A.07.

⚠️ **`VAR_0x8004` IS A PARTY SLOT INDEX, NOT A FLAG.** `GetSelectedBoxMonFromPcOrParty` (`pokemon.c:6846`) indexes `gParties[B_TRAINER_PLAYER][gSpecialVar_0x8004]` directly, with the single reserved value `PC_MON_CHOSEN` (0xFE, `party_menu.h:4`) diverting to the box. **The PC path HALTS** — there is no PC (I5-5, deferred past the slice) and renaming a party member when the script asked for a boxed one is the near-miss that reads as working.

⚠️ **AND THE PC GUARD'S OWN TEST WAS WEAK UNTIL THE BREAK TEST SAID SO.** Deleting the PC branch entirely left C.13 **green**, because 0xFE is also past the end of any party, so the range check below it halts with the same pause and the same untouched nickname — two competing rules agreeing on one fixture, this file's own recorded rule-(13) trap, hit again. C.14 is now written as the discriminator (the diagnostic is the PC one and **not** the range one) and says so at the assertion.

⚠️ **`display_name()` — THE NICKNAME WAS A FIELD YOU COULD WRITE AND NEVER SEE.** `BattlePokemon.nickname` has existed since the trainer-data work and **nothing but a test harness ever read it**: the field party screen and eleven sites in `battle_screen_shared.gd` all read `species.species_name`. Without this, naming your Bulbasaur SPUD would leave every screen still saying Bulbasaur. **The field party screen is swapped; the battle screen's call sites deliberately are NOT.** The change is correct but visible: a Transformed Ditto reads DITTO through `display_name()` and PIKACHU through `species_name`, and **source agrees with `display_name()`** — that is a battle-behaviour change belonging to a pass that can be driven and looked at, not a rider on a field feature. B.06 pins the divergence on a fixture where the two genuinely disagree, since everywhere else they start equal.

⚠️ **DISCLOSED, AND THE LIVE DRIVE IS WHAT FOUND IT: WITH THE DEBUG PARTY LOADED, NICKNAMING THE STARTER RENAMES THE WRONG POKéMON.** `EventScript_GiveNicknameToStarter` hardcodes `setvar VAR_0x8004, 0` because in source a new game has an **empty** party, so the starter lands in slot 0. This project boots with `[M27D D5]`'s 3-member debug party, so `givemon` appends at slot 3 and slot 0 is a debug mon — the first drive produced `SPUD (species Bulbasaur) Lv12` alongside an un-renamed `Bulbasaur Lv5`. **The script is source-exact and the debug party is the anomaly**, confirmed by re-driving with an empty party (`SPUD (species Bulbasaur) Lv5`, party screen `▶ SPUD  Lv5  20/20`) and pinned by D.02-D.05, which run on an empty party. It disappears with the debug party itself, which M27L owns. Recorded rather than patched: making the special prefer whatever `givemon` last placed would diverge from source AND hide the fixture problem.

**Tests**: new `m27k_kc_nickname_test` **44/44**. Six guards break-tested. ⚠️ **The counts are stated after checking what produced them**, per the K-b correction: OK-not-a-grid-cell fails 2 (A.06, A.07), empty-refused fails 2 (A.08, A.09), opening on the preset list fails 2 (A.01, A.04), `display_name` ignoring the nickname fails 4 (B.02, B.05, B.06, D.05), the PC fall-through fails **1** (C.14 — see above), and always-rename-the-lead **reports 6 but only 1 is load-bearing**: C.07 proves it, C.09/C.10 restate it through another surface, and C.13/C.14/C.15 are cascade — forcing the slot to 0 means the bad-slot guards can never be reached at all. Regression: all 11 overworld suites green (`m27a` 514, `m27f_script_vm` 136, `m27f_stage4` 63, `m27i_i5_party` 58, `m27i_i4_bag_screen` 48, `m27i_bag` 47, `m27k_newgame` 48, `m27k_starter` 37, `m27o_field_poison` 57, `m27o_whiteout` 33, `m27h_catching` 49).

**[M27K K-c2 — the other four nickname sites] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. ⚠️ K-c's own claim that these "need no new mechanism, only their scripts driven" was WRONG, and driving them is what proved it.** Three opcodes were missing and **two silent pre-existing bugs** fell out, both older than K-c.

⚠️ **THE STARTER IS THE ODD ONE OUT, AND THAT NARROWS K-c's DEBUG-PARTY DISCLOSURE TO THE STARTER ALONE.** Every gift script routes through `Common_EventScript_GetGiftMonPartySlot` — `getpartysize` / `subvar VAR_RESULT, 1` / `copyvar VAR_0x8004, VAR_RESULT`, i.e. **the LAST slot**, which is exactly where `givemon` appended. Only `EventScript_GiveNicknameToStarter` hardcodes 0, and it can only get away with that because a new game's party is empty. So the Eevee, the Game Corner prizes and the three Cinnabar fossils are immune to the debug-party hazard **by construction**; K-c's recorded consequence applies to the starter and nothing else. Pinned by E.04 on a party where "last slot" and "slot 0" give different answers.

⚠️ **BUG 1, AND IT IS THE BIGGER ONE: 19 OF THE 23 `givemon` CALL SITES SILENTLY GAVE NOTHING.** `_resolve_number` was integer-or-var only and never consulted `_literal`, so `givemon SPECIES_AERODACTYL, 5` resolved the species through `var_get`, found no such var, and handed `_give_mon` a **0** — which refuses. The corpus splits **19 direct `SPECIES_*` constants against 4 var references**, and `[M27K K-a]` shipped without noticing because the one script it drove (the starter) passes `PLAYER_STARTER_SPECIES` — one of the 4 that worked. **This is the same lesson as the Birch/Oak correction in a new dress: a green test proves the path it took, not the paths it didn't.** Fixed by falling through to `_literal`, ordered var-first to mirror `VarGet`'s own `VARS_START` comparison; an unset var still reads 0, so nothing regresses.

⚠️ **BUG 2: `givemon`'s VAR_RESULT IS A THREE-WAY CODE AND THIS WROTE A BOOLEAN — WRONG ON BOTH BRANCHES.** Source is `MON_GIVEN_TO_PARTY 0` / `MON_GIVEN_TO_PC 1` / `MON_CANT_GIVE 2` (`constants/pokemon.h:167-169`). `_set_result(true)` wrote **1**, which reads as *"sent to the PC"* — so `EventScript_GiveAerodactyl`, whose very next lines are `goto_if_eq VAR_RESULT, 0 -> NicknameMonParty` and `goto_if_eq VAR_RESULT, 1 -> NicknameMonPC`, took the **PC branch on a successful gift to the party**. And the failure side was equally wrong: a full party wrote 0, which reads as *"given to the party"*. A full party now answers `MON_CANT_GIVE` deliberately rather than `MON_GIVEN_TO_PC` — source would box it, this project has no PC, and `Common_EventScript_NoMoreRoomForPokemon` is exactly where a 2 sends the script.

⚠️ **`addvar` AND `subvar` DO NOT RESOLVE THEIR OPERAND THE SAME WAY, AND THE CORPUS EXERCISES THE DIFFERENCE.** `ScrCmd_addvar` is `*ptr += ScriptReadHalfword(ctx)` — a raw immediate — while `ScrCmd_subvar` is `*ptr -= VarGet(...)`, which resolves a var reference (`scrcmd.c:589, 601`). Implementing both alike would make `subvar VAR_X, VAR_Y` subtract Y's **var-ID**. `subvar` really does carry 3 var operands (`VAR_0x8009`, `VAR_ASH_GATHER_COUNT` ×2) against 5 literals. Break-tested as its own discriminator: making `subvar` use the raw literal fails E.02 and nothing else. **DISCLOSED**: a SYMBOLIC constant operand resolves to 0 — source's assembler baked those to numbers and this project has no table for them, so `BLACK_FLUTE_PRICE`, `ROULETTE_SPECIAL_RATE` and 7 siblings read 0. All 9 are shop/roulette scripts belonging to M27G/M35, none in the corridor.

**New opcodes**: `getpartysize` (12 uses), `addvar` (104) / `subvar` (15), and the coins-box trio `showcoinsbox` / `hidecoinsbox` / `updatecoinsbox` (32 across the three), the last mirroring `showmoneybox`'s existing display-only no-op exactly. New `_set_result_value` because `getpartysize` writes a **count**, which the boolean `_set_result` cannot express — a party of 3 would land as 1 and every gift script would rename the second slot. New `FlagStore.has_var`, because `var_get` cannot tell "a var holding 0" from "not a var at all" and `_resolve_number` genuinely needs that distinction.

**Tests**: `m27k_kc_nickname_test` grew 44 → **59/59** with a new section E. Five more guards break-tested, counts checked rather than quoted: the `_resolve_number` fallback fails 4 (E.05 direct, E.06 consequential, E.08/E.09 the real Aerodactyl script), the boolean `givemon` fails 2 (E.06 direct, E.08 the script taking its PC branch), the full-party code fails 1 (E.07), `subvar`-as-literal fails 1 (E.02), and `getpartysize`-as-flag fails 5 — of which **E.09 accounts for three, one per driven script, which is one fact observed three times rather than three claims**. Regression: all 11 overworld suites green.

⚠️ **UPDATE 2026-08-05: the title screen and save-slot selection this section flagged as blocked on M27L are BUILT** — `[M27L L3]`/`[M27L L4]` shipped both (`scenes/overworld/title.tscn`, live-driven end to end, NEW GAME and CONTINUE both confirmed working). **The battle screen's own `display_name()` swap is DONE, 2026-08-05.** All ten real display-reading call sites in `battle_screen_shared.gd` (`_mon_label`, `_name_text`, `_format_debug_breakdown`'s two names, the ability-popup name label, both trainer/player send-out message builders, and all three `_status_label` prompts — fainted-replacement, choose-a-move, choose-an-action) now read `mon.display_name()` instead of `mon.species.species_name`. The eleventh original `species_name` site (`_make_mon`'s own fixture-species constructor, `:3797`) is a test-fixture BUILDER assigning a `PokemonSpecies.species_name`, not a display read, and was correctly left alone. **New regression coverage**: `message_pacing_test.gd` gained `_test_mon_label_uses_nickname_when_set` (a nicknamed attacker's move-announcement text now reads the nickname, not the species name) — 70/70 including the new case. Spot-regression across `m26_trainer_category_party_test` (130/130), `m25b_menu_test` (32/32), `m25c_message_log_test` (19/19) all green — every existing fixture has an empty nickname, so `display_name()` returns exactly what `species_name` did before and none of these needed a single expected-value change. `m26_b6_2_popup_node_test` fails 2/51 (G.05/G.05b) both **before and after** this change (confirmed by reverting `battle_screen_shared.gd` to HEAD and re-running) — pre-existing, unrelated, flagged not fixed. `battle_test.tscn`'s own CLI-narrated Struggle-loop runs clean (generic "Dummy-A"/"Dummy-B" names, no nickname set, unaffected by construction). The Transform consequence this entry originally flagged (a Transformed Ditto now reads DITTO in battle messages, not PIKACHU, matching source) was reasoned through rather than freshly tested — `display_name()` itself is unchanged code, already proven correct by K-c's own B.06 field-side test; only its NEW callers are what this pass tests.

⚠️ **`bufferboxname` remains unimplemented**, which is what the fossil scripts' PC branch halts on. Harmless today because `MON_CANT_GIVE` never routes there, but it is the second thing after `ChangePokemonNickname`'s own PC path to be blocked purely by I5-5's absent PC — two independent halts now waiting on the same missing system.

**Hall of Fame + credits: Step 0 done 2026-08-05, `docs/m27k_hof_credits_recon.md`.** Real source is `hall_of_fame_frlg.c`/`credits_frlg.c` — the wrong-file trap already caught twice this session (cinematic, title screen) pre-empted before reading either. **Two real, separate systems, not one**: Hall of Fame (party-parade + trainer-card screen, triggered on beating the Champion) hands off to a scripted farewell cutscene on a THIRD map, which only then triggers the credits proper (a 13-location Kanto camera-scroll with staff text plus 4 full-screen Pokémon-reveal interludes for Charizard/Venusaur/Blastoise/Pikachu) — credits end in a hard `SoftReset` to the title screen, not a return to play. ⚠️ **UNLIKE THE CINEMATIC AND OAK'S SPEECH, THIS ONE TOUCHES REAL PERSISTENT SAVE STATE** — a permanent "beaten the Champion" flag, and a size-capped ring-buffer save section (up to 50 past teams, 6 Pokémon each, species/level/shiny/nickname/OT-ID) readable later from a separate PC-browsing screen — neither concept exists anywhere in this project's own save system (`[M27L]`) today; both would be new fields, not a reuse. Rendering is mixed: Hall of Fame itself is plain-fades territory, credits genuinely needs a progressive (not instant) window-open reveal and a BG-affine zoom — both map onto ordinary Godot primitives, same "technique differs, beat doesn't" pattern as the cinematic. Recon only — nothing built, no decisions made.

**Still open in M27K**: `bufferboxname` (above), Hall of Fame + credits (recon'd, not built), the opening cinematic itself (recon done, decisions recorded, build explicitly deferred — see `docs/m27k_cinematic_recon.md`). Nothing committed to git, per standing instruction.

## M27L — Save/load

**[M27L L1 — serialisation and the slot round trip] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. The playthrough survives a restart.**

**Three decisions resolved by Rob at Step 0, 2026-08-03**: **(1)** a NEW snapshot format, leaving `team_storage`'s recipe alone; **(2)** a real playtime counter with the **Pokédex stubbed** so the CONTINUE card has its four rows; **(3)** `team_storage`'s box-rule violation **recorded, not migrated**.

⚠️ **A CORRECTION TO THIS BLOCK'S OWN STEP 0, MADE BEFORE BUILDING ON IT.** I reported "there is no serialisation precedent — no `to_dict`/`from_dict` anywhere". Wrong: `Bag`, `Wallet` and `RespawnPoint` already carry `to_save`/`from_save`, written **for M27L by name**, snapshot-shaped and clamping on load. Grepping one pair of names and reading the empty result as "no precedent" is this file's own rule (6) — *"grep found nothing" is a statement about the PATTERN* — walked into twice in one session after citing it twice. It shrank L1 by half. The recipe-vs-snapshot finding is unaffected: that was about `team_storage`, which really does carry only 7 fields.

⚠️ **THE SNAPSHOT IS ~16 FIELDS OF ~80, AND THE CUT IS SOURCE'S OWN.** The reference splits `gPlayerParty` (what a Pokémon IS) from `gBattleMons` (what is happening to it right now) and copies back after a battle; this project conflates both into `BattlePokemon`, so the split had to be made in `to_save`. Everything volatile — `confusion_turns`, `substitute_hp`, `perish_song_timer`, every stat stage, every `*_active` flag — is **absent on purpose**, since saving it would let a mid-battle state outlive a reload. **Stats are recomputed, not stored**: `_calculate_stats()` derives all six from species + level + IVs + EVs + nature, so storing them would be a second copy of a derived value. **`original_species`/`original_ability` are saved, not the live ones** — Transform, Conversion and Multitype all mutate in place, and saving the live value would make a mid-battle mutation permanent (B.04 pins it on a Ditto turned Pikachu).

⚠️ **THE MOVE ID LIVES IN THE RESOURCE PATH, AND THAT IS WHY NO DATA REGENERATION WAS NEEDED.** `MoveData` carries **no id field at all**; `MoveRegistry` resolves `move_%04d.tres` by convention, stated at the top of that file as its whole design. So `MoveRegistry.id_of()` recovers the id from `resource_path` rather than adding a field to 935 `.tres` files. ⚠️ It returns 0 for a hand-built `MoveData.new()`, which is every move fixture in this project's battle tests — real enough to be documented at the function.

⚠️ **`BADGE_FLAGS` MOVED FROM `overworld.gd` TO `FlagStore`, AND IT HAD TO.** The CONTINUE card counts badges **before any field scene exists**, and `overworld.gd` carries no `class_name`, so the constant was literally unreachable from a menu. A flag list belongs to the flag store in any case; the alternative was a second copy, which is the drift `badge_count()` was extracted to prevent. Both callers now delegate to one `FlagStore.badge_count()`. **This broke `m27o_whiteout_test` (33/33 → 22/23, with the total dropping because the runtime error aborted a whole function)** — caught by the regression sweep, fixed by pointing that assertion at the new home.

⚠️ **`JSON.parse_string` PUSHES AN ENGINE ERROR ON MALFORMED INPUT, WHICH IS WRONG FOR A SAVE LOADER.** Reading a corrupt slot is a NORMAL handled event here — a crash mid-write leaves exactly that — so logging an error every time the menu lists slots is noise. `read()` uses `JSON.new().parse()` instead. It also made the corrupt-file test unrunnable, since `run_overworld_tests.sh` fails any run with an ERROR line.

**Untrusted input is a rule, not a courtesy** (the scope doc's own §19, which refuses to inherit source's executable `ramScript`-inside-the-save): a truncated file, a future `version`, a missing `version`, a level of 9999, an HP above max and an over-long hand-edited name all fail closed or clamp. A corrupt slot reads as EMPTY rather than erroring, so the player can still start a new game over it. **An unreadable party member is dropped and the loss is RETURNED**, never substituted — a placeholder Pokémon appearing in someone's party is worse than a shorter party.

**Tests**: new `m27l_save_test` **55/55**. ⚠️ **Seven guards break-tested, and TWO came back vacuous** — the rule-(7) trap, caught only by injection. **C.03** (active_indices resets) passed with the reset deleted, because a fresh `BattleParty` already holds `[0]`; the fixture now seeds a doubles layout first so the negation is expressible. **D.05b** was worse and more interesting: seeding money/bag/flags proved nothing, because **every `from_save` already clears its own container** (`Bag.from_save` calls `clear()`, `FlagStore.from_save` rebuilds both dictionaries, `Wallet` assigns) — so the only state `apply()`'s `reset()` uniquely clears is the **pending battle-return trio**, and that is a real leak (loading a save mid-return would resume the loaded playthrough into the previous one's map). Both re-injected afterwards and both now fail exactly one assertion. Regression: all overworld suites green.

⚠️ **STILL OPEN IN M27L**: **L2** — the title screen and the slot-selection UI that this payload exists to feed, plus the SAVE affordance itself (nothing calls `build_payload` in production yet; the start menu needs its SAVE entry). **L3** — wiring NEW GAME to `run_new_game()` and CONTINUE to `apply()` + `position_of()`. ⚠️ **Three slots is this project's own invention** — the GBA has ONE save (`HAS_SAVED_GAME`/`HAS_NO_SAVED_GAME`), so there is no reference behaviour to be faithful to and a later session should not go looking for a `slot` concept in source. `team_storage.gd` remains a **recorded, Rob-approved** box-rule violation at `user://teams/`, to be fixed when simulator profiles are actually built.

**[M27L L2 — the SAVE affordance and the playtime counter] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. The payload is reachable in play.**

Live-driven in the real scene: START → **SAVE** → *"Would you like to save the game?"* → YES → *"SAVING…\nDON'T TURN OFF THE POWER."* → *"ROB saved the game."*, with `user://saves/slot0/slot.json` on disk holding `PewterCity_Frlg (45, 21)` and the real party.

**Text is verbatim** from `data/text/save.inc`, and SAVE is added to `FieldStartMenu` **unconditionally** — worth stating because the two entries above it are not. `BuildNormalStartMenu` gates POKéDEX and POKéMON on flags and then adds SAVE with no condition at all (`start_menu.c:349`): you can save before you own a single Pokémon. G.01 uses an EMPTY flag store, because a fixture with the flags set could not tell an unconditional entry from a gated one.

⚠️ **THE "SAVING…" PAGE IS KEPT DELIBERATELY EVEN THOUGH THIS WRITE IS INSTANT.** On the GBA it warns about real flash memory; here `SaveManager.save` returns in well under a millisecond. It stays because it is the beat the player expects between the yes and the confirmation, and dropping it would make a successful save read as though nothing happened. ⚠️ `gText_AlreadySavedFile` is **deliberately not asked** — source asks it because the GBA has ONE save; with three slots the overwrite question belongs to slot SELECTION (L3), and asking it here would be asking about a slot the player never chose.

⚠️ **`\p` IS A PAGE-BREAK CONTROL CODE, NOT A STRING ESCAPE.** `SAVE_FAILED` was first written as one string containing source's own `\p` and GDScript refused to parse the whole file — every other multi-page line in this scene is already an Array for exactly this reason.

⚠️ **BUG FOUND BY THE LIVE DRIVE #1: A YES/NO OPENED OUTSIDE THE SCRIPT VM HAD NO INPUT DRIVER AT ALL, AND IT IS OLDER THAN L2.** The only driver lived inside `_drive_script`'s `WAIT_YES_NO` branch, so `[M27K K-b]`'s own gender question — *"Are you a boy? Or are you a girl?"*, which does `_yes_no.open()` then `await _yes_no.chosen` with no VM running — **could never be answered from the keyboard**. This is the SECOND instance of K-b's exact defect, found the same way and missed the same way: **a driver that reaches past the input layer cannot test the input layer.** K-b's drive called `confirm()` directly, as did mine until the flow visibly stalled. Fixed with a non-VM yes/no block in `_process`, placed ABOVE the message-box block because a yes/no draws over the message it is asking about (layer 85 vs 80) and must take input first.

⚠️ **BUG FOUND BY THE LIVE DRIVE #2: `{PLAYER}` RENDERED THE FALLBACK FOR A NAMED PLAYER.** The drive saved as a player called ROB and printed *"LEAF saved the game."* `TextBuffers.identity` is written in exactly ONE place — `OverworldSession.reset()` — so a session named through any other path left it pointing at a different object, and every one of the corpus's **1193 `{PLAYER}` uses** silently rendered the placeholder. Two references kept in step by hand, which is to say not kept in step. Fixed with `TextBuffers.active_identity()`: the static stays as an OVERRIDE (test sites set it directly, and a battle-side test has no session), falling back to `OverworldSession.identity`. H.01 deliberately CLEARS the override, since a fixture that set it could not see the bug at all.

⚠️ **PLAYTIME ACCUMULATES AS A FLOAT AND IS REPORTED AS AN INT.** Ticking `+= delta` into an int truncates every frame — at 60 fps that loses most of an hour per hour, which looks like a working counter right up until someone reads it. It ticks ABOVE every menu and script gate (a menu open is still time played) but BELOW the `_in_battle` return, which is a **stated hole**: battle time is not counted yet.

**Tests**: `m27l_save_test` 55 → **70/70**, new sections G and H. Three more guards break-tested, each failing exactly one assertion (gating SAVE → G.01; int playtime → G.06; dropping the identity fallback → H.01). ⚠️ Adding SAVE broke `m27i_i4_bag_screen_test` (48 → 44) — its E section asserted a 2-entry menu and a wrap to index 1; both now read the list rather than a literal, so the next entry added cannot break them again. Regression: all 12 overworld suites green.

⚠️ **STILL OPEN IN M27L**: **L3** — the title screen and slot-selection UI, NEW GAME wired to `run_new_game()`, CONTINUE wired to `apply()` + `position_of()`. `Overworld.active_slot` is a **stated stand-in at 0** until that exists, since nothing can have chosen a slot yet.

**[M27L L3 — the title screen and slot selection] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. A playthrough can be chosen, started and resumed.**

⚠️ **THIS SCREEN INVERTS SOURCE'S SHAPE, BECAUSE SOURCE HAS NO SLOTS.** `main_menu.c` knows one save and offers CONTINUE / NEW GAME / OPTION against it. Here the **slot is chosen first**, and what that slot already holds decides which action it is. The CONTINUE **card** is ported faithfully — source's own four fields in source's own order, PLAYER / TIME / POKéDEX / BADGES (`main_menu.c:274-277`), with TIME as `H:MM` the way `MainMenu_FormatSavegameTime` prints it.

⚠️ **A FINDING THAT BEARS ON ROB'S OWN STUB DECISION, RECORDED RATHER THAN ACTED ON UNILATERALLY**: `MainMenu_FormatSavegamePokedex` (`main_menu.c:2193`) gates the entire POKéDEX row on `FlagGet(FLAG_SYS_POKEDEX_GET)` — **source simply does not draw it before you own a Pokédex**, which in this project is never, M33 owning the dex. Rob's call (2026-08-03) was to show a stubbed row instead of fabricating a count, and that is what ships: the row reads `POKéDEX 0 (not yet tracked)`, visibly not a real number. `TitleScreen.SHOW_DEX_ROW` is the one line between the two behaviours, so switching to source's omission needs no rework. I.10 pins that the marker is present, so no later session reads the 0 as a count.

⚠️ **THE SLOT LIST CLAMPS WHERE SOURCE'S START MENU WRAPS, DELIBERATELY.** A wrap on a three-item list makes it easy to overshoot from the last slot onto the first — which may hold a real playthrough you are about to start a new game over. Break-tested: wrapping fails I.04.

**An empty slot is ONE line reading `NEW GAME`, not a card with four blanks in it** — blank fields read as a broken save rather than a free slot. `open()` **re-reads disk every time** rather than trusting what it had, so a slot deleted or corrupted since the last visit reads empty (break-tested: caching fails I.11).

**`begin_continue()` returns `pending_return`'s OWN SHAPE**, so CONTINUE reuses the post-battle resume path the overworld already has rather than growing a second one.

⚠️ **ONE GUARD CAME BACK VACUOUS AND THE FIX IS THE INTERESTING PART.** I.14 asserted only that continuing an EMPTY slot returns nothing — which stays true with the guard deleted, since `position_of({})` is empty either way. The real damage of dropping it is that `apply({})` runs and **wipes the live playthrough in memory**, so a mis-aimed CONTINUE on a blank slot would erase the session. I.14b now asserts the session SURVIVES, and re-injection fails exactly that one assertion. Same shape as L1's D.05b: the observable return value was not where the harm was.

**Tests**: `m27l_save_test` 72 → **87/87**, new section I. Five guards break-tested (wrap → I.04, blank card → I.02, cached slots → I.11, dropped dex marker → I.10, empty-slot continue → I.14b). Regression: all overworld suites green.

⚠️ **NOT WIRED TO BOOT, AND THAT IS A DECISION FOR ROB, NOT AN OVERSIGHT.** `project.godot`'s `main_scene` is `scenes/main.tscn`, which is the **battle SIMULATOR's** entry point ("Start Battle") — the RPG has never had a boot path at all. Pointing the project at a title screen would change what launching the project *means* for the simulator half, so `TitleScreen` ships reachable and tested but not installed. `Overworld.active_slot` remains a stated stand-in at 0 until that wiring exists.

**[M27L L4 — the boot path] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. The RPG is launchable.**

Live-driven end to end, both branches: `main.tscn` → **Start Adventure** → the slot list → a slot → the field. **NEW GAME** on empty slot 0 lands in the overworld with Oak's speech running; **CONTINUE** on slot 2 shows the real card —

```
   SLOT 3
      CONTINUE
      PLAYER   ROB
      TIME     1:02
      POKéDEX  0  (not yet tracked)
      BADGES   1
```

— and arrives in the field as ROB with the saved party and its **lead still on 7 HP**.

⚠️ **A SECOND ENTRY POINT, NOT A REPLACEMENT — Rob's call, 2026-08-03.** `run/main_scene` stays `scenes/main.tscn`, which is the battle SIMULATOR's entry and has been the only thing it launched since `[M23.0b]`. This project is two things sharing one binary, and repointing the boot scene would have changed what launching it MEANS for the simulator half. So `main.tscn` now offers **Start Battle** and **Start Adventure** side by side, both as real scene-tree nodes per this file's standing UI preference. J.01 pins that `main_scene` is unchanged, J.03 that both destinations actually resolve — a boot button pointing at a missing scene fails at the press, which is the worst moment to find out.

⚠️ **`active_slot` MOVED FROM THE OVERWORLD TO `OverworldSession`, AND IT HAD TO.** L2 held it on the field as a stated stand-in; a battle is a real scene swap, so the chosen slot would have been discarded by the first trainer fought and **the next SAVE would have written to slot 0** whatever the player picked. Break-tested: keeping the field's own copy fails J.05.

⚠️ **`pending_new_game` IS CONSUMED ON READ**, the same shape as `pending_return`. A flag left set would re-run Oak's speech **every time the overworld rebuilt — which is after every single battle**. Break-tested: reading without consuming fails J.07. The speech is fired `call_deferred` from `_ready` because the naming screen and message box are children of the field scene and cannot exist earlier.

**Starting a NEW GAME resets the session first**, so choosing it over a slot loaded earlier in the same run cannot inherit that slot's party and flags. A CONTINUE whose slot vanished between the listing and the press **re-opens the list** rather than loading an empty playthrough.

**Tests**: `m27l_save_test` 87 → **95/95**, new section J. Four guards break-tested, each failing exactly one assertion (field-local slot → J.05; unconsumed flag → J.07; `reset()` forgetting both → J.08; the RPG button skipping slot selection → J.04). Regression: all overworld suites green.

⚠️ **STILL OPEN, AND VISIBLE IN THE DRIVE:** a NEW GAME still lands with `[M27D D5]`'s **3-member debug party**, because `player_party()` lazily builds it whenever the party is null. That is the same scaffolding `[M27K K-c]`'s starter-slot hazard depends on, and it is what makes that hazard reachable — a real new game should start with an EMPTY party and receive the starter from Oak's script. Not new to L4, but L4 is the first thing that shows it to a player.

**[M27L L5 — an honest new game] COMPLETE — 2026-08-03. You start in your bedroom with nothing, like source.**

Live-driven: NEW GAME boots in `PalletTown_PlayersHouse_2F_Frlg` at local **(6, 6)**, standable, with **0 party members**.

⚠️ **THE DEBUG PARTY IS RETIRED AS THE DEFAULT, AND THIS CLOSES `[M27K K-c]`'s DISCLOSED HAZARD.** `player_party()` lazily built `[M27D D5]`'s 3-member team whenever the party was null, so **a brand new game arrived holding a team it was never given**. Source is unambiguous: `NewGameInitData` calls `ZeroPlayerPartyMons()` (`new_game.c:155`). That default is what made K-c's starter-slot problem reachable at all — `givemon` appended at slot 3 while `EventScript_GiveNicknameToStarter` hardcodes `setvar VAR_0x8004, 0` — so with an empty party the starter now lands in slot 0 and the script renames the Pokémon it just gave you. K.04 asserts that directly. ⚠️ **`build_debug_player_party()` still EXISTS** — no longer the default is a different thing from deleted, and tests and debug boots still want a team (K.03).

⚠️ **A NEW GAME STARTS IN THE BEDROOM, AND `start_map` IS DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED.** Source: `WarpToTruck` (`new_game.c:136`) sends FRLG to `MAP_PALLET_TOWN_PLAYERS_HOUSE_2F` at **(6, 6)** — the truck is Hoenn's opening and Kanto's is the bedroom. `NEW_GAME_MAP`/`NEW_GAME_CELL` are their own constants because `start_map` is the **debug/corridor boot** that M27C's whole seam story is built on; repointing it would move that scaffolding to answer a question about the new game. The flag is **peeked** here and **consumed** later in `_ready` — one read decides WHERE, the other decides WHEN.

⚠️ **TWO REAL BUGS FELL OUT OF BOOTING SOMEWHERE `start_map` DOES NOT NAME.** `_resolve_start_cell` validated `start_cell` against `start_map`, so it was checking the spawn tile **in a different town from the spawn** and silently falling back to "first walkable"; and `load_neighbours(start_map)` loaded the wrong map's seams. Both now use the actual boot map, which a battle return had also always needed.

⚠️ **THREE GUARDS CAME BACK VACUOUS, AND THE FIX IS THE GENERAL LESSON.** K.05-K.07 assert what `NEW_GAME_MAP`/`NEW_GAME_CELL` *hold*, and deleting the code that USES them failed nothing — a constant is not a behaviour. New **K.08-K.10 boot the real field** and assert where the player actually stands; re-injection now fails K.08/K.09. Same shape as L1's D.05b and L3's I.14: **the observable value was not where the harm was.**

**Tests**: `m27l_save_test` 95 → **105/105**, new section K. ⚠️ Retiring the default broke three suites that leaned on it (`m27f_stage4` 63→48 with a whole function aborting, `m27i_i5_party` 58→48, `m27o_field_poison` 57→42) — all three now seed the debug team **explicitly**, which is more honest than the implicit default ever was. Regression: all 13 overworld suites green.

## M27M — Map authoring tooling *(new block, scoped and approved 2026-07-30)*

**[M27M-T — trimmed TileSet] ⚠️ OUT OF SCOPE — Rob's call, 2026-08-09. CUT, not deferred.** Re-measured that day against the post-Part-C tree and the numbers came back BETTER than the original recon (6.1x tile definitions and 10.0x load, from real trimmed twins, against Rev 1's 4.0x/8.9x) — and it was cut anyway, with those numbers in hand. **So a future session finding it worthwhile has found nothing new and must not re-propose it.** Accepted cost: TileSet construction is ~21 ms per pair, so boot grows toward ~1.3 s once all 60 pairs are built. One correction from that pass is worth keeping independently of the tier, because other work can rely on it: ⚠️ **a metatile routes to exactly TWO of the three planes, never three** — the original recon multiplied by 3 and so overstated the work by 50%. Full detail in `docs/m27m_trimmed_tileset_recon.md` §0, kept as a record rather than a plan. Original scoping follows.

**[M27M-T — trimmed TileSet] SCOPED 2026-07-30, not built. ⚠️ Scope of record is `docs/m27m_trimmed_tileset_recon.md`.** Measured rather than estimated: a real trimmed twin of the Pallet Town pair loads in **1.71 ms against the full set's 15.25 ms — 8.9x**, 25 KB → 6 KB. Region-wide only **11,036 tile definitions are actually placed** against the 132,480 `create_tile()` calls made today (**4.0x**), and all fourteen corridor pairs together would build in **~31 ms** — about what ONE cold tileset costs now. Baked scenes need no re-bake (M27M2 made the TileSet an `ext_resource`), `check_bake_diff` and the overlay are unaffected, and no consumer iterates tiles. **The failure mode is SILENT and is the whole design constraint**: `set_cell` accepts a coord whose tile was never created, stores it faithfully, and renders nothing — so the trim pass must ship with its own coverage proof, not after. Three hazards are recorded there with measurements: the trim set must be computed region-wide per pair rather than per-bake (or a later map silently renders with holes), it must union `border[]` as well as `metatile[]` (**5 border ids region-wide appear in no map body**), and it conflicts head-on with M27M's authoring requirement — resolved by treating `trim`/`expand` as two idempotent operations on ONE artifact rather than shipping two files.

**A thirteenth M27 block, orthogonal to C's stitching work.** Added because this is Kanto with an ORIGINAL story: the region's 421 maps are imported, but new maps and new art are inevitable, and **there is currently no way to create either.** Scope of record while this is unbuilt: the approved mockup at `https://claude.ai/code/artifact/0cc55049-a0d4-4675-8b0c-eb33853611b0`. Nothing here is implemented.

**The architectural decision this rests on, Rob's call: THE SCENE BECOMES THE SOURCE OF TRUTH.** Import Kanto once, then hand the artifacts over to authoring rather than keeping a re-runnable pipeline. That retires machinery deliberately rather than by neglect — the baker's `_scene_divergence` guard and `check_bake_diff` both answer *"does this scene still contain only what the baker would produce?"*, and once maps carry hand-painted art **every map is non-reproducible by design**, so both become permanent false positives (the same failure fixed this session, but semantic and unfixable). `provenance` loses its guard role for the same reason. **`attr_explicit` matters MORE, not less** — it marks cells whose collision/elevation is an inherited guess, which is exactly what every newly painted cell is. Keep both guards until the Kanto import is declared done, then retire them on purpose. Accepted cost: a bug found later has no bulk-fix path except a script that rewrites `.tres` files directly.

**Painting already works and needs nothing built** — `_build_tileset` calls `create_tile` for EVERY tile in the atlas, not just used ones, so Godot's own tilemap editor can paint any metatile in a pair today. What is missing is everything downstream of the paint.

**Three findings that shaped the design.** (1) **A `behavior` custom data layer is already declared on every baked TileSet and never populated** — nothing writes it, nothing reads it; someone scaffolded this exact seam and stopped. So per-metatile attributes belong ON THE TILE via Godot's own custom data, not in a parallel resource keyed by id. (2) **Custom art needs almost no new convention**: reference metatile ids are bounded by the GBA's 10-bit mask (the corridor genuinely reaches 1023) while `MapData.metatile` is `PackedInt32Array`, so custom metatiles take ids ≥ 1024 as extra rows on the same per-pair atlas — `id % 32, id / 32` still resolves and `source_id == plane` is untouched. All 33 atlases are 0.9 MB, so headroom is not a concern. (3) **The two-plane paint is the trap.** A metatile routes to one or two of three planes, so painting a `NORMAL` one into Ground alone renders NOTHING — the identical bug that made half the C3 skirt invisible, waiting for the first person to hand-paint.

**Sub-tiers, in dependency order.** **M27M1 — populate the tile's custom data** (behaviour + layer type at bake time; finishes the half-built seam). **M27M2 — shared per-pair `TileSet` `.tres`**, replacing the copy currently embedded as a SubResource in each scene: 60 shared resources instead of 421, and adding art then updates one file rather than every consumer. **M27M3 — authoring layer + plane routing**: paint into one layer, a `@tool` distributes to the correct planes using the layer type now on the tile. **M27M4 — change detection into the review backlog**: compare painted ids against `MapData.metatile` — which earns its place NOT as a cache of the scene but as the record of what the collision and elevation were last authored AGAINST — then seed via the existing `author_cell_with_defaults()` and clear the explicit bits so the cell surfaces in `review_cells()`. Without it, painting a wall over a path leaves the cell walkable AND still marked confirmed. **M27M5 — new maps from Kanto art**: blank `.tscn` + `.tres` written straight to `scenes/maps/`, no importer and no JSON (`assets/maps/` is gitignored as regenerable output, so authored input cannot live there; §1.9's re-import policy already says *"authored maps simply are not in that set"*). **M27M6 — behaviour picker.** ⚠️ **NO METATILE PICKER WILL BE BUILT — Rob's call, 2026-08-09: *"I don't need the picker if I have the sync button and then later have a behavior overwrite ability."*** A visual palette for choosing ART is redundant: Godot's own tile palette already is one, and `Sync Painted Tiles` (M27M4) makes what you paint with it real. So M27M6 keeps ONLY its original narrow scope — **a named dropdown for choosing a BEHAVIOUR** (240-value enum, plain typed ints with no enum hint; `metatile_behavior.gd` already carries the generated `NAME_BY_ID` table) — and **merges into Part B**, which is the paint mode that applies it. Neither half is useful alone: a dropdown with nothing to paint into, or a brush that takes raw ints.

⚠️ **AND PART B'S PURPOSE IS NARROWED BY THE SAME DECISION.** It is no longer an authoring path — behaviour follows the metatile automatically and is right by construction for every Kanto tile. It is an **OVERRIDE**: the escape hatch for when the derived behaviour is wrong, and the only way to tag behaviour at all on M27M7's new art, which has no sidecar entry. That is a smaller job than "the behaviour brush" sounds, and it is why it sits after `pokemart` rather than before it. **M27M7 — new art**: 16x16 metatiles authored directly (NOT the GBA tiles+metatiles.bin encoding, which exists for hardware constraints that do not apply), appended past id 1023, each tagged once via M27M6.

**What stays a human's job, and why it is irreducible.** Measured across all 421 maps / 230,619 cells: behaviour and layer type vary by placement **0%** — per-metatile by construction, so a painted tile carries them. Collision varies **52.0%** and elevation **52.1%**, so they are properties of the POSITION and cannot be derived from the tile at all. That split is the whole design: tags travel with the tile, collision and elevation get a guess plus a review flag. **New art is the one exception** — a brand-new metatile has no behaviour or layer type anywhere, so both are authored ONCE PER TILE at ingestion, not per placement. Layer type is not derivable even in principle: whether the player walks in front of a tile or behind it is a decision.

**Two questions deliberately left open.** Whether painting directly into `Ground` should be blocked or silently routed; and whether an explicit skirt opt-out flag is worth having (see C3's note — the current empty-`border` mechanism works but cannot distinguish "deliberately none" from "lost its border").

**M27M2 SHIPPED EARLY, 2026-07-30 — it turned out to be the fix for C4's crossing stutter.** Each baked scene embedded its own `TileSet` as a `SubResource`, and building one is **2208 `create_tile()` calls** — a flat ~27 ms per scene load regardless of map size. `map_baker` now writes one shared `TileSet` per tileset PAIR to `assets/map_tilesets/` and references it as an `ext_resource`: **4 files cover the 8-map corridor, 60 would cover all 421**, replacing 421 embedded copies. `take_over_path()` after the save is load-bearing — without it the instance handed to the layers carries no `resource_path` and `ResourceSaver` writes it straight back into the scene as a `SubResource`, sharing nothing. Its original purpose is unchanged: adding custom art now updates one file per pair instead of every consumer.

**[M27M Part C — atlas deduplication] COMPLETE 2026-08-09. Scope of record is `docs/m27m_behaviour_scope.md` §4b; this is the index entry.** The atlases were a flattened composite of primary+secondary, so the primary's 640 metatiles were re-rendered into every pair — 81% of all atlas area. New `scripts/overworld/atlas_layout.gd` is now the ONE place that knows id → (source, coords): **six** TileSet sources per pair (0-2 primary, 3-5 secondary), with the secondary half re-based by 640. Both paint sites (`map_baker._paint`, `map_manager.set_metatile`) call it, and the duplicated `ATLAS_COLS = 32` is deleted from both. **47,424 atlas cells → 28,224; 1.22 MB → 0.63 MB.**

⚠️ **THE DESIGN'S HEADLINE ASSUMPTION — "the primary is stored once per primary tileset" — IS FALSE FOR ONE OF THE TWO PRIMARIES, and it was found by a PIXEL proof, not by the coverage check.** On hardware both tilesets share ONE tile/palette address space, so a primary metatile may name tile index ≥640 or palette slot ≥7 and pick up whatever the *currently paired* secondary put there — a deliberate idiom for a shared building tileset defining a per-building fixture. Rendered to a file it has no single answer. Measured across all 421 converted Kanto maps: **`general_frlg` 0 of 640 borrow; `building_frlg` 56 of 640, and 53 of those are placed, on 208 real cells.**

⚠️ **"BORROWS" IS NOT THE SAME AS "RENDERS WRONG", AND A FIRST REPORT OF THIS CONFLATED THEM.** Screenshot verification (2026-08-09) forced the correction.
