[Everything below this point predates the decomposition and is retained per this file's own convention of not rewriting history.] ⚠️ **THE SCOPE OF RECORD IS `docs/overworld_scope.md` (rev 11, 2026-07-28) — read that, not this row, before any overworld work.** Rev 11 added a full reference sweep for systems no prior scoping pass had named: an entire missing **new-game/endgame arc** (title screen, save-slot selection, starter choice, **player naming** — the concrete unblocker for the `_PLAYER_BACK_PIC = Leaf` placeholder — Hall of Fame, credits), **seven lead-Pokémon ability encounter modifiers that are ON at `GEN_LATEST`**, and five newly-surfaced systems with decisions attached (**badge-gated level caps** with a real default table, **Pokérus — enabled by default and driven by the day counter**, the **FRLG map-preview screen — effectively on for a Kanto project, with Kanto art already in the reference**, the dialogue **name box** — which lands on the Dialogue Manager boundary — and **NPC followers**, distinct from the Pokémon followers already ruled out). It also sized the confirmed exclusions at **~67,000 lines** of reference source and named five subsystems that were OUT by implication but never stated (trading, link/Union Room, Mystery Gift, Easy Chat, the link minigames). It merges Rob's own overworld scope document with `docs/m27_recon.md` and `docs/m27a_recon.md`, both of which are now SUPERSEDED and kept as history only. **Two claims repeated later in this row are confirmed WRONG and were corrected in rev 10**: (1) map/NPC/encounter **DATA is NOT authored — it is IMPORTED**; the reference holds **421 Kanto maps**, **62 FRLG tilesets** and **264 of 388 wild-encounter entries** for Kanto, all verified from source. The settled strategy is **import first, Rob authors tweaks after**, for geometry AND encounters; only *content and meaning* (who says what, rosters, story triggers) is authored from scratch. (2) The `pallettown.tscn` spike is **NOT the starting point** — it is hand-painted over third-party metatile art the real `map.bin` cannot address. **Also new in rev 10, and absent from every earlier scoping pass: FRLG metatile attributes are 32-bit while Hoenn's are 16-bit** (`METATILE_ATTR_BEHAVIOR_MASK_FRLG 0x000001ff`, `LAYER_MASK_FRLG 0x60000000`, plus a real `MapLayout.isFrlg` flag) — a Hoenn-shaped reader misparses every Kanto tileset **with nothing failing loudly**, making it the highest-severity silent failure in the importer. Rev 10 also adds a required **0–15 → 5-bucket elevation mapping table** deliverable, a **numbers-reconciliation gate** (§22.1) covering the map count and script-opcode count, and five already-researched battle-side carry-ins. [Original 2026-07-28 framing, superseded but kept per this file's own convention:] **HIGH-LEVEL SCOPING DONE 2026-07-28 — `docs/m27_recon.md`. Blocks and dependencies only, no sub-tiers, by Rob's explicit instruction; each block still needs its own scoping session, and Rob reviews before that happens.** Proposed decomposition: **M27A** map foundation (tile/metatile rendering, map format, collision, loading, connections/warps) → **M27B** player avatar and movement → **M27C** map objects and NPCs → **M27D** field script engine; **M27E** encounters/battle handoff and RETURN needs all four; **M27F** field UI and inventory needs D; **M27G** player identity is small and early. **Three findings that shape it.** (1) **There is an ABANDONED prior attempt in the tree**: `scenes/maps/pallettown.tscn`, dated 26 June — before essentially all the battle-UI work — a Godot-4 `TileMapLayer` over a hand-pulled metatiles PNG that is hand-painted (not generated from reference map data), collision-free (zero physics layers/terrain sets/custom data) and **referenced by nothing**. It is a spike, not a foundation, but it quietly implies a technical direction (native Godot tilemaps + hand-authored maps + third-party metatile art) that M27A should confirm or reject deliberately rather than inherit. (2) **The scope-shaping fact: this project is Kanto with an original story, so map/NPC/encounter DATA is AUTHORED while SYSTEMS are ported** — the same reasoning already recorded for trainers in `[M26B3-1]`. That inverts this project's usual balance: the reference stops being a spec to reproduce and becomes a design reference, so Step 0 expectations differ from every prior milestone, and content-authoring throughput matters as much as engine correctness. It is also why 940 reference maps do not imply 940 maps of work. (3) **Reference scale, for sizing**: 940 maps, a field script engine of **393 macros / 237 script commands** (comparable to the battle script engine this project already ported), and `event_object_movement.c` at **12,245 lines — the second-largest file in the entire source tree**, behind only `battle_script_commands.c`, which is the clearest signal that NPC movement is a real subsystem rather than a detail of the player controller. **Three boundary questions recorded for Rob** (doc §5): the **M27E-vs-M29 split** (M29 already owns "Encounters & catching"; the workable line is M27 owns the TRIGGER — grass step, transition, return — and M29 owns CONTENT and MECHANICS, but the two rows currently overlap as written); **what M27 explicitly excludes**, given M28/M29/M30/M31/M32/M33/M34 all claim adjacent scope and several are hard to build without touching M27 systems (HM field effects, M32, especially); and **save interaction**, since the overworld is the first system with genuinely persistent state (position, flags, variables, inventory) while M34 owns save/load — M27D's flags/variables in particular have nowhere to live today. **Recommended next step: scope M27A alone first** — it carries the decision every other block inherits and is the one place where getting it wrong is expensive to undo. **Already-researched items that live inside M27** (recorded so they aren't re-derived): the Mugshot transition → M27E, per-ball send-out particles → end of M27, shiny Pokémon → encounter-generation side, and the 86 unpulled Kanto/FRLG trainer sprites → whenever the Kanto roster is authored. **Also worth knowing: M25h-1.4/1.5's real full-screen Bag and Party views are a genuine head start on M27F, not a rebuild.** **Battle transitions, incl. the Mugshot effect** (added 2026-07-26): **no battle-transition item existed anywhere in this roadmap before now** — confirmed by direct search, zero mentions. Transitions are the overworld→battle wipe animations, so they belong to this milestone's own encounter-triggering/glue scope rather than to M26's in-battle graphical mandate. Investigated 2026-07-26 while scoping M26B3, and recorded here so the finding isn't lost: `B_TRANSITION_MUGSHOT` (`include/battle_transition.h:38`) is the Gym-Leader/Elite-Four entry animation, selected by `GetTrainerBattleTransition()` only when `DoesTrainerHaveMugshot(trainerId)` passes (`src/battle_setup.c:861-871`) — ordinary trainers fall through to Team Magma/Aqua-specific and generic transitions. **It uses NO dedicated portrait asset**: it scales and rotates the same 64×64 trainer front pic the battlefield sprite uses (`GetTrainerFrontPicMugshotCoords` / `GetTrainerFrontPicMugshotRotation`, `src/battle_transition.c:2589-2660`) across coloured diagonal banners driven by an HBlank scanline effect (`HBlankCB_Mugshots`, `:2303`) over `sEliteFour_Tileset`, with five per-trainer palette variants (`MUGSHOT_COLOR_PURPLE/GREEN/PINK/BLUE/YELLOW`, `:875-881`) plus a player-gender palette. **This closes the question of whether a "portrait" concept exists anywhere in the reference: it does not.** There are exactly two trainer-sprite uses, both the same 64×64 front pic — the battlefield sprite (M26B3) and this transition. This is the milestone where the project expands from battle-simulator-only into the full RPG game loop. **Per-ball send-out particle animations** (added 2026-07-26 at Rob's request, deferred to the END of this milestone): the eight non-Poke-Ball particle variants. Only the Poke Ball's is built (M26B3-6a); the other eight are unreachable until ball variety exists. Fully researched and tabulated in M26B3-6a's own entry so nothing needs re-deriving — nine distinct `particleAnimationFunc`s plus a per-ball `animNums` selecting which frame of the shared sheet, covering Great/Ultra/Safari/Master/Dive/Timer's spiral fan-outs (rotating angle, some with elliptical x/y radii) and Repeat/Premier's own step functions. Sequenced here rather than in M26 because it is cosmetic depth with no consumer until catch/ball variety lands. Source itself flags the area unfinished: `TaskFunc particleAnimationFunc; //To Do: assign and possibly create different particles`. **Ball seals are NOT part of this** — they are a Gen IV feature absent from this reference entirely; see M26B3-6a's own note. **Shiny Pokémon** (new 2026-07-24, from a direct source/current-implementation comparison recon — see this file's own battle-screen-display-gaps recon note below): `TrainerPartyMon.is_shiny` is real, already parsed from actual trainer data, but `BattlePokemon` has no equivalent field, no shiny sprite/palette variant ever loads, and source's own `TryShinyAnimation` (the sparkle played on every send-out, `battle_anim_throw.c`) has no equivalent here — a `shiny.png` sparkle overlay sits unused in two vendored asset packs. Scoped here rather than under M26's battle-UI-polish umbrella because shininess is fundamentally an encounter-generation concern (rolled once, at Pokémon-creation time — trainer party load here, and eventually wild-encounter generation under M29) that battle merely DISPLAYS; the display-side consequence (sprite variant + sparkle animation on send-out) belongs here too rather than being split across two milestones. | `---` |
| M28 | Evolution — **SCOPED 2026-08-04, scope of record is `docs/m28_recon.md`.** Boundary unchanged from 2026-07-28: the evolution MECHANICS stay here; the evolution *scene* is overworld presentation triggered from **M27H**'s battle-return path (confirmed the reference itself doesn't draw this line — species/stat mutation and the visual state machine are one function there, `evolution_scene.c`; the split is this project's own architectural choice). Real reference scope: **8 `EVO_*` methods × 39 `IF_*` conditions**, but this project's own 386-entry `data/evolutions.json` (already extracted, pre-existing, previously only consumed by Eviolite's `CanEvolve` gate) uses just **9 conditions × 3 methods** — the other 30 conditions are genuine Gen 4-9-era noise for this roster, not a surface to build toward. **Trade evolution needs ZERO new infrastructure** — all 13 `trade`-method entries already have a parallel `item`-method entry to the identical target (`ITEM_LINKING_CORD` + named items), confirmed by a full programmatic scan, not sampled. **A real correction to common assumption, confirmed from source**: Pokémon DO learn a move on evolving (`MonTryLearningNewMoveEvolution`, `src/pokemon.c:6360`, the identical function/level-0-wildcard rule this project's own M20b `_try_learn_move_at_level` already implements) — moves are not just carried over untouched. HP follows the identical flat-additive-delta rule as level-up (literally the same `CalculateMonStats` function source-side). Multiple-eligible-evolution resolution is **first-table-order-match-wins**, an explicit, source-commented departure from vanilla. Nickname auto-update maps almost for free onto this project's own `nickname`/`display_name()` split from M27K K-c (`nickname == old species_name` → still-default check). **3 real gaps found and flagged, not fixed**: no Everstone/held-item evolution-block exists anywhere; Shedinja (`EVO_SPLIT_FROM_EVO`) isn't representable in the current schema, leaving it permanently unobtainable; friendship never increases/decreases anywhere in this codebase (already independently flagged in `docs/m18_5h_recon.md`), so `IF_MIN_FRIENDSHIP` evolutions are checkable only against each species' static starting value. `IF_TIME`/`IF_NOT_TIME` have no real backing clock — the reference's own `GetTimeOfDay()` is a persistent save-state RTC clock, not wall-clock, and the `AnimTask_GetTimeOfDay` hit in this project's own M36 animation VM is a coincidental namesake with zero connection to it. Proposes M28a (core mutation primitive, extending Transform's `_reset_mon_species`/`_reset_mon_stats`/`_reset_mon_ability` precedent into a *permanent*, not switch-reverted, variant) → M28b (level-up dispatch, reuses M20b) → M28c (a narrow item-trigger seam, deliberately NOT blocked on M27I's full Bag UI) → M28d (trade items — likely collapses into M28c, same mechanism) → M28e (deferred: Beauty/time/region conditions). 5 decisions for Rob in the doc's own §6, the two headline recommendations being: build a real Everstone now (cheap, large behavioral gap without it) and defer a persistent in-game clock rather than build one just for 2 Eevee evolutions. | `---` |

| M35 | **Advanced Battle Systems** — consolidates every exclusion/deferral flagged during M24 scoping (`docs/m24_recon.md`), not new discovery. **EXPANDED 2026-07-28: M35 now also explicitly owns the BATTLE SIMULATOR's own game modes and difficulty**, per Rob's call — the RPG's difficulty is settled and deliberately narrow (see M27J: Reduced/Normal/Bonus EXP variants, Nuzlocke mode, a toggleable per-battle item limit, and a universal in-battle speed multiplier), and everything competitive-flavoured belongs here instead. That includes the eight reference alternate-mode flags declined for the RPG (`B_FLAG_INVERSE_BATTLE`, `B_FLAG_AI_VS_AI_BATTLE`, `B_FLAG_SLEEP_CLAUSE`, `B_FLAG_NO_WHITEOUT`, `B_VAR_NO_BAG_USE`, `B_VAR_WILD_AI_FLAGS`, `B_BADGE_BOOST`, `B_FLAG_SKY_BATTLE`) — sleep clause and inverse battles in particular are simulator features, which is why declining them on the RPG side was safe. Full detail in `docs/overworld_scope.md` §29. Consolidated items: **Trainer Pools** (procedural/pool-based party generation — species clause, role tags, pick/prune rules; confirmed zero real usage across all 855 base trainers but real expansion infrastructure, deferred per `docs/m24_recon.md` §6.1); **full AI-flag engine** (the complete 34-flag bitmask AI system — OMNISCIENT, SMART_SWITCHING, PREDICT_MOVE, PREDICT_SWITCH, SMART_TERA, etc. — beyond the narrow 6-combination extension M24 ships; deferred per §6.2, flagged there as a candidate to pick back up starting around M31 onward); **facility/special trainers** (Trainer Tower, Trainer Hill, Battle Frontier trainers, Secret Base trainers — own money-formula branch — and roaming/`AI_FLAG_SAFARI`/`AI_FLAG_FIRST_BATTLE` special cases, none part of the core 855-trainer `trainers.party` dataset M24 covers; deferred per §6.3 — **the dedicated recon session §6.3 asked for is now DONE, 2026-08-04: `docs/m35_battle_frontier_recon.md` covers all 7 Battle Frontier facilities (Tower/Dome/Factory/Palace/Pike/Pyramid/Arena) plus the shared BP-economy/symbol/streak/trainer-pool/AI-flag/Apprentice infrastructure underneath them, with per-facility port-cost verdicts and 7 open scope questions for Rob in its own §5 — nothing implemented yet, and Trainer Hill/Battle Tent/Secret Base trainers/roaming trainers are explicitly flagged as still needing their own separate recon (that doc's §4), not covered by this pass**); **full rematch/postgame progression system** (the persistent, save-state-backed rematch-tier mechanic — `trainerRematches[]`, `HasTrainerBeenFought`, gym-leader rematch escalation; M24 ships base trainers only, deferred per §6.5 pending M34 or an interim save-state mechanism). **Mid-battle trainer-slide messages** (added 2026-07-26, deferred here out of M26B3's rework scope — Rob's call on placement): the reference has a whole conditional trainer-dialogue system where the opponent's sprite slides back in mid-battle to comment on the fight. Triggers are a real enum checked via `ShouldDoTrainerSlide(battler, TRAINER_SLIDE_*)` at first-turn events (`src/battle_main.c:3798-3817`) and in end-turn (`src/battle_end_turn.c:1428-1434`) — including `TRAINER_SLIDE_LAST_LOW_HP`, `TRAINER_SLIDE_LAST_HALF_HP`, `TRAINER_SLIDE_PLAYER_LANDS_FIRST_CRITICAL_HIT`, `TRAINER_SLIDE_ENEMY_MON_UNAFFECTED`, plus faint-time hooks (`trytrainerslidemsgfirstoff`/`trytrainerslidemsglaston`, `data/battle_scripts_1.s:2741, 2817, 2852`). Scripts at `data/battle_scripts_2.s:273-311` use `trainerslidein` / `handletrainerslidemsg ... PRINT_SLIDE_MESSAGE` / `trainerslideout`. **Placed in M35 rather than given its own milestone** because M35 is already this project's declared consolidation point for trainer-depth deferrals (Trainer Pools, the full AI-flag engine, facility trainers, rematch progression) and this is the same category — per-trainer behavioural richness beyond the base 855-trainer dataset. It needs the trainer-sprite slide-in/slide-out mechanism from **M26B3-4** to exist first, plus a per-trainer message data structure this project does not have. **Not gathered during the 2026-07-26 recon**: the full `TrainerSlideType` enum listing and the `sTrainerSlides` data structure — a future session scoping this must start with its own Step 0 on both. See `docs/m24_recon.md` for full reasoning on each item — not duplicated here. **Optional/nice-to-have, explicitly lower-priority than every item above and NOT required for M35**: Secret Power/Nature Power/Camouflage's shared `gBattleEnvironment`-analog blocker (the reason all 3 are currently permanently excluded) would incidentally be removed by even the minimal environment-ID infrastructure M23.11 Phase 5's background work builds — flagged only for possible future prioritization whenever Phase 5 lands, per `docs/m23_11_phase5_recon.md` §1.3; not scoped, not scheduled. **Hail/Snow collapse — the standing open question behind Snowscape** (added 2026-07-27, surfaced by M26B4's weather-animation work; **optional, not scoped, not scheduled**, same standing as the item above): this project models source's genuinely different Hail and Snow as one `WEATHER_HAIL` constant. Source keeps them distinct — **Hail deals end-of-turn chip damage and has no stat effect; Snow deals no chip and instead grants +50% Defense to Ice-types** — the latter being a mechanic this project has never implemented in any form. The collapse was made a **permanent design decision by Rob in `[D2 batch]`** (confirmed then via a full grep sweep that no separate Snow mechanic existed anywhere), and that decision is **not reopened here** — this note exists only because the same gap has now surfaced from three independent directions (`[D1 cheap clusters]`'s own flagged-gap paragraph, `[M17c]`'s Snow Warning, and now M26B4), and each new consumer inherits it silently rather than re-deciding it. **Already shipping with the simplification, per this file's own entries**: Snow Warning and Slush Rush and Ice Body (`[M17c]`), Aurora Veil's hail gate (`[M16c]`), Snow Cloak's chip-damage exemption (`[M17n-2]` follow-up), Solar Beam/Blade's weather halving (`[D2 batch]`), Forecast (`[M17n-10]`), the `EFFECT_WEATHER` cluster and Snowscape (move 809, which sets hail here) (`[D1 cheap clusters]`). **M26B4's own resolution, for the record**: `snowflakes.png` IS pulled and Snowscape keeps its own authentic move animation, while the per-turn weather replay follows weather STATE and so shows hail — so a Snowscape user sees snowflakes once, then hail each turn. That is a **known, accepted consequence of the collapse, not a defect**; it is recorded at the asset itself (`scripts/gen_weather_effect_sprites.py`'s own header) and pinned by `m26_b4_1_weather_asset_test`'s D.01/D.02, so a future session cannot "fix" it by deleting the asset or remapping Snowscape without first revisiting the collapse. **If it is ever revisited**, the real cost is a new `WEATHER_SNOW` constant plus re-deciding every consumer listed above, plus building the +50%-Ice-Defense mechanic from scratch — sized here only so a future session need not re-derive it; see `docs/m26_b4_recon.md` for the full weather recon. **Real battle-end flow, confirmed no dedicated screen exists in source** (found while scoping M26's own menu-chrome work, `battle_main.c`): source has no separate Win/Lose screen at all — `HandleEndTurn_BattleWon`/`HandleEndTurn_BattleLost` just run ordinary battle-script text (defeat message, money-received text) in the SAME message box already on screen, then `BeginFastPaletteFade`/`FadeOutMapMusic` straight into `ReturnFromBattleToOverworld` — no distinct window art, nothing to pull. This project's own "Win/Lose screen + Play Again button" is a genuine invention, needed only because this is a standalone battle simulator with no overworld to fade back into; giving it real chrome (like M26's other menu work) would be inventing fake authenticity for a screen that was never authentic to begin with. Deferred here rather than to M26 specifically because the REAL source-accurate battle-end behavior is inseparable from systems M35 already owns — money-received text, seen-flag updates, and (directly) the rematch/postgame progression system (`trainerRematches[]`, `HasTrainerBeenFought`) this milestone's own consolidated scope already covers above. When M35 is scoped, revisit the end-of-battle flow as part of wiring those systems in, rather than treating it as a standalone chrome pass. | `---` |
| M36 | **Move Animation Engine** — created 2026-07-29 by Rob's resolution of the 5 decisions in `docs/m26_f1_recon.md` §7, exercising the split-out option M26F's header always reserved (M26F1 keeps only its balance/setup fix; M26 closes without waiting on animation batches). ⚠️ **SCOPE OF RECORD IS `docs/m26_f1_recon.md` — read that, not this row.** This row was **restructured 2026-07-30 (Rob-approved)**: it had reached **51,224 characters on one table line**, 4.6% of a file already flagged as near its size cap, with every batch appending another paragraph to the same cell. The detail now lives in the recon doc, which was already the declared scope of record and already carried fuller versions of nearly every entry; the four sections that had no home there (M36D batches 1-3, M36-S, M36-H, the Essentials fallback note) were relocated **verbatim** rather than summarised. Nothing was lost or reworded. **A tiered faithful port** of the reference's battle-anim system with a GRACEFUL-FALLBACK contract — any move referencing an unimplemented behavior plays the current generic hit-effect, so the full roster is covered at every stage and fidelity only grows. Fidelity bar: 1x GBA frame-accurate. **Status: 779 of 845 IN-SCOPE moves playable (92.2%) as of M36D batch 39, 2026-08-03. **SCOPE, Rob 2026-08-03: Z-Moves and Max Moves are OUT entirely** — the cut is source-exact at `FIRST_Z_MOVE = MOVES_COUNT_GEN9` (id 847 Malignant Chain is the last in-scope move, 848 Breakneck Blitz the first excluded), removing 87 of the 932 bound moves. Restating against the new denominator LOWERED the figure (91.3% of 932 -> 91.1% of 845) because 12 of the 87 excluded moves were already playable; the batch-37 gain then took it to 91.7%. Deferrals are all ONE surface: `AnimTask_FakeOut`, `AnimTask_MoveTargetMementoShadow`, the spotlight trio (batch 26), Rapid Spin's elevation (batch 27) and Acid Armor (batch 28) are every one of them a WIN0/WIN1 stencil or per-scanline DMA effect; `AnimTask_SketchDrawMon` (batch 31) joins them. Three exceptions sit outside that surface: `AnimTask_TransformMon` (batch 28, hardware MOSAIC plus a species-sheet swap), the three multi-phase SPAWNERS still open after batch 34 (LeafBlade / AirCutter / EruptionLaunchRocks — portable, but a batch's work each; WaterSport / BrineRain / CreateIons closed in batch 34), and `AnimTask_CreateBestowItem` (draws the player's real held-item icon). Nothing is deferred for an unread step function — building that surface would clear all of them at once.** Iconic Gen 1-3 closed at 70/70; Tier 2 (remaining Gen 1-3) and Tier 3 (Gen 4+) both past 50%. **Sub-tiers** — A Extraction ✅ · B Runtime core ✅ · C Core behavior batch ✅ · D Batched expansion 🔶 ONGOING (39 batches + deferral clearance; the per-scanline surface was built in batch 37) · E Backgrounds & screen effects ✅ (E1/E2/E3) · **S SFX & audio surface — SCOPED 2026-08-04, `docs/m36_s_recon.md`; nothing built.** Every sound in the reference engine (move SFX and BGM alike) is a compiled tracker "song," never a standalone clip — but the checked-in tree DOES carry real, human-readable `.mid` files per move SFX (`sound/songs/midi/`, 132 `se_m_*`), which a raw play-back gets the wrong TIMBRE for since the real instrument sample lives in a separate `voicegroups` layer (`sound/direct_sound_samples/`) — rendering one from the reference tree alone means building a small MIDI+sample synthesizer, not a file-format decoder. **147 distinct `SE_*` move-SFX constants, ~3,546 call sites (~24x reuse)** — orthogonal to M36's own 779/845 playability figure, since sound has always been a zero-cost no-op. **The M36 VM already has fully-built, correctly-shaped hooks for this** — confirmed directly in `scripts/battle/anim/anim_script_vm.gd`'s own `sound_cues()` accumulator and `anim_behaviors.gd`'s `createsoundtask` dispatch (`_sound_immediate`/`_sound_cry_wait`, the latter correctly modeling that a cry-family sound task blocks script pacing even with no audio playing) — no VM/opcode changes needed, only a consumer. **Recommended real-asset source: the vendored `Essentials_v19.1` pack, not the reference tree's own MIDI+voicegroup data** — `Audio/SE/Anim/` (137 real files, `.ogg`/`.mp3`) is directly playable already, at the cost of a real per-move name-mapping decision (a mix of literal move-name matches and a hand-curated generic-effect table, the same shape `gen_moves.py`'s own name resolution already does) rather than a 1:1 filename join. **Cries are a real, separate, and genuinely SIMPLER subsystem — recommended as its own milestone item, not folded into M36-S**: standalone per-species `.wav` files with no synthesis needed (1,161 in the reference tree, 655 in Essentials, ALL-CAPS-species-named — maps almost for free onto the existing `species_name_to_id.json` bridge), despite covering far more entries than move SFX. Proposed phasing: S1 (a generic `SfxPlayer` reading `sound_cues()`, `AudioStreamPlayer2D` for the pan data the VM already captures — `SOUND_PAN_ATTACKER`/`_TARGET` — verified with placeholder tones first) → S2 (the real Essentials-sourced move-SFX pull + name-mapping) → S3 (cries, as their own item) → S4 (stretch, NOT recommended near-term: a from-scratch MIDI+voicegroup synth against the reference tree itself, the only path here needing real DSP work). 4 open decisions recorded in the doc's own §5, each with a recommendation (split cries out; Essentials over reference-tree synthesis; a silent no-op fallback is fine for SFX-ambiguous moves, matching M36's own established graceful-fallback philosophy; build real attacker/target panning now since the data's already free). · **H Hand-authored animations — RESEARCHED, NOT STARTED** (escape hatch for moves whose port reads badly; diverges from the 1x fidelity bar by design, so it is a recorded exception rather than a drift). **A session handoff exists at `docs/handoff.md`** — live M36 state, ranked next actions, the traps that are not obvious from this file, and an explicit list of what is NOT verified. Written 2026-08-03; it covers M36 only and says so, since M27 was being built in parallel by Rob. **Where to look in `docs/m26_f1_recon.md`:** §5 proposal and §7 decisions · one section per sub-tier and per M36D batch · **`M36 — open look-calls and capability gaps`** (three appearance judgements awaiting Rob, plus the one capability gap) · **`M36 — running lists`** (upstream bugs reproduced deliberately · deferred behaviors and why · disclosed divergences — each a lookup that previously existed only inside batch prose) · `M36D batch ordering` (Decision 5's phase order amended by Rob to yield-ordering, 2026-07-30). **Standing rules for this milestone**, kept here because they govern how the next batch is run rather than recording what a past one did: (1) **Judge a batch by the shared machinery it retires, not the move count** — a behavior only completes a move if it was that move's LAST blocker, so the greedy ranking measures reach, not yield. (2) **Read each target move's ACTUAL missing set** before selecting; inferring from family names produced batch 5's recorded miss. (3) **Ask at Step 0 which candidates mutate a BATTLER** — visibility, displacement, scale/rotation or blend. That is the leak class this port has hit five times, and the VM's four restore nets exist because of it. (4) **Defer rather than guess** when a step function has not been read in full; batch 10 deferred five and batch 11 ported four of them correctly as a result. (5) **Do not "fix" a reproduced upstream bug or a disclosed divergence** without checking the running lists first. (6) **"grep found nothing" is a statement about the PATTERN, not the source.** Batch 19 added a second dress: **a missing OUTPUT file is not an absent INPUT** — three backgrounds were recorded across two batches as "never pulled" when the generator was refusing them and saying so on every run. Run the tool before describing why its output is missing. Three of batch 15's four deferrals were search failures (`static void` vs `void`; `struct Sprite*` vs `struct Sprite *`), not hard code. Defer for an unread step function, never for an unsuccessful search — they produce the same empty terminal. (7) **An assertion that cannot fail is indistinguishable from one that passes.** A spawning test guarding on `if node != null:` can skip silently — batch 13 found EIGHT wrong template names hiding 26 never-run assertions this way. Resolve template names by CALLBACK from `templates.json`, never by guessing, and keep `_test_every_template_name_resolves` green. **Batches 23 and 24 both shipped a vacuous guard that only the INJECTION caught, and the lesson behind them is the one to watch: a guard whose injection you had to hand-pick is a guard you have not tested.** Batch 23's lattice check asserted only "starts above the screen" (true of any downward march) and its zigzag check read the column-to-column jump as a sweep (so a bolt with no alternation reported one). Batch 24's phase check compared two sprites of DIFFERENT AGES, which deviate differently whether or not the mechanism under test exists at all. Injecting the plausible misreading is not a formality after the tests pass — it is the only thing separating a guard from a decoration; when the first injection passes, suspect the assertion before crediting the code. **THE PREVENTIVE FORM, which is what actually works: derive each assertion from the NEGATION, not from the truth.** The habit that produces weak guards is writing "what does the correct version do?" and asserting that. Ask instead "what would the specific plausible WRONG version do differently?" and assert the difference — ideally by writing the injection BEFORE the assertion. Every rule-(7)/(13) instance recorded here would have been caught at authoring time by that one change, without needing to be rediscovered per batch. (10) **A `Sin()` in a step function does not mean the sprite waves — the ARGS decide.** Batch 27's Sucker Punch test asserted a vertical wave reasoned from the sine call, and failed: both real call sites pass amplitude ZERO, so the term is inert wherever it is actually reached. Port the term, but check what the scripts feed it before describing the motion — the same discipline as rule (2). (11) **Upstream duplicates functions rather than calling them; check before porting.** Seven batches have turned up four verbatim groups (`AnimGunkShotParticles`≡`AnimToTargetInSinWave`, `AnimGrassKnot`≡`AnimSuckerPunch`, and `AnimForcePalm`≡`AnimPunishment`≡`AnimGunkShotImpact` — three names, one implementation), identical line for line. Register an alias and assert the identity, so a later session cannot quietly produce a second implementation to keep in step. (13) **A fixture where two competing rules AGREE cannot tell them apart.** Batch 31's Helping Hand guard used a stage where the partner rule and the side rule both give the same answer, so deleting the partner rule passed; batch 32's Dragon Rush guard asserted only that two sides DIFFER, which is true whichever battler you key on, because attacker and target are always opposite in singles. When a claim is "keyed on X, not Y", build the fixture where X and Y disagree — or pin the direction absolutely against source. Batch 38's "it moved horizontally" (true at any fixed-point scale) and batch 39's "it accelerates" (true of constant gravity as well as quadratic) are the same shape and were each caught only by injection — see rule (7)'s preventive form. Batch 39's took TWO rewrites to find an assertion that discriminates at all, which is the cost of writing the guard before knowing what it must exclude. (15) **A fixture that cannot express a claim's NEGATION must say so at the assertion.** Odor Sleuth's clones mirror because their phases start 180 degrees apart, not because the phases step opposite ways — only x is drawn, and `cos(t+128) == -cos(t)` either way, so the step directions are invisible here (b34). Zen Headbutt's "not side-mirrored" claim is invisible for a fixture reason instead: `FakeStage.facing_sign()` is a fixed 1.0 (b35). **Distinct from (7) and (13)** — there the assertion was weak and could be strengthened; here the claim is true, load-bearing and untestable. Pin what IS observable and state at the assertion that the rest rests on the source read alone, so a green test never implies coverage it lacks. (14) **Sample points matter as much as assertions.** Batch 32's spin check sampled at the sine's minimum, where stepping either direction moves Y identically, and reported a correct mirror as a failure-free match. Check what the maths is doing at the frame you chose. (12) **Which ARG REGISTER a query task answers on is per-task, not a convention.** Batch 30 found `AnimTask_IsTargetPartner` and `AnimTask_GetLycanrocForm` writing arg 0 while every neighbour writes arg 7 — correct in both cases, because those two are read by a `jumpargeq 0` on the very next line and `jumpargeq` does not reload the registers. Normalising either direction breaks real consumers. Read the call site, not the family. (9) **A comment saying "nothing consumes this" is a claim with an expiry date.** Batch 24 found `AnimTask_StartSinAnimTimer` registered as a literal no-op because, when ported, nothing read the phase register it drives — true then, false the moment `_to_target_in_sin_wave` landed, and Flamethrower had been rendering a lockstep flame stream ever since. The same batch found the VM's `_load_args` clearing all eight arg registers, which reads as hygiene and silently severs source's one cross-command channel (`gBattleAnimArgs` is a persistent global; `Cmd_createsprite` writes only the args it was given). **When a port stubs something out because it has no consumer, the stub is a bet on the future, so say so at the stub and re-check it when a plausible consumer arrives.** (8) **A silent no-op edit is the same defect in a third dress.** Rule (6) itself failed to apply once because a quote-escaping mismatch made `str.replace` match nothing, and nothing errored. Assert the anchor exists and verify the file changed. | `---` |

## M27P — Overworld polish *(new block, opened 2026-08-07 — OPEN AND EMPTY BY DESIGN)*

⚠️ **THIS BLOCK IS A REVIEW SURFACE FOR ROB, NOT A SCOPED WORK LIST.** It
exists so overworld polish candidates have one named home instead of
accumulating as loose notes in other blocks' headers. **Every item that lands
here is in/out by Rob's call** — nothing is committed to by being written
down, and any candidate may be cut outright, deferred, or moved to the block
that really owns it.

⚠️ **DELIBERATELY EMPTY — no speculative content added**, following **M25i**'s
own precedent in this file ("deliberately left open/unscoped, no speculative
content added"). A block opened with a pre-populated candidate list would read
to a later session as scope Rob had already approved, which is the exact
confusion the C/T/A key's `A` slot exists to prevent. Items are added by Rob,
or proposed to Rob and added only once he says so.

**Sequencing is not assumed.** `M27O` carries its own "sequenced at the end of
M27 at Rob's direction" note, so whether polish runs before or after whiteout
is an open question for Rob — not something this block's position in the file
decides.

**Standing rules apply unchanged**: the C/T/A key above governs any row this
block grows, and rule 1 — never fill `A` — is in force here as everywhere
else.


## M27Q — Entity & event authoring *(scoped 2026-08-08; Q1–Q4 BUILT the same day, Q5 deferred indefinitely)*

**Where this block stands, 2026-08-08.** `Q1` `Q2` `Q3` `Q4` are built and
green; **`Q5` is deferred indefinitely by Rob and is not scheduled work**. The
paragraphs below were written as a proposal and are kept as the scope of
record — where a decision was later taken, it is marked inline at the tier
rather than by rewriting the proposal around it.

⚠️ **THE `A` SLOT IS STILL EMPTY FOR ALL OF IT.** Built and tested is not
approved, and one thing in particular has NEVER BEEN SEEN IN PLAY: Q1 changed
every trainer's AI, leaving 1,205 of 1,477 measurably weaker, and no human has
fought one. That is the largest unverified claim in this block.

**Four things were found that were not in the plan**, each recorded at its own
tier: the `CHECKVIABILITY` importer bug (80 trainers silently missing a flag
this project already implements), the `@tool` placeholder bug (which made every
Q2/Q3 affordance on `TrainerData` invisible), the trainer-class dropdown
data-loss bug caught by its own coverage assertion, and the overlay
contamination footgun, now closed structurally rather than by discipline.

⚠️ **SCOPED BY RECON, ORIGINALLY AWAITING ROB'S IN/OUT CALL.** Nothing below is built and
nothing is approved. Every number is measured; the command that produced it is
named. The `A` slot stays empty here as everywhere else.

**Why its own block rather than sub-tiers of M27M.** M27M is authoring tooling
and this is authoring tooling, so folding in was the obvious move — but every
one of `M27M1`–`M27M7` and `M27M-T` is tile-, block-, or art-centric, and none
of them touches a placed entity. This block is entity-side throughout: NPCs,
trainers, scripts, dialogue. Kept separate so "which tier owns the tile grid"
and "which tier owns the things standing on it" never have to be disambiguated
by reading the sub-tier body. Rob's call, 2026-08-08.

**Modelled on HexManiacAdvance's object-event panel**, which unifies the event
struct, the script bytes and the trainer table behind one selection
(`deepwiki.com/haven1433/HexManiacAdvance` §9.4). ⚠️ **The port of that idea is
NOT a docked panel, and that is the block's main design decision.** HMA needs a
dock because a hex editor has no scene tree and no inspector — it invents the
whole selection-and-properties surface. This project has both, and
`plugin._select_entity_at` + `MapOverlay.next_in_stack` already resolve a click
into a selected node with stacked-entity cycling. A dock would re-solve
selection sync, multi-select, deselect and freed-node guards — code that does
not exist, on the project's one surface with no automated coverage. Everything
node-scoped goes in the **Inspector**; the two project-wide tools are buttons.

⚠️ **THE FIVE FIELDS SPLIT ACROSS THREE STORAGE CLASSES, AND THE SPLIT IS THE
PLAN.** `movement_type`, `trainer_key` and `script_label` are `@export`s on the
placed node — in-memory until the scene is saved, no `ResourceSaver`, and
Inspector undo already covers them. `ai_flags` and the team roster live in a
shared `data/trainers/<KEY>.tres` — a real disk write, and the one that pays
the `EditorFileSystem` rescan that cost the collision-paint path ~5 s per
stroke. Dialogue preview is derived and writes nothing. Sequencing the cheap
class first is what keeps `ResourceSaver` out of the block until Q4.

**Measured during scoping (2026-08-08), and three of these correct claims made
elsewhere in this file:**

- ⚠️ **`movement_range_x/y` IS already imported** — `gen_map_import.py:555-556`
  emits `range_x`/`range_y`, and `grep -h "^range_x = \|^range_y = "
  scenes/maps/*.tscn | sort | uniq -c` finds **218 non-zero values** across the
  32 baked maps. Any plan listing it as a data prerequisite is working from a
  stale premise.
- ⚠️ **`ScriptVM.describe()` cannot render a script.** It is a runtime state
  dump of a *running* VM (`pc`, `current_op`, `pause_reason`, …,
  `script_vm.gd:2251`). No static op-stream renderer exists anywhere. The data
  is already structured (`ops_by_label[label]` is `Array[{"op","args"}]`), so
  writing one is formatting work — but it is work, and `describe()` is not it.
- **Nothing validates `script_label`.** `OverworldEntity._get_configuration_
  warnings` checks only `elevation`; `NPC` adds `movement_type`, `TrainerNPC`
  adds `trainer_key`/`sight_range`. A misspelled script label is silent in the
  editor, silent at boot, and first surfaces as the VM halting `UNRESOLVED`
  mid-conversation.
- **The shared-`.tres` blast radius is effectively nil.** A python scan of
  `assets/maps/*.json` gives **432 trainer placements / 418 distinct keys / 14
  keys used twice / 0 spanning more than one map** — every duplicate is a
  same-map adjacent pair (`TWINS_KIRI_JAN` at Route14 (13,51)/(12,51), and 13
  more across `COOL_COUPLE`/`CRUSH_KIN`/`SIS_AND_BRO`/`YOUNG_COUPLE`), i.e.
  double-battle partners sharing one trainer record. Correct data, not a hazard.
  **No cross-map warning is owed.**
- **`data/map_scripts.json` holds 17,159 labels**, not the 17,137 this file
  quotes elsewhere, and is **8.6 MB** — so an in-editor validator must cache it
  statically, never parse per-validation.
- **Inspector customisation is available and entirely unused here.**
  `ClassDB.class_exists` confirms `EditorInspectorPlugin`/`EditorProperty` in
  4.7.1; `grep -rn "_get_property_list\|_validate_property\|EditorInspector
  Plugin\|@export_enum"` over `scripts`/`addons`/`scenes` returns **nothing**
  outside the vendored dialogue_manager.

**Sub-tiers, in dependency order.**

**Q1 — the `ai_flags` wiring fix.** ⚠️ **ASSIGNING AI FLAGS IS THEATRE UNTIL
THIS LANDS, WHICH IS WHY IT IS FIRST AND WHY IT SHIPS ALONE.**
`TrainerAI.from_trainer_data()` exists and copies `ai_flags` correctly
(`trainer_ai.gd:98-100`) and has **zero non-test callers**; the single live
construction site is `battle_screen_shared.gd:1828-1829`, which does
`TrainerAI.new()` + `ai.tier = Tier.SMART` while `opp_trainer_data` sits
resolved six lines above and is handed to `set_trainer_data()` for money,
items and the intro banner but never for AI. ⚠️ **THIS IS NOT A STRICT
UPGRADE AND WANTS A DELIBERATE CALL.** Parsing every `.tres` gave, *as first
measured and BEFORE the importer fix two paragraphs down*, **1183 × `1`, 173 ×
`7`, 87 × `3`, 15 × `0`, 13 × `11`, 5 × `23`, 1 × `15`** = 1477. (Both
corrections below revise this; the figure is kept because the reasoning that
follows it is what found them.)
⚠️ **AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS BLOCK GREPPED `^ai_flags = ` AND MISSED THE 15
ZEROES** — Godot omits an `@export` sitting at its default when it writes a
`.tres`, so the absent line IS the value. Same grep-vs-measure failure as the
`OBJECT_EVENT` 26-vs-25 precedent; parse, do not grep.

⚠️ **AND ASKING "ARE WE MISSING AI OPTIONS?" FOUND A SECOND, REAL BUG — 80
TRAINERS WERE MISSING A FLAG THIS PROJECT ALREADY IMPLEMENTS.**
`gen_trainer_data.AI_TOKEN_MAP` had no `CHECKVIABILITY` key, and
`resolve_ai_flags` used `.get(key, 0)`, so the standalone token contributed
**zero with no warning**. Source spells it standalone on 80 trainers —
`AI: Check Bad Move / Try To Faint / Check Viability`, including both
`TRAINER_RIVAL_OAKS_LAB_*` — every one of which imported as `3` instead of
`7`. It stayed invisible because `AI_CHECK_VIABILITY` *was* referenced, inside
the `BASICTRAINER` composite, so the constant looked used and nothing read as
absent. Fixed 2026-08-08: the key was added AND the silent default replaced
with a `SystemExit` naming the token, because the `.get(..., 0)` was the real
defect and the next added flag would have dropped the same way (same
kill-the-bug-class discipline as `normalize()`'s collision guard). Break-tested
with a synthetic `Omniscient` token; 80 `.tres` files changed, one line each,
`3` → `7`, zero regeneration drift.

Post-fix distribution: 1183 × `1`, **253 × `7`**, 15 × `0`, 13 × `11`, 7 × `3`,
5 × `23`, 1 × `15`. Against the `7` every trainer used to run at, Q1 leaves
**1,205 strictly weaker** (1183+15+7), 253 unchanged, 13 mixed (`11` trades
CHECK_VIABILITY for FORCE_SETUP) and only **6 stronger** — and all 1,477 lose
proactive switching, because zero of them carry `AI_FLAG_SMART_SWITCHING` (no
roster value uses any bit above 4). Source fidelity, and most of Kanto gets
measurably dumber. Ships with its own regression run, not bundled with tooling.

⚠️ **Q1 SITS IN FRONT OF `M26F2`, IT DOES NOT SUPERSEDE IT — and an earlier
draft of this block got that wrong by calling M26F2 "stale".** M26F2
(orig. M25g) is two gaps under a doubles-sounding title, and only one of them
is doubles: **(a)** `_score_move_doubles`/`choose_action_doubles` never received
the `ai_flags` gating M24c gave the singles path (77 of 854 doubles-format
trainers), and **(b)** RISKY/FORCE_SETUP_FIRST_TURN shipped narrow — a small
bonus-scoring slice per flag against source's ~15-25 per-move-effect cases each
— which lives in the SINGLES scoring path (`trainer_ai.gd:527`, `:548`, `:862`)
and has nothing to do with doubles. Neither statement is wrong. What neither
names is the construction site above: singles received the `ai_flags` CODE
(nine branch sites) but no path receives the `ai_flags` DATA, so **(a)** is moot
until Q1 lands and **(b)** is unaffected either way. Flagged, not silently
fixed — whether M26F2 gains a note pointing here, or Q1 is absorbed into it, is
Rob's call.

**Q2 — Inspector affordances, no plugin.** One-file edits to files that already
exist, each closing a silent-failure path rather than adding a view.
`_validate_property` in `npc.gd` swaps `movement_type`'s hint to
`PROPERTY_HINT_ENUM` over `MovementTypes.ALL` (**89 entries**, generated) —
~5 lines, the value stays a `String`, so baked scenes, `check_bake_diff` and
the importer are untouched, and the typo class `_get_configuration_warnings`
currently only reports *after the fact* becomes unrepresentable. A
`script_label` configuration warning, matching the shape the sibling warnings
already use. `@export_flags("Check Bad Move", "Try To Faint", "Check
Viability", "Force Setup First Turn", "Risky")` on `TrainerData.ai_flags` — one
annotation, and Godot renders the checkbox set natively. An "Open trainer
resource" button calling `EditorInterface.edit_resource()`. ⚠️ **NO TEAM EDITOR
IS BUILT HERE OR ANYWHERE**: `TrainerData.party` is already
`Array[TrainerPartyMon]` of `Resource`s with `@export`s, so Godot's own
array-of-resources editor handles add/remove/reorder and every per-mon field
today, and the save goes through Godot's dirty-tracking rather than a
`ResourceSaver` call this project owns.

**Q3 — the entity inspector plugin.** The only real build. One
`EditorInspectorPlugin`, `_can_handle` scoped to `NPC`/`TrainerNPC`,
`_parse_begin` so the controls sit above the Node2D/CanvasItem sections:
a static op-stream listing for `script_label`; dialogue preview resolving each
`message` op through `map_texts.json`; a read-only team roster from
`TrainerRegistry`; decoded `ai_flags` bits; and filtered `EditorProperty`
pickers for `trainer_key` and `script_label`, which need typeahead rather than
an enum (**623 `_FRLG` trainer keys**, **17,159 script labels** — both far past
what a dropdown can carry, the same reason `graphics_id`'s **387** ids are
deliberately left as free text). ⚠️ **ONE THING TO SETTLE BEFORE BUILDING:
`EventRegistry` IS POPULATED AT RUNTIME**, by `ScriptDriver.setup()`, so nothing
in the editor knows which authored labels exist. Either the plugin calls
`AuthoredEvents.register_all()` itself (static, idempotent, refuses duplicates)
or it reads `map_scripts.json` directly — and that file is 8.6 MB.

**Q4 — the name-collision checker, as a button not a panel.** ⚠️ **RE-SCOPED
2026-08-08, AND THE ORIGINAL FRAMING IS RETIRED AS WRONG — Rob's observation.**
This was scoped as a *free-flag/var allocator*, ported from HexManiacAdvance,
which scans for the next unused flag starting at `0x21`. **That feature is
meaningless here.** HMA needs it because a GBA save is a fixed bit array and
you can genuinely run out; `FlagStore` keys on **free-form strings**
(`flag_get(flag_name: String)`), so there is no pool, no exhaustion, and
nothing to allocate. Write `FLAG_SETO_MET` and it works.

⚠️ **BUT THE OPPOSITE RISK IS REAL AND GETS WORSE, NOT BETTER, IN A STRING
WORLD.** With numbered flags the failure is running out. With names the failure
is silently REUSING one: author `FLAG_HIDE_ROUTE22_SIGN` and if Kanto already
uses it, the new NPC and some Route 22 event now share state, with nothing
anywhere reporting it. There is no equivalent of `EventRegistry.merge_into`'s
loud collision refusal for flags — that guard covers script labels only.
Measured over `data/map_scripts.json`: the corpus already references **1,004
distinct `FLAG_*` names and 236 distinct `VAR_*` names**, across **16 name
families with five or more members** (so near-misses are easy), with the
longest name at 59 characters. That is not a namespace anyone holds in their
head.

So Q4 answers **"is this name taken, how often, and where"** rather than
"what is free". One query, three parts to the answer:

- **count** — `0` means the name is yours; `1` is probably your own single use;
  anything higher means you are about to share state with existing content.
- **locations** — which maps and which labels, so a hit is actionable rather
  than merely alarming (`FLAG_HIDE_ROUTE22_SIGN — 3 uses: Route22,
  Route22_Gate, ViridianCity`).
- **the same treatment for `VAR_*`**, `trainer_key` and text labels, which is
  the "show uses" reverse index folded in — it is the same traversal, and
  splitting them would mean walking 17,159 op lists twice.

**Cheaper than when first scoped, because Q3 already paid for the index.**
`ScriptPreview.ops_index()` parses and caches both corpora (8.6 MB, 226 ms,
once per session) and `texts_index()` the 11,596 text rows, so the scan is a
walk over data already in memory. Sources: `ops_by_label` for
`setflag`/`clearflag`/`goto_if_set`/`goto_if_unset`/`call_if_set`/
`call_if_unset` and `setvar`/`addvar`/`subvar`/`copyvar`/`compare`, plus every
placed entity's `visibility_flag` and every `Trigger`'s var.

⚠️ **The rules go in a testable class, the button goes in the plugin** — the
same split `ScriptPreview` and `entity_inspector.gd` already use, for the same
reason recorded there.

**THE NAMING CONVENTION IS SETTLED — `FLAG_AUTHORED_<MAP>_<THING>`, Rob's
call, 2026-08-08.** A checker only *reports* collisions; a convention makes
them structurally impossible, which is strictly better, and it was settled
while exactly ONE authored flag existed. Zero of the imported 1,004 names
begin `FLAG_AUTHORED_`, so the prefix is a clean namespace by measurement
rather than by hope. The map segment is what separates two maps that both want
a `_SIGN_READ`.

Applied retroactively the same day, at a total cost of three lines:
`FLAG_AUTHORED_SEA_BREEZE_READ` → `FLAG_AUTHORED_PALLETTOWN_SEA_BREEZE_READ`
(it predated the convention and carried no map segment). Doing it at one flag
is the whole reason the convention was worth settling before Q4 rather than
after.

⚠️ **THE CONVENTION GOVERNS NAMES YOU INVENT, NOT REFERENCE-DEFINED REGISTERS
YOU DELIBERATELY SHARE — and this distinction is load-bearing for the checker.**
Measured across `scripts/events/`, authored code references three flag/var
names: `FLAG_AUTHORED_PALLETTOWN_SEA_BREEZE_READ` (unique), plus **`VAR_RESULT`
and `VAR_TEMP_1`, which BOTH also appear in the imported corpus — correctly**.
`VAR_RESULT` is source's own shared result register that every yes/no box
writes to, and `VAR_TEMP_1` is deliberately temp precisely because a temp var
clears on map change (see `PalletTownEvents`' own note). Sharing them IS the
mechanism. So Q4's checker must treat reference-defined registers as expected
overlap rather than collisions, or it will report two permanent false
positives, and a tool that cries wolf twice on a corpus this size gets ignored
— which costs more than not building it.

**Value scales with content, so timing is a real question.** With 14 authored
labels and a handful of flags this is holdable in your head; at fifty it is
not. Building it before there is anything to collide with is speculative.

**Q5 — custom team builder investigation. ⚠️ DEFERRED INDEFINITELY — Rob's
call, 2026-08-08. Not scheduled, not next, not blocked on anything.** Q2
shipped Godot's native array-of-resources editor for `TrainerData.party` at
zero cost, and until authoring a real trainer with it proves annoying there is
no evidence a bespoke builder is worth anything. Building one first would be
picking a solution before the problem is known to exist. **The trigger to
revisit is experiential, not a date**: hand-author a few trainers, and if the
raw `species_dex`/`move_ids` int fields genuinely get in the way, reopen this
with that as the brief.

⚠️ **ONE PIECE OF Q5 SHOULD NOT WAIT FOR IT, AND IS THE REASON THIS TIER IS
DEFERRED RATHER THAN DELETED.** `MovepoolResolver.legal_move_ids` calls
`ResourceLoader.exists()` ONCE PER CANDIDATE MOVE NAME — `get_learnable_moves()`
returns ~87 entries for Bulbasaur alone, so a roster sweep is roughly 300,000
filesystem checks across 3,444 party mons. That is not only a Q5 problem: the
**simulator's existing team builder calls the same function on every
species/level change**, so the cost is already being paid live, per keystroke,
today. Hoisting the implemented-move-id set out of the loop (one
`DirAccess.get_files("res://data/moves/")`) is a small, independent fix worth
doing on its own merits, and it unblocks any future roster lint for free.

The original question, kept for whenever it reopens: does authoring a trainer team want Godot's
native array-of-resources editor (free, shipped in Q2), or the simulator's own
`scenes/team_builder/team_builder_screen.tscn`, or a third thing? **The real
distinction is legality, not layout.** The simulator's builder enforces move
legality **by construction** — `MovepoolResolver.legal_move_ids(dex, level)` is
its only selectable pool, so an illegal moveset is unconstructible there — while
the native array editor is a raw int field that will accept any move id at any
level. So the two produce different *classes* of data, and the choice decides
whether hand-authored trainers can be illegal. Also in scope: whether the same
pass can lint the **1,477 imported** trainers, since it is the same function.
⚠️ **A PREREQUISITE THE RECON ALREADY HIT: `legal_move_ids` IS TOO SLOW TO
SWEEP THE ROSTER.** It calls `ResourceLoader.exists()` once per candidate move
name and `get_learnable_moves()` returns ~87 entries for Bulbasaur alone —
roughly 300,000 filesystem checks across 3,444 party mons. Two attempted
full-roster scans were killed at 900 s and 300 s, and a third returned an
internally contradictory result (`cache=0` after 3,444 calls) — **the harness
was broken, so no hit-rate figure is recorded here rather than a wrong one.**
Hoisting the implemented-move-id set out of the loop (one
`DirAccess.get_files("res://data/moves/")`) is the fix, and it also speeds the
team builder's own per-keystroke move-list refresh. **Q5 is where that hoist
gets measured, not assumed.**

**What this block deliberately does NOT do.** No docked panel. No custom team
editor. No script authoring in the Inspector — read-out only, with authoring
staying in `scripts/events/` per `docs/field_script_authoring.md`'s one-corpus
decision. No cross-map shared-trainer warnings (measured at zero). No
`graphics_id` dropdown (387 ids, no typeahead in Godot's enum control, worse
than typing). And no `ResourceSaver` call this project owns — every `.tres`
write in Q2 goes through Godot's own dirty-tracking and Save, which is the same
conclusion the M27M plugin already reached the hard way.

**Standing rules apply unchanged**: the C/T/A key governs any row this block
grows, and rule 1 — never fill `A` — is in force here as everywhere else.


## M27S — The static corpus audit *(built 2026-08-10)*

**`ScriptVM._literal` returns 0 for a token it has no case for, and zero is a valid number** — so nothing errors, nothing halts, the script runs to completion, and the comparison quietly does the wrong thing. **This bug class has landed six times**, every one found by PLAYING the one scene that used it and every one fixed by adding another `match` case: `YES`/`NO` (753 uses, inverted every yes/no branch in the region), `SPECIES_*` (295, the starter script could not name its own Pokémon), `PARTY_SIZE` (57, every "was anything chosen" check took Decline), `INGAME_TRADE_*` (13, every trade NPC read the wrong table row), `LOCALID_*` (Gary's own ball never disappeared) and `RIVAL_BATTLE_*` (heal-after-loss never fired). Nobody ever made the fallthrough itself fail loud. `ScriptAudit` is that.

⚠️ **IT IS GDSCRIPT, NOT `gen_map_scripts.py`, AND THE EARLIER RECOMMENDATION TO PUT IT THERE WAS WRONG — recorded because the reasoning was plausible.** That was argued from the three guards already living in the generator (the `pokemart` stock check, trainer-key canonicalisation, the `normalize()` collision assert), but every one of those is pure Python over data the generator itself produces. This one needs `_literal`, which is GDScript, so the generator version would have to REIMPLEMENT the resolver — two hand-kept copies of one rule, which is exactly the `check_bake_diff`/`map_baker` normalisation drift this project already paid for with a permanent false positive. **Calling the real function is the whole point of the tool.**

**Measured: 183,265 arguments across 17,159 labels, parse 209 ms + scan 301 ms** — cheap enough to sit in the ordinary suite rather than a separate pass.

⚠️ **THE CORRIDOR SPLIT IS WHAT MAKES IT USABLE.** Whole-corpus it finds **1,977 distinct unresolved tokens**, dominated by `ANIM_` (13,253 uses), `SE_` (4,077) and `SOUND_` (3,673) — battle-animation and contest scripts this project cannot reach. Restricted to labels owned by a baked map it finds **139**, which is a list a human reads.

⚠️ **ROUGHLY 90% OF THE VOLUME IS CORRECT AND MUST BE WHITELISTED, OR THE TOOL IS NOISE.** `STRING_CONTEXT` names 16 families consumed BY NAME rather than as a number — `LOCALID_` (812 corridor uses), `TRAINER_` (792), `MAP_` (190), `ITEM_` (165), `SE_`/`MUS_`, `MOVEMENT_TYPE_` (a `String` by design), `STR_VAR_`, `FADE_`, and so on. **Each carries its reason and each was confirmed by reading the consumer**, not assumed from the name: `FADE_TO_BLACK`/`FADE_FROM_BLACK` in particular were an open question until `FieldNativeEvents`' own handler was read and found to compare `dir.begins_with("FADE_FROM")` — a raw string, never `_literal`. A family whitelisted on a guess is how an audit stops finding things.

**FIVE REAL FINDS ON THE FIRST RUN, and the tool had no knowledge of any of them:**

- ⚠️ **`REQUIRED_CAUGHT_MONS` / `REQUIRED_OWNED_MONS` / `REQUIRED_SEEN_MONS`** — `goto_if_lt VAR_0x8006, REQUIRED_CAUGHT_MONS`, so `< 0` is never true and the Route 11 / Route 15 Aides always behave as though you have caught enough. **This independently rediscovered a gap the project already knew about**: `[M27I I3b]` found these file-scoped `.equ` constants, measured **32 region-wide**, noted `REQUIRED_CAUGHT_MONS` is declared **three times with three different values (30/40/50)** — which is why a global table is wrong and the fix needs FILE SCOPE — and explicitly flagged-not-fixed it. The audit finding a known-deferred real bug with no prior knowledge is the strongest validation available.
- ⚠️ **`PARTY_NOTHING_CHOSEN`** — `0xFF` (255) in source. `[M27G G2]` correctly WRITES 255 into `VAR_0x8004` on a cancel, but any script comparing against the symbolic constant reads **0** — so the write half is right and the read half is broken.
- ⚠️ **`MON_GIVEN_TO_PC`** — `givemon`'s own three-way result code. `[M27K K-c2]` fixed the WRITE side (0/1/2 rather than a boolean); the constant still resolves to 0, so `goto_if_eq VAR_RESULT, MON_GIVEN_TO_PC` reads the party branch. `MON_GIVEN_TO_PARTY` is accidentally correct, being 0 anyway.
- **`B_OUTCOME_*`** (4 distinct, 12 corridor uses) — `goto_if_eq VAR_RESULT, B_OUTCOME_WON` against 0. Not previously flagged anywhere.
- **`SIGN_LADY_READY`** — Pallet Town, the corridor's first map. Same file-scoped `.equ` class, but **it happens to work**: the VM treats the name as an opaque var key, so set-then-read is self-consistent. Latent, not broken.

**A BASELINE, NOT A WALL** — the `m36_leak_harness` `KNOWN_LEAKS` shape. Real unresolved constants exist today; failing outright would mean a red suite until every one is fixed, which is how a guard gets deleted. `CORRIDOR_UNRESOLVED_BASELINE := 139` is pinned so a NEW one fails, and **shrinking it is the definition of progress**.

**Tests: section BF, 8 assertions** (`m27a_step_resolver_test` 625 → 633). Non-vacuity is asserted directly (BF.06: a token that DOES resolve is not reported), which is what a whitelist growing to cover the world would fail. **Three injections, each caught by its own guard AND by the baseline**: a whitelist that swallows everything fails BF.02/BF.07; letting mixed-case labels through fails BF.05 and takes the baseline 139 → **5,049**; dropping the `VAR_`/`FLAG_` store-key exclusion fails BF.04 and takes it to **445**.

**The obvious next step is not more tooling** — it is the file-scoped `.equ` resolution `[M27I I3b]` deferred, which alone clears `REQUIRED_*` and `SIGN_LADY_READY` and needs `gen_map_scripts.py` to resolve `.equ`/`.set` WITH FILE SCOPE plus a corpus regeneration. `PARTY_NOTHING_CHOSEN`, `MON_GIVEN_TO_PC` and `B_OUTCOME_*` are one `_literal` case each.


## M27R — Corridor completion, first custom map, and basic audio *(planned 2026-08-08)*

**The goal in one line: the 32-map corridor playable end to end, one hand-made
map in it, and enough sound that it does not play in silence.** Scoped from
measurement, not from the roadmap's own wish list — the blockers below were
found by walking the **666 script labels reachable from corridor placements**
and checking every op against `ScriptVM`.

### Phase 1 — close the corridor

⚠️ **THE REAL GAP IS MUCH SMALLER THAN THE OPCODE-COVERAGE FIGURE SUGGESTS,
AND MOSTLY IS NOT OPCODES AT ALL.** Of 130 distinct ops the corridor actually
reaches, only these are unhandled:

- **`pokemart` — 2 uses, the one real opcode gap.** Viridian and Pewter marts
  do nothing. It needs a shop UI, so it is the largest single item in this
  phase; **M27I** owns it.
- **The movement-action tail — FIVE actions, five uses.** ⚠️ **CORRECTED
  2026-08-08: an earlier draft of this plan said "~53 uses" and it was wrong.**
  That figure came from grepping `movement_runner.gd` for literal action names,
  but `_build()` GENERATES them by concatenation (`"walk_in_place_faster_" +
  suffix`), so the literals never appear and 48 already-implemented uses read
  as missing. Re-measured by asking `MovementRunner.action(name)` itself,
  across the 101 movement scripts the corridor's `applymovement` calls
  reference (1,166 actions checked): **`emote_exclamation_mark`,
  `face_original_direction`, `face_player`, `jump_2_down`, `nurse_joy_bow` —
  one use each.** The same grep-vs-measure failure this file already records
  for `OBJECT_EVENT` 26-vs-25 and for the `ai_flags` zeroes; three times now,
  always the same shape.
  ⚠️ **They are five, but they are not five table entries.** `face_player` and
  `face_original_direction` need runtime context the static table has no slot
  for (where the player is; where this entity spawned facing). `jump_2_down` is
  an arc, not a frame count. `emote_exclamation_mark` needs the "!" bubble —
  `[M27D D1]` already pulled `emotion_exclamation.png`, so the asset is there.
  `nurse_joy_bow` is a bespoke animation. Small, but real work rather than data.
- **`trywondercardscript` — 1 use.** Mystery Gift. A documented no-op, matching
  the existing named-no-op convention.

⚠️ **~53 IS A FLOOR, NOT A CEILING.** It was derived by following `goto`/`call`
chains from placed entities, which is how the game normally reaches a script —
but a map's own `OnFrame`/`OnTransition` tables enter scripts by another route
that this walk does not model. A real playthrough is what confirms the number.

### Phase 2 — one custom map that PLAYS, not just paints

⚠️ **ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING HERE.** Painting is already possible; making a
painted tile MEAN anything is not, and building the brush first means painting
the map twice.

- **M27M1 — behaviour onto the tile at bake.** The `behavior` custom-data layer
  is already declared on every baked TileSet and **never populated** (measured:
  zero tiles carry a value). Everything below reads it, so it goes first.
- **M27M4 — write painted ids back into `MapData`**, pulling behaviour off the
  tile. ⚠️ **THIS IS THE ONE THAT TURNS PAINTED GRASS INTO GRASS.** Without it
  a new map looks correct and plays inert: no encounters, no surfing, no
  ledges, because `StepResolver` reads `MapData.behavior` and nothing writes
  it.
  ⚠️ **IT IS SMALLER THAN IT SOUNDS, BECAUSE THE OVERLAY EDITOR ALREADY BUILT
  MOST OF THE PATH — Rob's point, 2026-08-08, and worth stating so a later
  session does not rebuild an editor that exists.** `[M27B Change 2 / Step D]`
  shipped the whole editing apparatus: `paint()`/`apply_edit()` for collision
  and elevation, `snapshot_cells()`/`restore_cells()` wired into the editor's
  own undo, `review_cells()`/`review_count()` and the dirty banner,
  `author_cell_with_defaults()` for inherit-a-guess-and-flag-it,
  `save_map_data()` behind the Save Map Data button, and the
  `provenance`/`attr_explicit` record. M27M4's own scope text expects to
  inherit it — "seed via the EXISTING `author_cell_with_defaults()`".
  What is genuinely missing is narrow: **`MapData` has exactly four mutators**
  (`set_attr_explicit`, `set_collision`, `set_elevation`,
  `author_cell_with_defaults`) and **there is no `set_metatile_id` and no
  `set_behavior`** — so nothing can write the two arrays this tier is about —
  plus the detection itself, comparing painted atlas coords against
  `MapData.metatile`, which nothing anywhere does. **Two setters and a
  comparison, not a write path.**
- **M27M3 — the metatile brush, RESCOPED (Rob, 2026-08-08).** ⚠️ **It asks
  "in front of the player or behind?", NOT "what layer type is this?"** Layer
  type is a GBA hardware taxonomy for how source packed its BG layers, and it
  is **not load-bearing at runtime** — grepped: nothing reads
  `MapData.layer_type` to decide anything; draw order comes from `PLANE_Z`
  alone. The brush writes all three planes at the cell, each with its own
  source; the non-routed one is transparent (measured: all 10,494 non-routed
  atlas cells are fully blank) so it costs nothing and needs no routing
  lookup. `MapManager.set_metatile` already does this paint and is green at
  20/20 — the work is exposing it, not inventing it.
- **M27M5 — a blank new map**: `.tscn` + `_data.tres` straight to
  `scenes/maps/`, no importer and no JSON.
- **Connect it** — a `Warp` at each end, plus `connections` if it should stitch
  rather than door.

**Not needed for a FIRST custom map**: `M27M6` (behaviour picker, now merged into Part B) and `M27M7`
(new art) only matter once you are authoring original tiles; Kanto's art
covers this.

### Phase 3 — basic audio *(scope and approach deliberately OPEN)*

**What Rob asked for: a little audio — basic town/map music and battle music.
Not the full sound engine.** How it is built and how far it goes are
deliberately left undecided here; this section records only what is true today
so the scoping session starts from facts.

⚠️ **THE ASSET SITUATION IS LOPSIDED, AND IT INVERTS THE OBVIOUS PLAN.**
`assets/Essentials_v19.1/Audio/` is already in the repo and tracked:

    SE   858 files (.ogg/.wav)   — a large, usable effects library
    ME    22 files (.ogg)        — jingles: victory, capture success, …
    BGM   35 files, of which 32 are .mid and 3 are .ogg
    BGS    0

⚠️ **GODOT DOES NOT PLAY MIDI, AND THOSE 32 FILES ARE INERT BYTES** — none has
a `.import` sidecar, so the engine never ingested them. The three usable `.ogg`
BGM tracks are *Evolution*, *HoF room* and *Hall of Fame* — **not a town theme
and not a battle theme among them.** So the half Rob asked for (town + battle
music) is the half with no assets, while the half nobody asked for (858 sound
effects) is ready to wire.

That makes BGM an **asset-sourcing question before it is a code question** —
render the MIDIs through a soundfont, find `.ogg` equivalents, or commission
them. That decision belongs to Rob, not to an implementation session.

**Seams that already exist**, so the code half is small when it comes:
`playbgm`/`playse`/`playfanfare`/`waitfanfare`/`fadeoutbgm`/`fadedefaultbgm`
are named no-ops in `ScriptVM` (`script_vm.gd:559`), and the battle animation
VM already records an SE id and pan per cue for **M36-S**. ⚠️ **Do NOT port
source's m4a engine** — channel priority and DirectSound allocation are
hardware accommodation; the fidelity that matters is which cue plays and when.

### Suggested order

**movement tail → `trywondercardscript` → M27M1 → M27M4 → M27M3 → M27M5 →
connect → audio scoping → `pokemart`.**

The movement tail is five actions across the whole corridor, so it is the
smallest thing here and closes the last `UNKNOWN` in cutscene playback. M27M1/M27M4
precede the brush so a map is painted once. Audio scoping sits before
`pokemart` because it is a decision Rob makes rather than work, and
`pokemart` is the biggest single build with nothing waiting on it.

**Standing rules apply unchanged**: the C/T/A key governs any row this block
grows, and rule 1 — never fill `A` — is in force here as everywhere else.

### Standing Step 0 checklist item: does this foe-targeting move's real script call `typecalc`?

Discovered independently four times before being made a standing checklist item: `[D2 batch 2]` (Foresight/Odor Sleuth), `[D4 bundle 3]` (Purify/Nightmare/Spite), `[D4 Bundle 6]` (Toxic Thread/Venom Drench), and the `[EFFECT_STAT_CHANGE audit]` that finally swept the rest of the family (16 more moves — Growl/Leer/Screech/etc.).

This project's `BattleManager._phase_move_execution` runs every foe-targeting move with a real type through one general type-immunity gate before letting the move's own effect logic run, on the assumption that real source similarly checks `typecalc` for every such move. That assumption is false for an entire family of status moves: any move whose real battle script never calls `typecalc` (confirmed so far: `BattleScript_EffectForesight`, `BattleScript_EffectStatChange` and everything that maps to it, `BattleScript_EffectReflectType`) is NEVER subject to a type-immunity check in the real engine — including against its own primary, flavor-intended use case (Nightmare vs. a sleeping Normal-type target; Toxic Thread vs. a Steel-type switch-in; a plain Normal-type stat-lowering move vs. a Ghost-type).

**Before implementing or auditing any foe-targeting status move (or move family) going forward**: read the move's real battle script directly (`data/battle_scripts_1.s`, or trace `sMoveEffectTable`/`battle_move_effects.h` to find which script the move's `.effect` maps to) and confirm whether it calls `typecalc` anywhere. If it does not, the move needs the same treatment as the precedents above — either its own dedicated `is_*` exemption (if it also needs its own dispatch branch for other reasons) or, if it's one of a family sharing a no-`typecalc` script, the shared `MoveData.stat_change_bypasses_type_gate`-style flag — checked against this project's own `TypeChart.TABLE` to confirm the exemption is actually reachable (not moot, like the 9 Memento-shaped exclusions above) before spending test-writing effort on it.

### Standing Step 0 checklist item: a new consumer of a dormant `MoveData` field needs a full-roster grep, not just the moves already in mind

A recurring gap shape across this whole M19/M21.5 arc: a `MoveData` field already exists in the schema and already has real ability/item logic reading it, but was never actually SET on every move that should carry it per source — only on the specific moves a session happened to be thinking about at the time. Confirmed instances: Ice Ball's `ballistic_move` (flagged in a comment since `[M16b]` but never actually set on the data dict until `[M17n-1]`), Air Balloon/Iron Ball's groundedness (`[M18t]`), Mega Punch/Headlong Rush's `punching_move` and Aerial Ace's `slicing_move` (M21.5 Session 3's Part 4 Punch-family check), and 69 more moves across 10 flag categories found by M21.5's own Bucket 1 full-roster sweep.

**Before building any new ability/item mechanic that reads an existing-but-previously-unconsumed `MoveData` field**: grep the full roster (`gen_moves.py`, or the reference `moves_info.h` struct field directly) for every move that should carry the flag per source — not just the specific named moves already in mind for that session. A session scoped to "does move X need this flag" will reliably miss sibling moves Y and Z that need the identical flag for the identical reason.
