## RPG progress tiers *(Rob's call, 2026-08-08)*

A ten-tier maturity ladder for the project as a whole — product-playability
and polish, not feature completion. This is **orthogonal to the M-number
milestone system above**: milestones track *what* gets built; these tiers
track *whether the shipped result is playable end to end* and how rough it
still is. A tier only advances when its criteria are actually true of the
running game, not when the milestone work that enables it lands.

**Subject to change** — this ladder is Rob's current thinking, not a locked
spec; tier boundaries and criteria may be revised as the project's actual
shape becomes clearer.

**Current tier: Tier 1. Working toward: Tier 2.**

| Tier | Definition |
|---|---|
| 1 | *(current)* Baseline — the state before Tier 2's criteria below are met. |
| 2 | **Pre-Alpha** *(current goal)*. Battle sim playable with occasional bugs (25% of move animations confirmed). RPG playable through the first badge, with one custom-authored map. |
| 3 | **Alpha.** Playable through 2 badges. Custom encounters on 3 maps; 2 or more custom maps. Battle sim/engine bugs almost gone — visible bugs still occasional. 50% of move animations confirmed. |
| 4 | **Pre-Beta.** Playable through 4 badges. Orange Islands started. |
| 5 | **Beta.** Playable through 4 badges. Orange Island maps created; some scripts created for the area. |
| 6 | **Quality Beta.** Orange Islands finished, with occasional issues. |
| 7 | Playable through 8 badges. |
| 8 | **Polish tier.** |
| 9 | **Playtest launch.** |
| 10 | **Launch.** |

Update the "current tier" marker as tiers close, the same way the M-number
milestones above track state — do not let it go stale.

## Development workflow

### Handoff: who does what

- **Rob commits.** Claude Code does not commit or push. Sessions end with
  changes left in the working tree; the standard closing line in this file's
  own status history is *"No commit made this session — per standing
  instruction, Rob commits."* (later sessions phrase it *"Nothing committed to
  git, per standing instruction."*). Both mean the same thing.
- **Rob runs full-suite sweeps.** Full `scripts/count_assertions.sh` sweeps
  are Rob's manual step. Claude Code runs tier-specific suites for the work in
  hand; it does not run a full sweep to populate a status column (see the
  `C/T/A` status key's own rules).
- **Rob owns approval.** The `A` slot in the `C/T/A` column is Rob's alone —
  never filled by Claude Code under any circumstances. See the status key.

### Running a verification scene

Self-contained, works from any cwd (see the working-directory rule above —
this form is mandatory, not stylistic):

```bash
/home/rob/Godot_v4.7.1-stable_linux.x86_64 --headless --path /home/rob/GodotAsImagined/as-imagined scenes/battle/SCENE.tscn
```

**Fresh-checkout baseline:** `scenes/overworld/m27a_step_resolver_test.tscn`
reads **44/44** (90 gated) in a clean clone, and **134/134** only after
`python3 scripts/gen_map_import.py all` regenerates the gitignored map JSONs
AND the corridor is baked — it announces which mode it is in. Both totals must
balance against `EXPECTED_TOTAL` via Z.99; a section that bails without
crediting `_gated` silently drops assertions the balance check then cannot
see, which is exactly how ~50 of them went missing once. Note `reference/pokeemerald_expansion` is a
git submodule, so a fresh clone needs `git submodule update --init` before any
generator runs. See docs/overworld_scope.md's "Regeneration chain" note.

**Verification scenes:**
- `scenes/battle/m36_leak_harness.tscn` — **[M36]** whole-roster LEAK / clean-run harness (784 tests): runs all 778 playable in-scope move scripts end to end and checks each TERMINATES, does not end in VM ERROR, leaves every battler at its resting position/scale/rotation/visibility/modulate/material, leaves no sprite or clone on the layer, and clears the scanline band. **This is defect detection, NOT fidelity verification** — it cannot tell you an animation looks right, only that it ran and cleaned up after itself. Its first run found **23 real leaks across 22 moves** (**all since FIXED at the root, 2026-08-03 — the baseline is EMPTY and must stay so**), one root cause in three dresses (the VM's cleanup nets only cover the TRACKED path, so direct `node.rotation`/`node.modulate`/`node.scale` writes and sprites never passed to `notify_spawned` all escape). Those are PINNED in `KNOWN_LEAKS` as a baseline that fails if the set changes in either direction; **shrinking it is the definition of done**, and the fix is a core-VM change deliberately left as its own task.
- `scenes/battle/m36d_batch_test.tscn` — **[M36D]** expansion batch (66 tests): asserts ported MOTION, not just that sprites appear — powder drifting downward from the TARGET while oscillating, vortex particles rising while circling, Bite's symmetric converge/retreat, the fist being genuinely static, the absorption orb travelling toward the ATTACKER (the one particle that runs backwards, so getting it wrong would look wrong in every draining move), an arc departing from its chord at mid-flight, the slice's 24-frame budget (20 motion + 4 hold), every mon task displacing AND exactly restoring position and scale, the elliptical orbit starting at the mon's own position, and end-to-end runs of the iconic moves this batch unblocked.
- `scenes/battle/m36e3_bg_behaviors_test.tscn` — **[M36E3]** BG-dependent behaviors (52 tests). Shaped around the two ways these fail INVISIBLY and PERMANENTLY: an open-ended effect registered as a COUNTED task hangs `waitforvisualfinish` forever (so the psychic cycle and the sliding scroll are asserted to NOT count, and to still be running after 200 frames), and a shake that resets the scroll to zero instead of its captured value silently cancels a scroll in progress (so the shake starts on an already-scrolled background and must return to exactly that). Also: the palette rotation's DIRECTION (rotating the wrong way makes the ramp flow backwards and the wave look like it is receding), the 8.8 accumulator delivering a pixel on the 4th frame for a quarter-pixel velocity (truncating instead would freeze every slow scroll dead), the mirror flag being CONDITIONAL on side, the haze blend table's source-exact plateaus, MetallicShine's recolour going through a SHADER not modulate (the defect this check caught) and its third sweep being real 32 frames, and — the headline — Surf installing the side-appropriate asset, travelling the MIRRORED direction per side, cycling 7 palette entries not the psychic 11, and ending on its own blend ramp with no sentinel.
- `scenes/battle/m36e_background_runtime_test.tscn` — **[M36E2]** background runtime (30 tests): BG integer-id -> name resolution through the extractor's own constant table, and above all the fade ORDER — the screen must be FULLY black at the moment the background swaps, asserted by recording the fade value at each swap, because a port that swapped first and faded second would look wrong in a way no 'did the texture change' check would catch. Also: the two wait opcodes releasing on different phases, changebg swapping with no fade at all, restorebg clearing only once black, an unknown background still costing its full frames (so pacing holds), and the leak invariant — finishing a run clears any background it swapped in.
- `scenes/battle/m36e_background_asset_test.tscn` — **[M36E1]** background-pull integrity (20 tests): the reference table still having 84 entries, every composited background loading and being exactly the 32-cell tilemap width and 8px-aligned and matching its indexed geometry, the headline consumers being present (psychic/thunder/surf/hyper-beam), each background carrying REAL imagery rather than a flat fill (the failure mode a broken tile lookup produces), and palette-only variants sharing tiles AND tilemap upstream while rendering visibly differently — which is what proves the recolor step actually applied.
- `scenes/battle/m36_coverage_report.tscn` — **[M36D]** NOT a test: the sequencing tool. Prints per-tier coverage (iconic Gen 1-3 / remaining Gen 1-3 / Gen 4+) plus a greedy 'what to port next' ranking. Run it before planning any M36D batch.
- `scenes/battle/m36c_anim_behaviors_test.tscn` — **[M36C]** core behavior batch (66 tests): registration, COVERAGE ACTUALLY MOVING (the acceptance set Pound/Tackle/Flamethrower must play, plus a roster-wide count with a regression floor), ported math against the reference (ShakeMon toggling to ZERO vs ShakeMon2's +/- oscillation, hitsplat affine size presets and ~9-frame lifetime, sin-wave beam travel and its 30-frame budget, controller-sprite out-and-back symmetry, query-task return values), MONS ALWAYS RESTORED (every displacing behavior returns the battler exactly, and an aborted run still records the true base for restoration), and whole-script runs proving Flamethrower's full 22-flame stream with no sprites or counter slots leaked.
- `scenes/battle/m36b_anim_vm_test.tscn` — **[M36B]** animation runtime core (53 tests): AnimData loading/sheet/frame-sequence resolution (incl. the indexed-offset case), the behavior registry's STATIC WALK (both choosetwoturnanim arms, call targets, goto loops terminating, unknown labels reported), THE FALLBACK CONTRACT (empty registry plays nothing; PARTIAL registration still falls back — all-or-nothing per move), VM semantics against stub behaviors (arg register file per create-command, call/return running Flamethrower's subroutine all 22 times, turn-parity branch selection, waitforvisualfinish blocking on live sprites, the frame-ceiling guard, and erroring rather than half-playing if the fallback check is bypassed), the dispatcher's verdicts/coverage report, and a WHOLE-ROSTER walk proving all 932 bound moves yield a decidable verdict and 0 play with no behaviors registered. Prints the live port size (587 distinct behaviors; top blockers) — expect that number to fall as M36C/D land.
- `scenes/battle/m36a_anim_extract_test.tscn` — **[M36A]** battle-animation extraction integrity (71 tests): the extracted command program (opcode/arity conformance against the published signature table, label bounds, aliased-label preservation, Pound's exact opcode sequence), the move/general/status/special dispatch tables, the 412-row tag table (incl. the 2 NULL rows and the SAP_DRIP_2 palette asymmetry), 1,148 sprite templates, frame data (file-qualified keys; ANIMCMD_FRAME count pinned at 784 against source), the 410 pulled sheets (load/tile-alignment/geometry-vs-index/transparency, composite tile counts vs declared VRAM size), and the CROSS-FILE chains that matter most for M36B — every createsprite names a real template, every jump names a real label, every template's tag resolves to a pulled sheet, every frame table and entry resolves, plus one end-to-end walk (Flamethrower script → template → tag → sheet → frame sequence). Regenerate with the three `scripts/gen_battle_anim_*.py` extractors; all are idempotent.
- `scenes/battle/battle_test.tscn` — M1 battle loop (Struggle to faint)
- `scenes/battle/damage_test.tscn` — M2 damage formula and type chart (24 tests)
- `scenes/battle/status_test.tscn` — M3 status conditions (75 tests)
- `scenes/battle/move_test.tscn` — M4 move registry, damage via loaded moves, freeze-thaw (45 tests)
- `scenes/battle/stat_test.tscn` — M5 stat stages, secondary effects, accuracy, flinch (78 tests)
- `scenes/battle/tier3_test.tscn` — M6 multi-turn, semi-inv, fixed damage, recoil, drain (62 tests)
- `scenes/battle/tier4_test.tscn` — M7 Substitute, Counter/Mirror Coat, Protect, Destiny Bond, Disable, Encore, Bide, Metronome (86 tests)
- `scenes/battle/ability_test.tscn` — M8 abilities: Huge Power/Pure Power, Levitate, Thick Fat, Intimidate, Drizzle/Drought stubs, Speed Boost, Static, Flame Body, Rough Skin, Synchronize (59 tests)
- `scenes/battle/switch_test.tscn` — M9 switching: BattleParty unit tests, voluntary switch, volatile clear, non-volatile persist, Intimidate on switch-in, Roar/Whirlwind forced switch, Baton Pass passable transfer, faint replacement, full-party-faint battle end (64 tests)
- `scenes/battle/ai_test.tscn` — M10 Trainer AI: type effectiveness scoring, KO preference, type immunity avoidance, wasted status avoidance, two-turn penalty, BASIC vs SMART tier, proactive switch (all-immune / has-bad-odds), faint replacement, full battle integration (26 tests)
- `scenes/battle/weather_test.tscn` — M11 Weather: Drizzle/Drought set weather, duration countdown, expiry, no-clear-on-switch, same-weather no-op, overwrite, rain/sun damage modifiers (discriminating composition test), sandstorm/hail chip immunity, modifier revert on expiry, AI weather-aware scoring (64 tests)

- `scenes/battle/m16a_test.tscn` — M16a Tier A move effects: RESTORE_HP (Recover/Slack Off/Heal Order), FOCUS_ENERGY (crit stage +2), GROWTH (+1/+2 Atk+SpAtk, sun doubling), OHKO (Guillotine/Horn Drill/Fissure/Sheer Cold — level check, custom accuracy, type immunity, semi-inv bypass) (52 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m16b_test.tscn` — M16b Tier B move effects: MINIMIZE (+2 evasion, minimized flag gated on success), DEFENSE_CURL (+1 defense, defense_curled set unconditionally), Stomp minimizeDoubleDamage (exact ×2 post-roll modifier), ROLLOUT/Ice Ball (5-hit power doubling 30→60→120→240→480, Defense Curl doubling, interruption/miss resets), MAGNITUDE (weighted power table, power_override plumbing) (55 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m16c_test.tscn` — M16c Tier C move effects (screens): REFLECT/LIGHT_SCREEN (exact floor(dmg/2) per-category reduction, 5-turn duration, already-up no-refresh, doubles ⅔ reduction, switch persistence), AURORA_VEIL (hail gate, independent coexistence with Reflect/Light Screen, no double-stacking, reduces both categories), crit bypass, Brick Break (clears target's side pre-damage, own hit unaffected) (60 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m16d_test.tscn` — M16d Tier D move effects: SPIKES (opponent's-side 3-layer stacking, per-layer maxHP fraction, grounded/Levitate immunity, switch persistence), TOXIC_SPIKES (1-layer poison vs 2-layer toxic, Poison-type absorb, Steel-type immunity, ungrounded immunity), STEALTH_ROCK (type-effectiveness damage table, hits Flying-types unlike Spikes), RAPID_SPIN (clears one hazard in Spikes→Toxic Spikes→Stealth Rock order, fires even hitting a Substitute), TRICK_ROOM (exact speed-order reversal within a priority bracket, priority still overrides it, toggle-off, 5-turn expiry) (71 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m16e_test.tscn` — M16e Tier E move effects: PURSUIT (doubled power + turn-order interception striking the original switcher before its switch resolves, verified against direct `DamageCalculator` calls), PAIN_SPLIT (current-HP averaging both directions, floor rounding, Substitute block), CONVERSION (type from the literal first move slot, already-that-type failure), CONVERSION_2 (resist-type selection against the target's last USED move — not "last hit by" — exclusion-before-indexing, deterministic via `_force_conversion2_pick`, plus a direct-conflict discriminator against an earlier hit of a different type), PSYCH_UP (copies all 7 stat stages plus the Focus Energy volatile, overwrite semantics), Baton Pass (`focus_energy` now passes; `minimized`/`defense_curled`/`rollout_turns` still correctly excluded), type-reset-on-switch (a Conversion-mutated type reverts to the original species type after a voluntary switch-out and back in) (58 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m16_review_test.tscn` — M16 Milestone-End Review, Area 3: Trick Room × Pursuit turn-order integrity — Pursuit interception still fires correctly under Trick Room regardless of which side is naturally faster, doubled power and switch completion both still correct, and Trick Room's own speed-reversal is unaffected when a Pursuit-carrying Pokémon's target isn't switching (singles only — doubles combination explicitly flagged as untested in `docs/decisions.md`) (8 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m17a_test.tscn` — M17a Tier A move effects: 32 damage-pipeline-modifier abilities across attack-stat, defense-stat, post-type-effectiveness, and base-power hook points (Overgrow/Blaze/Torrent/Swarm, Marvel Scale, Compound Eyes, Battle Armor/Shell Armor, Multiscale, Filter/Solid Rock/Tinted Lens, Adaptability, Rock Head, Sniper, Toxic Boost, Sand Force, No Guard, Guts, Hustle, Heatproof, Iron Barbs, Fur Coat, Tough Claws, Steelworker, Steely Spirit, Battery, Power Spot, Rocky Payload, Ice Scales, Defeatist, Flare Boost), including the doubles-ally cases (Battery/Power Spot/Steely Spirit) and Guts' burn-halving exemption (83 tests)

- `scenes/battle/perish_song_test.tscn` — `[Perish Song]`: data integrity incl. the dormant `target=TARGET_ALL_BATTLERS` field finally being set; cast dispatch hitting both sides including the caster itself, an already-counting mon keeping its own timer rather than being reset by a second cast, the all-unaffected fail case, and the Soundproof per-target exclusion (the real generic-gate bug this move's own first test run caught and fixed); the full 4-tick countdown-to-faint sequence via direct `_phase_end_of_turn()` calls (3 count-down-only ticks, then the fatal 4th); doubles reachability (all 4 combatants affected by one cast); an ordinary Tackle negative control (29 tests)
- `scenes/battle/transform_test.tscn` — `[Transform]`: data integrity; a basic successful Transform (species/types/stats/ability/moves all copied, PP capped at min(realPP,5) proven both directions); all 4 fail conditions independently (semi-invulnerable target, target/attacker already Transformed, Substitute-blocked); stat_stages copied from the target rather than left at the attacker's own pre-Transform value; the shared-Resource-mutation-hazard regression test (Transform then Conversion on the transformed attacker, confirming the real target's own species.types is untouched); switch-out restoration via two direct `_do_voluntary_switch` calls (confirming the reset fires on switch-IN, not switch-OUT); times_hit's deliberate permanence across switch-out+in; ability-copy correctness under forced Neutralizing Gas (raw copy bypasses suppression at copy time, resolves correctly afterward via the normal accessor); Transform bypassing Protect; Instruct failing to force a Transform re-use; a negative control (59 tests)
- `scenes/battle/ban_flag_audit_test.tscn` — `[M19.5]` Task 1: data integrity for the 91 additions + 4 removals (spot-checked representative moves per flag family); live dispatch-level discriminators for the 5 flags with real gaps found this session (`BAN_METRONOME`/`BAN_SLEEP_TALK`/`BAN_ENCORE`/`BAN_ASSIST`/`BAN_SKETCH`) each excluding a representative newly-fixed move from its pool/target; regression guards (not fixes) for the 3 flags already fully correct going in (`BAN_MIMIC`/`BAN_COPYCAT`/`BAN_ME_FIRST`) (29 tests)
- `scenes/battle/mimic_transform_composition_test.tscn` — `[M19.5]` Task 2: Mimic then Transform in the same stint (no switch-out in between) — confirms `pre_transform_moves` captures Mimic itself, not the temporarily-mimicked move, and that a full switch-out+in cycle afterward correctly reverts the moveset to Mimic (not the Transform target's copied moveset, not the temporarily-mimicked move) (12 tests)
- `scenes/battle/move_smoke_test.tscn` — `[M19.5]` Task 3: a dedicated, standalone, independently-runnable full-roster load-integrity check (deliberately NOT folded into any other suite), scanning IDs 1-934 via `ResourceLoader.exists`/`load()` directly (self-updating, not a hardcoded ID list) and emitting one assertion per implemented move, mirroring `item_registry_test.gd`'s own Section 1 convention — confirms every one of the 717 implemented moves loads cleanly as a genuine `MoveData` instance (717 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m20_exp_test.tscn` — `[M20 EXP implementation]` + `[M20a]`: the ported 211-entry `EXP_SCALING_FACTORS` table and distribution/difficulty constant tables; direct `_compute_exp_award` unit tests (same-level self-check, asymmetric-levels worked example, distribution %, difficulty % — all hand-verified via Python first); full single-participant and doubles 2-participant integration (each recipient's share independently computed from their own level); the switched-out-but-alive-still-counts / fainted-before-the-kill-excluded eligibility pair; a 2-opponent trainer-battle-shaped scenario proving opponent-switch-in resets per-slot participant tracking rather than carrying over stale history; Difficulty Setting proven through a real end-to-end battle; a player-side-faint negative control; 3 real-species integration spot-checks (Bulbasaur/Ivysaur/Charizard, via `PokemonRegistry`'s now-real `exp_yield` data) (37 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m20a_data_test.tscn` — `[M20a]`: a pure data-completeness check (no `BattleManager` involved), one assertion per species confirming `exp_yield > 0` and all 6 `ev_yield_*` in `[0,3]` for all 386 species in `pokemon.json`, independent of `scripts/gen_exp_ev_yield_data.py`'s own internal validation (387 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m20b_test.tscn` — `[M20b]`: single level-up; the multi-level-up-in-one-award case (Bulbasaur 8→12 via one large Exp jump, `level_up` firing 4 times ascending: 9,10,11,12); the HP-delta math specifically (flat additive rule, hand-computed max_hp before/after, both a below-max and an exactly-at-max starting current_hp); a negative control (one Exp short of the next threshold — nothing changes, no signals fire); the Bulbasaur-style multi-move-per-level case (level 15 → both Poison Powder and Sleep Powder in one level); plain auto-learn under 4 moves; the already-known-move skip; the 4-moves-known skip case (`move_learn_skipped` fires, moveset untouched); the 4-moves-known forced-replacement-slot override case (`_force_move_replacement_slot`, slot overwritten, PP reset, other 3 slots untouched); a dedicated evolution-interaction-safety test (mid-battle species reassignment Bulbasaur→Charizard, confirming the next level crossed learns the NEW species' move, proving no stale cache); and one full-battle integration test driving the real `_award_exp_for_fainted_opponent` call site end-to-end (30 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m20c_test.tscn` — `[M20c]`: data integrity (EV cap constants, `POWER_ITEM_EV_BONUS`, the 6 Power items' `ev_boost_stat` values, Macho Brace's stays -1); a basic single-stat grant; the key discriminator (2 participants sharing a kill both receive the FULL undivided EV yield, contrasted directly against their own scaled *Exp* amounts); the per-stat cap clamp (251+3→252, not 254); the total-cap-breaks-loop-in-STAT_-order case (DEF and SPATK fill to exactly 510, SPDEF — processed last — gets nothing); Power Item's +8 bonus and Macho Brace's ×2 doubling tested independently, plus a dedicated mutually-exclusive-on-one-held-item test; the max-level-still-gains-EVs case through the real `_award_exp_for_fainted_opponent` call site; the EVs-don't-retroactively-change-stats discriminator (only a subsequent `_calculate_stats()` call makes a granted EV visible); Klutz suppression of the Power Item bonus; and a player-side-faint negative control (30 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m21_test.tscn` — `[M21]` doubles interaction cleanup: item 7's TrainerAI doubles-slot-exclusion fix (`choose_replacement`/`_best_switch_target`, plus a singles negative control); item 3's Red Card double-trigger guard (synthetic STATUS move for the non-primary attacker to avoid whole-battle-aggregation contamination, plus a single-holder negative control); item 1's Shell-Bell-skipped/Life-Orb-skipped-when-Red-Card-switches-the-attacker pair via the new timeline-adjacency signal pattern, plus a normal-Shell-Bell-heals negative control; item 4's Self-Destruct/Explosion ally-hit in doubles, plus a singles-no-ally negative control; item 9's synthetic-`MoveData` Snatch-beats-Magic-Bounce doubles ordering test; item 2's 6 spread-accumulation tests via the new `_dispatch_doubles_spread_with_signals` direct single-dispatch helper (Shell Bell heals once from the accumulated total, Life Orb recoils once not per target, one-target-immune/one-target-fainted mid-resolution still accumulate correctly, ally-hit damage included in the total, and a single-target-unchanged negative control) — item 5 (Dragon Darts) and item 8 (Trick Room × Pursuit doubles turn-order) both deliberately shipped with NO test function, deferred to a future turn-order-splice session per Rob's own choice (33 tests)
- `scenes/battle/new_item_b_test.tscn` — `[NEW ITEM B]` status-move spread-targeting dispatch: data integrity for all 9 moves' `is_spread`/`target_includes_ally` flags; Growl hitting BOTH opponents in a real doubles dispatch (the core fix); Venom Drench's per-target-independent poison condition (one poisoned target gets all 3 drops, one non-poisoned target gets none, confirming Venom Drench's own pre-existing `is_venom_drench` branch — deliberately excluded from the new generic dispatch — still works correctly); Teeter Dance confusing both opponents AND the user's own ally (`target_includes_ally`); Substitute blocking one target while the OTHER target in the same spread use is still hit normally; a Magic Bounce holder reflecting Growl back at the original attacker while the OTHER (non-Magic-Bounce) target is STILL hit normally in the same spread use; a singles negative control (signal-snapshotted to the first stat-stage change, avoiding whole-battle-aggregation); a single-target-move-in-doubles negative control (Scary Face still only hits the one selected opponent) (29 tests)
- `scenes/battle/new_item_a_test.tscn` — `[NEW ITEM A]` 9 damage-category moves missing `is_spread`: data integrity for all 9 moves' `is_spread` flags plus the Surf/Earthquake scope-boundary confirmation (`target_includes_ally` still false); Swift and Rock Slide each hitting both opponents in a real doubles dispatch; Eruption's HP-scaled power confirmed computed ONCE and applied identically across two structurally-identical targets, with a discriminator proving a full-HP attacker deals strictly more damage than a half-HP one; Shell Trap arming then dealing spread damage to both opponents; Razor Wind's full two-turn charge-then-release sequence (charge turn deals no damage, release turn hits both opponents); the existing 0.75x spread-damage-reduction modifier confirmed composing correctly with a newly-flagged move; Surf and Earthquake each explicitly proven to hit both opponents but NOT the user's own ally (the intentional partial-fix boundary — **since closed by `[NEW ITEM C]`**, see the `new_item_a_test.tscn` update note below); a singles negative control (31 tests, updated in place by `[NEW ITEM C]` — A.10/A.11/H.03/I.03 now assert Surf/Earthquake DO hit the ally, matching the new correct behavior)
- `scenes/battle/new_item_c_test.tscn` — `[NEW ITEM C]` `TARGET_FOES_AND_ALLY` full-roster ally-hit sweep: data integrity for all 13 newly-fixed moves plus explicit confirmation Self-Destruct/Explosion/Teeter Dance needed no changes; Discharge hitting BOTH opponents AND the ally in a real 3-target doubles dispatch (the core fix); the 0.75x spread-damage-reduction modifier confirmed still applying correctly in the 3-target (2 opponents + ally) case; Parabolic Charge's drain confirmed genuinely per-hit — exactly 3 separate `drain_heal` events fire, and total healing equals the sum of each individual hit's own 50% drain including the ally's, not a combined-then-halved total; Sparkling Aria confirmed curing the ally's own burn too, not just the opponent's; `AbilityManager.pressure_pp_cost` called directly to reconfirm it still returns exactly 3 for a 2-Pressure-holder-opponent scenario even with a THIRD Pressure holder sitting in the ally slot; a Rock Slide negative control (no `target_includes_ally`, correctly still excludes the ally); a singles negative control (30 tests)
- `scenes/battle/new_item_d_test.tscn` — `[NEW ITEM D]` shared accuracy-roll architecture gap: a fully-deterministic semi-invulnerable-plus-grounded independence proof (Earthquake, `damages_underground`, hits both a grounded target and one that's mid-Dig in the same spread use; Rock Slide, lacking that flag, hits only the grounded one — the core per-target-independence proof); partial-miss-vs-full-miss signal-shape discriminators (`move_missed_target` per individual miss; `move_missed` only when EVERY live target missed); Blunder Policy firing on a full miss but not a partial one; a 60-trial statistical evasion-divergence proof (a 0-evasion target hits every trial regardless of a +6-evasion target's own ~33% independent rate); Shell Bell accumulation confirmed to exclude a missed target's contribution; a singles negative control confirming the non-spread path is completely unaffected (18 tests)
- `scenes/battle/turn_order_splice_test.tscn` — `[Turn-order-splice trio]` items 5/8/11/12/13: item 8's Pursuit-transitivity-fix reproduction (20 internal trials asserting the pursuer always sorts before its intercepted switcher regardless of the random speed-tie tiebreak, a multiple-simultaneous-pursuers doubles scenario preserving their own relative order, a no-Pursuit negative control); item 12's Shell Trap doubles splice (holder moves to occupy the slot immediately after its own attacker) plus a singles negative control; item 11's Round stable-partition promotion (a single later Round user, multiple later Round users preserving their own relative order) plus a singles negative control; item 13's real Gen8+ bubble-swap via direct `_turn_order` construction (a partial swap that stops early, a full walk-to-the-end when genuinely slower than everything remaining, a true no-op when already faster than everything remaining); item 5's Dragon Darts smart-target redirect via deterministic semi-invulnerability (normal hit with no redirect, the core redirect-on-miss case, a genuine total miss when both the original target and ally are unhittable, no-redirect-when-ally-fainted, no-redirect-when-ally-type-immune, a singles negative control) (26 tests)
- `scenes/battle/m21_closeout_test.tscn` — `[M21 closeout]` Acupressure ally-choice + Lightning Rod/Storm Drain attacker-ally redirect: Acupressure raising the ally's own stat when explicitly chosen (the core fix), falling back to self when the resolved target is an opponent or a fainted ally, a maxed-out chosen ally failing outright rather than falling back to self, a singles negative control; Lightning Rod redirecting onto the attacker's own ally (the core fix), a negative control confirming the pre-existing target's-ally redirect case is unaffected, and both directions of the both-candidates-qualify turn-order tie-break (9 tests)

**Note:** if you add a new file with `class_name`, run an import pass before the test scenes
will see it:

```bash
/home/rob/Godot_v4.7.1-stable_linux.x86_64 --headless --path . --import
```

### Data pipeline scripts

Moves, abilities, and (as of the M18 item-data-infrastructure session) items each
follow the same two-track pattern: a hand-authored Python dict as the
human-editable source of truth, regenerated into Godot-native `.tres` Resource
files the engine loads at runtime. Run any of these from the project root, then
re-import (see the note above) before running a scene that depends on new/changed
`.tres` files:

```bash
python3 scripts/gen_move_targets.py  # data/move_targets.json  — RUN BEFORE gen_moves.py
python3 scripts/gen_moves.py      # data/moves/move_NNNN.tres    (MoveData)
python3 scripts/gen_abilities.py  # data/abilities/ability_NNNN.tres (AbilityData)
python3 scripts/gen_items.py      # data/items/item_NNNN.tres    (ItemData)
```

⚠️ **`gen_moves.py` now HARD-FAILS without `data/move_targets.json`** — it reads
every move's real `.target` from there and injects it, rather than the field
being hand-written per entry (110 moves are TARGET_USER, and `get_live_targets`
reads the field to decide whether the player is even asked to pick a target, so
a hand-maintained list would rot into self-buffs prompting for an opponent).
Same two-step shape as `gen_move_descriptions.py`. `MoveData.target`'s class
default and that generator's `DEFAULTS["target"]` are both **TARGET_SELECTED**
and must stay in step: a `.tres` with no `target` line has to load as SELECTED,
not as 0/TARGET_NONE.

All three scripts share one structure: a list of dicts (`MOVES`/`ABILITIES`/`ITEMS`),
a `DEFAULTS` dict (skip a field in the emitted `.tres` when it equals its class
default, keeping files lean), a `FIELD_ORDER` list (explicit emit order), a
`render()` function, and a `main()` that writes one file per entry to a fixed
`data/<kind>/` directory, ID zero-padded to 4 digits.

Loading convention: `MoveRegistry.get_move(id)` / `ItemRegistry.get_item(id)` are
path-convention loaders (`res://data/<kind>/<kind>_%04d.tres % id`, no preload
table — see `move_registry.gd`'s own doc comment for the full rationale). There is
no `AbilityRegistry` — abilities are loaded ad hoc per call site (`load("res://data/abilities/ability_%04d.tres" % id) as AbilityData`)
because `AbilityManager`'s actual mechanic dispatch is 100% hardcoded ability-ID
constants and never reads `.tres` fields at battle runtime; the `.tres` layer there
is a metadata catalog (name/description/ai_rating/interaction flags), not a
mechanism input. **Items follow the `MoveRegistry` pattern** (`ItemRegistry`,
`scripts/battle/core/item_registry.gd`), since item mechanics are likewise
hardcoded in `ItemManager` (`HOLD_EFFECT_*` constants) but item data may need
by-ID lookup from future non-battle code (team-building, UI) the same way moves
do.

**Item scope note:** as of M18a, `gen_items.py`'s `ITEMS` dict only contains the
40 items M18a actually implemented — every future M18 sub-tier (M18b onward) must
add its items there and regenerate `.tres` files, matching the moves/abilities
convention, rather than wiring item data inline into `ItemManager` or a test file.
See `docs/decisions.md`'s item-data-infrastructure entry for the full rationale
and the M18a-era gap this closed.
